# Romans

## Romans 1  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-09

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10658

<!-- post 10658 · 2023-06-09 05:23 · scripture -->

**Romans 1:1-7**
*Paul, a servant of Jesus Christ, called to be an apostle, separated unto the gospel of God, (Which he had promised afore by his prophets in the holy scriptures,) Concerning his Son Jesus Christ our Lord, which was made of the seed of David according to the flesh; And declared to be the Son of God with power, according to the spirit of holiness, by the resurrection from the dead: By whom we have received grace and apostleship, for obedience to the faith among all nations, for his name: Among whom are ye also the called of Jesus Christ: To all that be in Rome, beloved of God, called to be saints: Grace to you and peace from God our Father, and the Lord Jesus Christ.*

<!-- post 10659 · 2023-06-09 05:59 · study -->

The epistle to the churches of Rome, written by the Apostle Paul prior to his arrest in Jerusalem and his being taken under house arrest to the seat of the empire, as described in the book of Acts. 

Paul describes himself in his greeting as first and foremost a servant of Jesus Christ. A minister doing the work of the Lord, which he specifies as being separated unto the Gospel of God. As we know, he was an evangelical Apostle to the Gentiles to give them the good news of God, the faith in His Son for eternal life. This good news.

Paul tells us parenthetically that this is the same Gospel given by the prophets in the Old Testament, the holy scriptures. As evidenced by our readings, shown by the Spirit, and as taught by Jesus himself. 

The gospel of God is concerning His Son, our Lord, which was made of the seed of David *concerning the flesh,* meaning only the incarnate aspect of manhood, that it is of the lineage of David as promised. But more, Christ Jesus is *declared to be the Son of God with power*, meaning His godhood is shown by what? The spirit of holiness He possessed, and by His resurrection from the dead. These things testify that Jesus was God made flesh, again as promised by God through the prophets. 

Building on this; *By whom we have received grace.*.. Grace is God's mercy in giving salvation by the faith that acknowledges that all these facts are true. God promises a savior in the writings of the prophets, from the lineage of David after the flesh, but being the Son of God in the spirit by His holiness and resurrection. 

...and apostleship; Paul transitions from establishing the faith common to himself and the intended audience of believers, giving the creed of what they all believe as Christians, to his specific calling as an Apostle.

*For obedience to the faith among all nations*; *For,* meaning in the context for the purpose of, he is given to be an Apostle to bring the Gospel which is obedience to the faith he has just laid out, to all nations. To all Gentiles. 

*For his name;* whose? Christ Jesus.

*Among whom are ye also the called of Jesus Christ.* The Romans are among the "all nations," and are also called to salvation the same way he was, in the same faith he enumerated, which is the same Gospel he preaches as Apostle, which is the same Gospel that God promises in the holy scriptures. 

*To all that be in Rome;* his greeting shifts from establishing baselines and his own credentials in the faith, to addressing the Roman believers, *called to be saints,* just as Paul was called to believe, so were they, saints just meaning those saved by faith. Grace and peace to them, well wishes, and again acknowledging that the grace and peace comes from God the Father and the Lord Jesus Christ, His Son.

<!-- post 10660 · 2023-06-09 06:17 · study -->

You can see how Paul builds an argument block by block, in one long thought that establishes the foundation of the idea he wants to convey. It is careful and methodical and of the Spirit in its simplicity, but confounding those who don't believe with its dense structure, making his writings a snare for those who don't actually hold the faith that he uses as the foundations for his epistles. Something that Peter addresses in his own epistle to the churches of Asia:

**2 Peter 3:13-17**
*Nevertheless we, according to his promise, look for new heavens and a new earth, wherein dwelleth righteousness. Wherefore, beloved, seeing that ye look for such things, be diligent that ye may be found of him in peace, without spot, and blameless. And account that the longsuffering of our Lord is salvation; even as our beloved brother Paul also according to the wisdom given unto him hath written unto you; As also in all his epistles, speaking in them of these things; in which are some things hard to be understood, which they that are unlearned and unstable wrest, as they do also the other scriptures, unto their own destruction.Ye therefore, beloved, seeing ye know these things before, beware lest ye also, being led away with the error of the wicked, fall from your own stedfastness.*

Peter both acknowledges Paul's standing as an Apostle, his writings as scripture, and that what he writes confounds the unlearned and unstable, the babe in Christ or the unbelieving. Peter appeals to them, saying since they have the faith, they should beware being taken by those who wrest with scripture out of ignorance or unbelief, by false doctrines pulled from the complexity of Paul's writings, as well as scripture in general. 

As Paul writes in **2 Timothy 2:15**
*Study to shew thyself approved unto God, a workman that needeth not to be ashamed, rightly dividing the word of truth.*

We study scripture for God's approval, and to not be ashamed, that is taken astray, caught off guard by an argument we don't have an answer for, being misled by those who wrestle with scripture to pull out false doctrines, etc. To be like the Thessalonians; 

**Acts 17:11**
*These were more noble than those in Thessalonica, in that they received the word with all readiness of mind, and searched the scriptures daily, whether those things were so.*

**1 Corinthians 2:15**
*Which things also we speak, not in the words which man's wisdom teacheth, but which the Holy Ghost teacheth; comparing spiritual things with spiritual.*

This is how we grow in grace and peace. This is how we protect ourselves from false doctrines or doubtful disputations by the unlearned and unstable, and the false prophets bringing another gospel. This is how we please God. This is how we can stand stedfast on our faith not being ashamed by naysayers or mockers. This is how we share our faith to others that they may receive the same forgiveness and grace that we have. 

So, as we study to be approved by God, it is important to get how the Spirit leads Paul to build his case, his outline for the theology that comes from our salvation. The implications for living in the faith that we share.

<!-- post 10666 · 2023-06-10 05:56 · scripture -->

**Romans 1:8-17**
*First, I thank my God through Jesus Christ for you all, that your faith is spoken of throughout the whole world. For God is my witness, whom I serve with my spirit in the gospel of his Son, that without ceasing I make mention of you always in my prayers; Making request, if by any means now at length I might have a prosperous journey by the will of God to come unto you. For I long to see you, that I may impart unto you some spiritual gift, to the end ye may be established;*
*That is, that I may be comforted together with you by the mutual faith both of you and me.*

*Now I would not have you ignorant, brethren, that oftentimes I purposed to come unto you, (but was let hitherto,) that I might have some fruit among you also, even as among other Gentiles. I am debtor both to the Greeks, and to the Barbarians; both to the wise, and to the unwise. So, as much as in me is, I am ready to preach the gospel to you that are at Rome also.*

*For I am not ashamed of the gospel of Christ: for it is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth; to the Jew first, and also to the Greek. For therein is the righteousness of God revealed from faith to faith: as it is written, The just shall live by faith.*

<!-- post 10667 · 2023-06-10 06:45 · study -->

Continuing Romans 1, Paul having introduced himself by his relationship to God through Christ, and stipulating the faith shared by believers unto salvation, he addresses the Roman saints, thanking God through Christ (our prayers to the Father are in the name of the Son), that the Roman churches are world famous for their faith. That Paul prays for them and to be among them, without ceasing. (Note this isn't in vain repetition. It just is his saying it is frequently an ask he makes)

Paul says that he, as a servant to the gospel, the shared faith he previously stipulated, and as a minister to it, that he serves God with his spirit in the gospel of the Son, and calls God to witness his sincerity to the readers. This is a letter, and Paul wishes to convey earnestness that may not be discerned from text alone. 

Paul's prayer is by the will of God he may have a safe and prosperous journey to them. All prayer should be to the understanding that God's will be done. 

Paul himself longs to see them, that he may impart unto them some spiritual gift, that he may be used of God to be a blessing, or to edify the brethren there. And that he himself would be blessed, given comfort being with them by the mutual faith they share. 

This is important to understand, it is why Paul began his letter this way, emphasizing what that faith is, in Whom, to what end, and that it is what joins him to these strangers half a continent away. 

All epistles are written to the saved. They follow the Gospels in the Bible, because it is understood that by the testimony of them we would come to believe and accept the gift of salvation, eternal life in Christ Jesus, given by God's grace. 

Most apostate religions claiming Christianity as their source, frame the epistles as written to the world in general. Not to the saved, because they teach that man can never know if they are saved or not. Because they teach works-based justification, they have another gospel. 

But as Paul says here, *that I may be comforted together with you by the mutual faith both of you and me.* Paul is clearly saved, he is writing to brethren of their shared faith. They are saints. Forgiven in Christ. Just as we are saints today, if we've been born again in faith. 

The naysayers today in the false apostate churches would charge Paul with the "sin of presumption," for assuming he is saved, let alone assuming others are. 

Paul continues, writing that he intended to come to them in the past, but was "let hitherto," led to where he is now at that writing. He intended to come to Rome before, (and God will grant him this soon enough, though not as Paul might think, but rather as a prisoner for the faith), that he could bear fruit in the city that is the seat of power for the empire. That he could get people saved by the Gospel, even as he has among the other Gentiles, the other nations of Asia Minor where he had traveled. 

He says he is a debtor to the Greeks and Barbarians, that is the people of the empire, of Macedonia, and to the nations considered barbarians, not of the formal Greco-Roman people, and to both the wise and unwise, the civilized and educated and the simple peasants, all of whom Paul has had great success in bringing to Christ. This has him excited to preach the same Gospel in Rome, to win souls. 

It is here, Paul having established all these things, reemphasizes once again the Gospel he is a servant to, that he begins to transition from introduction and stipulations to the meat of the arguments he, in the Spirit, will make. 

*For I am not ashamed of the gospel of Christ: for it is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth*.

The power of God unto salvation comes from the Gospel. The Gospel is as he has stipulated, that God the Father gave us His Son, who died and rose again, to fulfill the scriptures, given by the prophets, wherein He promised a redeemer. Faith comes by hearing the word of God. The preaching of the Gospel is being a servant of the Lord.

<!-- post 10668 · 2023-06-10 06:45 · study -->

And Paul makes the important distinction that while it was to the Jew first, it is also to the Greek, or the Gentiles, the other nations. That it is for **every one** who believes. 

And the sharing of that power of God contains His righteousness, to be revealed to us, faith to faith. As we share the Gospel and win souls by the power of His word, God reveals His righteousness to the world. 

This is important. 

God has revealed to us His good news, the power of salvation, by the prophets, in the scriptures, by the life and death and resurrection of Jesus, His son, and now by the power of the Gospel, given by those who believe unto others that may believe. It is a revelation into the very nature of God to understand that the just shall live by faith. 

Paul is going to further break down the revelations of God, how He reveals Himself to us that we may better understand Him, but this one is the most important. 

When we share the Gospel to others, we are an instrument that reveals the nature of God to others. His righteousness. 

And so, when we live blameless as we can, staying in the word, in prayer without ceasing for our brethren, praying for the will of God in Jesus' name, all to be a good example and rightly judge, that we may have fruit by winning souls, or be edifying, a comfort to others who share our faith, we are able to better serve Him, and be part of that revelation of Him to the world. 

We magnify His love and mercy. We demonstrate the power of His gift. We are the light that draws men to Him. We are partakers in His divine revelation. 

That is an awesome responsibility and a blessing greater than any other in this life.

<!-- post 10675 · 2023-06-11 08:00 · scripture -->

**Romans 1:18-23**
*For the wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hold the truth in unrighteousness; Because that which may be known of God is manifest in them; for God hath shewed it unto them. For the invisible things of him from the creation of the world are clearly seen, being understood by the things that are made, even his eternal power and Godhead; so that they are without excuse: Because that, when they knew God, they glorified him not as God, neither were thankful; but became vain in their imaginations, and their foolish heart was darkened. Professing themselves to be wise, they became fools, And changed the glory of the uncorruptible God into an image made like to corruptible man, and to birds, and fourfooted beasts, and creeping things.*

<!-- post 10676 · 2023-06-11 08:25 · study -->

So, in our prior instalment we see the righteousness of God is revealed to man through those that are saved sharing the Gospel of God in Christ Jesus with others. Here we see that God also reveals His nature, His wrath, from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men. 

Their literal unrighteousness is part of His wrath. Man is punished by their own sin. We see it all the time, that the consequences of sin is a curse, teaching the wages of sin is death. Nature has revealed to us this truth, the system of consequence. We see it in the downfall of our own current society. We see the effects of sin in our own lives. 

Because that which may be known of God is manifest in those who hold the truth in unrighteousness. God has shown men His judgment. 

Man has a conscience. The law of God is written in our hearts. The Spirit has come to convict men of sin that they may know they need salvation. The law worked in the OT times to do the same, as a schoolmaster to our unrighteousness that we may seek salvation in the Lord. 

These invisible things of God are visible, clearly seen, from the creation of the world through now. And His wrath is understood by His creation. Man understands right from wrong, as Paul gets into later in Romans. 

We see His power and destruction in nature. We see it in the flood. In Sodom and Gomorrah. The judgment of nations. 

We see the eternal power of the Godhead and so, all men are without excuse. 

But men that knew God through His revelation, weren't thankful to God and didn't glorify Him as God, but became vain in their imaginations. Men knowing the judgment and wrath of God made up stories to explain it. Devised often complex systems of works and worship of idols to justify their sin. Vain, meaning unprofitable and a waste of time. Even today they invent a false god out of the Biblical God, and their perverse version of Jesus, while ignoring the truth of His judgment. 

These people, the Bible says, have their foolish hearts darkened. Hardened. They become worse with time, clinging to their vain beliefs and not recognizing the truth of God through His revelations to us. 

Prophets. His word. His power. The Gospel. His wrath. These things are given that all men seeking truth shall find it. 

But those who became vain in their imaginations, whose foolish hearts are darkened, who profess themselves wise become fools, and change the glory of an uncorruptible God into idols, images made like unto corruptible man, like the Catholic church or the Orthodox that split off it. Or like unto birds, and animals and creeping things. Worship of nature. Worship of themselves even. 

Worship of the created more than the Creator. 

Because of all that...well, Paul tells us what ends up happening to these people in the following verses.

<!-- post 10686 · 2023-06-12 04:56 · scripture -->

**Romans 1:21-27**
*Because that, when they knew God, they glorified him not as God, neither were thankful; but became vain in their imaginations, and their foolish heart was darkened. Professing themselves to be wise, they became fools, And changed the glory of the uncorruptible God into an image made like to corruptible man, and to birds, and fourfooted beasts, and creeping things.*

*Wherefore God also gave them up to uncleanness through the lusts of their own hearts, to dishonour their own bodies between themselves: Who changed the truth of God into a lie, and worshipped and served the creature more than the Creator, who is blessed for ever. Amen.*

*For this cause God gave them up unto vile affections: for even their women did change the natural use into that which is against nature: And likewise also the men, leaving the natural use of the woman, burned in their lust one toward another; men with men working that which is unseemly, and receiving in themselves that recompence of their error which was meet.*

<!-- post 10687 · 2023-06-12 05:24 · study -->

And so, we rewind slightly to yesterday's verses to see the because that/wherefore/for this cause - logical progression of what follows those who hold the truth in unrighteousness. Who knowing God, Who is revealed to us in multiple ways, who knowing His judgment and His wrath, and knowing His righteousness, believe a lie and deny His power. 

Because when they knew God through revelation, but didn't glorify Him, but rather their imaginations, their foolish heart, which is darkened. And they worship the creature more than the Creator. Idols. Nature. Rocks. Fables. Traditions of men. 

**Wherefore God gave them up** to uncleanness. Because of their rejection of the truth for a lie, God let's their lust dishonor their own bodies. The curse of being left to their own lusts is used by God to punish them. The same as we see His wrath through the consequences of sin and repent, they are blind to it and so He gives them up to wallow in their lusts and receive the wages of their sin. 

Continuing the same thought, Paul writes; *For this cause God gave them up unto vile affections*. For which cause? They turned the truth of God unto a lie. What vile affections? *Women did change the natural use into that which is against nature. And likewise also the men, leaving the natural use of the woman, burned with lust one towards another.*

So we understand the process, God reveals His truth. Some refuse to see it, and not only refuse (as most people who never believe), but worse, they turn the truth into a lie. And are given up to their lusts, which brings them to God allowing their vile affections to take hold of them. He gives up. The Spirit isn't convicting them of sin, in fact their own delusions justify it in their darkened hearts. 

And this is clearly talking about sodomites and lesbians, and transvestites for that matter. Leaving the natural use, to go against nature. 

Why? That they receive *recompense in* themselves *their error which was meet*. That they suffer in their flesh, in their lives the wages of sin. That they are cursed by God to fulfill their own lust, and so be punished by the consequence of it. The recompense that was meet, meaning appropriate to, commiserate to the error. They get punished with being queers so they can suffer for it.

Because the wrath of God is a revelation to all men to lead them to repentance towards God in Christ, and because they reject that and turn that truth into a lie, and deny God His glory, and are unthankful in their vain imaginations, God gives them up to their own vile, unclean, not-normal, lusts that they burn with. 

They don't "struggle with sin," they are consumed by their own lust. God gives them up to it so they can suffer the fullness of His wrath. It's no longer a lesson to bring them to conviction that they may be saved. God has given them up to this.

<!-- post 10688 · 2023-06-12 05:45 · study -->

"God never gives up on us!" That's a lie. "It's never too late to repent and believe!" That's not what the Bible says. 

As longsuffering as God is, He is clear in His word that it is too late for some. The angels of the first habitation that fell in their pride. The people before the flood. The people of Sodom and Gomorrah. Pharaoh and the Egyptians. The people of Canaan who had committed abominations. The Edomites to whom God promised a land to, but they eventually were given up and destroyed. The wicked kings and queens who killed the prophets of God. Israel, the northern kingdom, the 10 tribes therein, bred out of existence and scattered. The Pharisees and Sadducees and scribes whom Jesus condemns in their own unbelief. Judah, destroyed for the unregenerate Jews who rejected the truth of Christ Jesus. 

*For he saith, I have heard thee in a time accepted, And in the day of salvation have I succoured thee: behold, now is the accepted time; behold, now is the day of salvation*.

In the book of Daniel, Babylonian Kong Belshazzar saw the writing on the wall. Literally. Judgement of God against him had been declared by the Spirit writing upon the wall of his feast hall. It was too late for him. That night Babylon fell to the Persians and Belshazzar was slain.

Over and over again we see God reveal the truth to people, and call them to repentance. Sometimes, like the Gentile city of Nineveh, they listen and are spared His judgment. God repents of the evil He would do to them as recompense for their error. 

Others, like Pharaoh, harden their hearts until it is too late. 

In the end times, as the day of the Lord approaches, the Bible tells us that there will be people in denial, believing their own vain imaginations, right up to the end. 

**1 Thessalonians 5:1-3**
*But of the times and the seasons, brethren, ye have no need that I write unto you. For yourselves know perfectly that the day of the Lord so cometh as a thief in the night. For when they shall say, Peace and safety; then sudden destruction cometh upon them, as travail upon a woman with child; and they shall not escape.*

And like the day of the Lord, only God knows the day or hour where He will give up people to their own destruction. 

It is a blessing of His mercy and grace, the longsuffering of the Lord, that for most people the final nail in their coffin is their natural passing to death and hell. They have the chance to change their hearts and accept the free gift of salvation right to the end of their lives. But not all people. 

Some refuse the truth so utterly God gives them up that they may feel His wrath by their own unnatural lusts consuming them.

The implications and understanding of salvation in Christ, and we are given it just after the Gospels and Acts for a reason, and reprobation is explained fully in the first chapter of it, again for a reason. 

That reprobates are the focus of the epistles of Peter and Jude, as well as Hebrews and other Pauline epistles should also be noted as the importance God gives it, as a way of recognizing and understanding just who they are, what they do and how they become that way.

Before they die perishing or are given up to their own foolish and darkened heart.

It is as important (and tangentially related) as the JQ, and the Biblical understanding of Jews in relationship to Christ, that they are antichrists.

It answers questions so many have regarding psychopaths, mass murderers, pedophiles, why tolerance of sodomites destroys a society, how the judgment of God works on those who aren't saved, here on this earth, in the flesh. 

Most people understand perishing, death and Hell. 

Few people understand salvation and eternal life.

And it is from this ignorance of scripture that the Devil tempts us. It is by getting people to listen to their own conceits and the logic of man, rather than scripture that Satan traps us in snares, leads us to sin and in our ignorance, sow tares among the wheat.

<!-- post 10709 · 2023-06-13 06:18 · study -->

"But this guy came to our church saying he was a sodomite for years and repented of it so he's saved!"

Repentance of sin isn't how we are saved. It is by faith alone. Right away it is a red flag, or should be. 

**Matthew 13:24-30**
*Another parable put he forth unto them, saying, The kingdom of heaven is likened unto a man which sowed good seed in his field: But while men slept, his enemy came and sowed tares among the wheat, and went his way. But when the blade was sprung up, and brought forth fruit, then appeared the tares also. So the servants of the householder came and said unto him, Sir, didst not thou sow good seed in thy field? from whence then hath it tares? He said unto them, An enemy hath done this. The servants said unto him, Wilt thou then that we go and gather them up? But he said, Nay; lest while ye gather up the tares, ye root up also the wheat with them. Let both grow together until the harvest: and in the time of harvest I will say to the reapers, Gather ye together first the tares, and bind them in bundles to burn them: but gather the wheat into my barn.*

The tares, or weeds that resemble wheat but produce no fruit, Jesus warned they would be sown among believers. 

Peter writes of reprobates coming in to churches, **2 Peter 2**, he preaches hard against them, calling them *natural brute beasts, made to be taken and destroyed, speak evil of the things that they understand not; and shall utterly perish in their own corruption; and shall receive the reward of unrighteousness.*

These are the tares among the wheat. This is the reprobate doctrine, and the warning that they shall creep in unawares, and sow discord and lead many astray. 

**2 Peter 2:17-22**
*These are wells without water, clouds that are carried with a tempest; to whom the mist of darkness is reserved for ever. Peter is teaching the same Reprobate Doctrine as Paul.

**Romans 2**
*Therefore thou art inexcusable, O man, whosoever thou art that judgest: for wherein thou judgest another, thou condemnest thyself; for thou that judgest doest the same things.*

*2 But we are sure that the judgment of God is according to truth against them which commit such things.*

*3 And thinkest thou this, O man, that judgest them which do such things, and doest the same, that thou shalt escape the judgment of God?*

*6 Who will render to every man according to his deeds:*

*7 To them who by patient continuance in well doing seek for glory and honour and immortality, eternal life:*

*16 In the day when God shall judge the secrets of men by Jesus Christ according to my gospel.*

*18 And knowest his will, and approvest the things that are more excellent, being instructed out of the law;*

*19 And art confident that thou thyself art a guide of the blind, a light of them which are in darkness,*

*20 An instructor of the foolish, a teacher of babes, which hast the form of knowledge and of the truth in the law.*

*21 Thou therefore which teachest another, teachest thou not thyself? thou that preachest a man should not steal, dost thou steal?*

*22 Thou that sayest a man should not commit adultery, dost thou commit adultery? thou that abhorrest idols, dost thou commit sacrilege?*

*23 Thou that makest thy boast of the law, through breaking the law dishonourest thou God?*

*24 For the name of God is blasphemed among the Gentiles through you, as it is written.*

*26 Therefore if the uncircumcision keep the righteousness of the law, shall not his uncircumcision be counted for circumcision?*

*27 And shall not uncircumcision which is by nature, if it fulfil the law, judge thee, who by the letter and circumcision dost transgress the law?*

*28 For he is not a Jew, which is one outwardly; neither is that circumcision, which is outward in the flesh:*

*29 But he is a Jew, which is one inwardly; and circumcision is that of the heart, in the spirit, and not in the letter; whose praise is not of men, but of God.*

<!-- post 10719 · 2023-06-14 06:14 · study -->

Carrying over from Chapter 1, **Romans 2** sees Paul transition from the establishment of God's gospel, His revelation through His righteousness to them that believe, and His revelation via His wrath, especially to the reprobate, those given up by God. 

He begins by reminding the reader that while the Reprobate is filled with unrighteousness, given over to a laundry list of sins, some unnatural, that there are many common to man. And if we judge them in their sin against the law, we need to remember we are judged by the same law with the same end, absent Christ. 

That *the judgment of God is according to truth against them which commit such a things*.

If we judge by the law, we are convicted by the same law. But through His revelation, and in God's longsuffering forbearance and the goodness of God, His wrath and our guilt in the law should bring us to repentance, towards the Gospel of God. 

Reprobate or unsaved, we store up wrath against ourselves and the righteous judgement of God, *who will render to every man according to his deeds.* 

To them that are saved, who in patient continuance in well doing seek for glory and honor and immortality, we are rendered eternal life. 

But to them that are contentious, who dispute the judgment of God, and do not obey the truth by believing the Gospel, but obey unrighteousness, they are rendered God's indignation. His wrath. 

There is no respect of persons with God. 

He doesn't play favorites. Jew or Gentile. The judgment of God is the same. And we have no excuse. 

Jews with the law, having the advantage of the oracles of God to show them His judgment, all are judged by the law, and Greek, the Gentiles, without the law are still aware as they accuse others or excuse others, because they have the law in their hearts and are given a conscience. Again, so man has no excuse. 

Men think highly of themselves by judging themselves against others in the law. Jew or Greek. Men deny the judgment of God by excusing those who do as they do, Jew or Greek. 

Hypocrisy and cope. 

But all come to the day when God shall judge the secrets of men by Jesus Christ according to the Gospel. 

All have sinned. The wages of sin are death. But in Christ we are forgiven. Paul will flesh this out more, but here is the beginning of the Romans' Road. The broad outline of the Gospel presentation that has led millions to Christ using the doctrines found in the book of Romans; all face judgment by the same law of God and the Gospel of Christ. 

Jews, with their book-knowledge of the law, who see themselves as understanding His judgments, work to be instructors to the unregenerate Gentile, a light to guide (which they ought to be, by God's design), Paul asks, are they keeping the law they have confidence in? Are they hypocrites? 

At the time of this writing, believing Jews made up most of the churches, and they were gifted to give the understanding of the scriptures to the new believers coming from a pagan background, unlearned to the prophets of God. Paul asks, do you practice what you preach, because by their hypocrisy the name of God is blasphemed among the Gentiles. 

Saved Christians, especially those who have been in the faith for any length of time, or who were raised by Bible believing parents in a Bible teaching church, are in a like manner in the position of the Jews. 

And in a like manner, many judge the unregenerate and unlearned by the law, while they themselves are guilty of the same, and there but for the grace of God deserve the same fate. 

We set ourselves as instructors of the word of God, to guide the babes in Christ, but do we sin as we judge against them in their sin? It applies to us now as much as to the 1st Century converted Jews. 

We ought not think too highly of ourselves, remembering we deserve the same judgment as even the worst Reprobate; Death and Hell. 

And as we teach new Christians, we should be as patient and longsuffering as God is towards us. Having the grace to understand we all sin and fall short.

<!-- post 11530 · 2023-08-10 12:50 · study -->

Answering a question with a brother about **Romans 2** had me really rethink something that I missed, and it clarified a lot of the misunderstanding around the chapter, specifically who it is written to. 

Because of the language Paul uses, specifically at the end of the chapter, many assume he is directly addressing Jews there, despite the greetings in chapter 1 being to the church in Rome, which are Gentiles. In my study, I did what a lot of people do and just squared the circle by saying basically the church has both Jews and Greeks (which is likely true, but irrelevant to what Paul is doing).

The entire epistle is a general one, and all believers are the audience. But there is a default understanding that some of it is actually targeted to Jews, in the church, based especially on the last half of chapter 2. 

But it's not. 

Paul's not writing to Jews. He's writing a rhetorical method of argument that addresses common points made about Gentiles, then Jews, in a methodical way. 

It's part of the larger argument that he's making in the epaitle. It isn't literally him saying to the Jew the things he says, but addressing questions often raised by Jews against Christians who were preaching that Gentiles were now made accessible to the Gospel of Grace. When Paul says "O man..." is like when James says "a man may say..."

It's a method of rhetoric common in the era. Anticipating and answering objections to stipulate certain points. 

And so after establishing that Gentiles, despite being pagans and many reprobate and given over, with no excuse, given up to all unrighteousness, have access salvation in Christ, Paul continues to hammer Jewish objections to this idea. 

*Behold, thou art called a Jew, and restest in the law, and makest thy boast of God, And knowest his will, and approvest the things that are more excellent, being instructed out of the law*...This is addressed to a rhetorical Jewish opponent who would make these claims, not specifically "to Jews."

Chapter three changes to 3rd person, because it was always the arguments being made, not the audience being addressed. 

*What advantage then hath the Jew? or what profit is there of circumcision? Much every way: chiefly, because that unto them were committed the oracles of God. For what if some did not believe? shall their unbelief make the faith of God without effect?*

Having chapters and verse helps to quickly reference scripture, but the breaks can cause confusion when a larger point is being made. Go back and read the entire epistle in one go, and consider it's Paul, in the Spirit, making several arguments, methodically. Not telling Jews one thing and Gentiles another.

## Romans 3  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-15

**Romans 3:1-10**
*What advantage then hath the Jew? or what profit is there of circumcision? Much every way: chiefly, because that unto them were committed the oracles of God. For what if some did not believe? shall their unbelief make the faith of God without effect? God forbid: yea, let God be true, but every man a liar; as it is written, That thou mightest be justified in thy sayings, and mightest overcome when thou art judged.*

*But if our unrighteousness commend the righteousness of God, what shall we say? Is God unrighteous who taketh vengeance? (I speak as a man) God forbid: for then how shall God judge the world? For if the truth of God hath more abounded through my lie unto his glory; why yet am I also judged as a sinner? And not rather, (as we be slanderously reported, and as some affirm that we say,) Let us do evil, that good may come? whose damnation is just.*

*What then? are we better than they? No, in no wise: for we have before proved both Jews and Gentiles, that they are all under sin; As it is written, There is none righteous, no, not one:*...

<!-- post 10736 · 2023-06-15 07:43 · study -->

Continuing the methodical building of Paul's arguments, from chapter 1 and 2; that Gentiles without the law are without excuse, and Jews with the law are without excuse, that the revelation of God is given us naturally as well as scripture and the prophets, that the truth of God is found in His wrath against those He rejects, and His judgment on sin is the same on all men, regardless of being a Jew or a Gentile, circumcision or uncircumcission. That we are worthy of death and we fear His judgment knowing it is correct (even as others knowing that what they do is deserving death, do it anyway and take pleasure in others sin), that the law could save if man didn't sin...Chapter 3 opens with the final stipulations leading to what is the clearest presentation of the doctrine of salvation in scripture. 

He concedes that while God is not a respecter of persons, the Jews did indeed have an advantage, having the law spelled out for them. The profit of circumcision, not the act itself but of being a Hebrew under the law, had the oracles of God committed unto them. They had Moses and the prophets. The temple and the scriptures. And the Jews who didn't believe the word, and rejected and killed Christ do not nullify the faith of God, which is true even in man's unbelief. Let God be true, but every man a liar. The righteousness of God isn't dependent on our righteousness and isn't undone by our unrighteousness. And by keeping the truth of God rather than the lies of man, we shall overcome. 

Those lies include our own reasoning against the truth of God. Our own dispute of His holy judgment. Remember the sin of the reprobate is to hold that truth of God in unrighteousness. To change the truth of God into a lie. To challenge His judgment against man's sin. To condemn others while excusing ourselves and the hypocrisy of those who say they keep the law, but offend in not just one, but all points. Trusting circumcision to save them. Their ethnic identity. These are the lies of men, but let God be true, and we shall overcome in the judgment. 

Paul then offers what a man may say, that if our sin, if our lies reveal His righteousness, is God wrong in His judgment? Does our sin making His holiness being shown mean His judgment against us is unrighteous? 

Like, "aren't we doing Him a favor by sinning? Showing His grace, so He shouldn't judge us so harshly!" Real Natural Man hours. 

God forbid. If God was unrighteous for judging the world that shows His glory in His wrath against sin, then how could He judge sin in the first place? It's a foolish argument. 

Paul had been accused by some, likely works-of-the-law salvationists given the context, who slanderously reported that Paul's teaching of the Gospel of grace meant he was saying if we believe and are saved, we have a license to sin. This is a common argument made by those trusting in their works of righteousness against those teaching the Gospel of reconciliation and grace. That it is "easy believism." That we tell people since they are saved from Hell and death by "saying a prayer," we give them the ok to do whatever they want in the liberty of Christ.

 *Let us do evil, that good may come.* That the grace of God is glorified by forgiveness of the most wicked, and so be even more wicked. (There are Jewish sects that practice this, without Christ; Sabbateans and Frankists, but that's a topic for another day). 

Summarizing the original point, having gone down a bit of a rabbit trail of natural man arguments against him, Paul asks the believing Jews in Rome, are they any better than the Gentiles, even with their advantage of having the law? No. In no wise. Because as he's already proven, all are under sin. All deserve death and Hell. All need salvation. None have any excuse. None have any natural advantage against the judgment of God, which is righteous and just. Even if our sin and His wrath brings to others the revelation of God's glory, even if our faith and forgiveness brings revelation of God's righteousness. 

Because there is none righteous; no, not one.

As Peter writes regarding Paul; *as also in all his epistles, speaking in them of these things; in which are some things hard to be understood, which they that are unlearned and unstable wrest, as they do also the other scriptures, unto their own destruction.*

So, to review, Paul has established the truth of God, the need for both Jew and Gentile to be saved. That all are dead in sin. 

Now, recall the larger context of these epistles, established in **Acts 15**;

*But there rose up certain of the sect of the Pharisees which believed, saying, That it was needful to circumcise them, and to command them to keep the law of Moses*.

These Judaizers were the main impetus for the epistles to the various churches by the apostles, to keep the newly regenerate Jews and Gentiles, babes in Christ, from being taken in by the false teachings of a works based salvation. 

Now the grace of God was established with Adam, and every just man from Seth on called upon the name of the Lord to be saved. His grace was given in faith to all ages, and every prophet spoke of His longsuffering and salvation given to those who hear His word and believe. 

In **Hebrews 11**, Paul gives a recitation of the more famous OT saints, saved by faith alone, after establishing what faith is.

*Now faith is the substance of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen. For by it the elders obtained a good report. Through faith we understand that the worlds were framed by the word of God, so that things which are seen were not made of things which do appear.*

The Gospel of grace caused the Jews to persecute Christians from city to city, even as they sought to creep into houses and corrupt them. 

The Gospel of grace was preached by the prophets, seeking to turn Israel to repentance, and they were murdered for it. 

As Jesus preached against the Jews in **Matthew 23**;

*Upon you may come all the righteous blood shed upon the earth, from the blood of righteous Abel unto the blood of Zacharias son of Barachias, whom ye slew between the temple and the altar.*

*And therefore did the Jews persecute Jesus, and sought to slay him, because he had done these things on the sabbath day. But Jesus answered them, My Father worketh hitherto, and I work. To the people taken in by the false gospel, who are under the yoke of the law and subject to the authority of men, primarily in the Catholic Church and its daughters, and even in the evangelical movement, Lordshippers and the like, the appeal of works is they never have to confront the truth that they aren't good enough, that there are none righteous, that they are going to Hell.

<!-- post 10746 · 2023-06-16 07:20 · study -->

Talk with them, soul winning and even online, and they will say they know they are going to heaven because they are a "good person," or that they attend mass and take communion and go to confession. But in the back of their mind they know this isn't true, that they imperfectly follow the law of God, and even fall short of the traditions of man. And so they will hedge by saying it is presumption to claim to know if you're saved. That they "think so, but God decides at judgment." That unbiblical things like purgatory exist as a Plan B is a testament to the cope people will cling to to deny that they are sinners deserving death and Hell. 

And the Gospel reminds them of this, triggers them, and their anger has resulted in persecution and murder of martyrs, just as the prophets slain in the OT times. Just as it did Christ. Just as it did the apostles and the early church and through the dark ages. 

Man doesn't want to humble themselves to the truth of their nature and what they deserve. They seek reassurance that they are good. That they deserve salvation. 

But we know this isn't true.

<!-- post 10747 · 2023-06-16 07:38 · study -->

And so, Paul is writing to the churches in Rome, many of the congregation are saved Jews, surrounded by reprobate pagan excess and put upon by Pharisees among their own people. 

He established that the Gentiles were beholden to the same Creator. He established that the law wasn't sufficient to save the Jew. He established that all are under sin.

And so we begin to get to the meat of things. 

*There is none righteous, no, not one: There is none that understandeth, there is none that seeketh after God.*
*They are all gone out of the way, they are together become unprofitable; there is none that doeth good, no, not one.*

This is quoting **Psalm 14**;

*The fool hath said in his heart, There is no God. They are corrupt, they have done abominable works, there is none that doeth good. The Lord looked down from heaven upon the children of men, to see if there were any that did understand, and seek God. They are all gone aside, they are all together become filthy: there is none that doeth good, no, not one. Have all the workers of iniquity no knowledge? who eat up my people as they eat bread, and call not upon the Lord. There were they in great fear: for God is in the generation of the righteous. Ye have shamed the counsel of the poor, because the Lord is his refuge.*

*Oh that the salvation of Israel were come out of Zion! when the Lord bringeth back the captivity of his people, Jacob shall rejoice, and Israel shall be glad.*

The workers of iniquity, those whose *mouths are an open sepulchre; with their tongues they have used deceit; the poison of asps is under their lip*; those teaching men false doctrines, their words are like the stench of death, of an open grave, because that is what their false gospel brings. It is poison. 

They eat the people up as bread, that they don't call upon the name of the Lord and be saved. 

This should sound familiar.

**Matthew 23**;
*14 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye devour widows' houses, and for a pretence make long prayer: therefore ye shall receive the greater damnation.*

*15 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye compass sea and land to make one proselyte, and when he is made, ye make him twofold more the child of hell than yourselves.*

*16 Woe unto you, ye blind guides, which say, Whosoever shall swear by the temple, it is nothing; but whosoever shall swear by the gold of the temple, he is a debtor!*

*17 Ye fools and blind: for whether is greater, the gold, or the temple that sanctifieth the gold?*

*18 And, Whosoever shall swear by the altar, it is nothing; but whosoever sweareth by the gift that is upon it, he is guilty.*

*19 Ye fools and blind: for whether is greater, the gift, or the altar that sanctifieth the gift?*

*20 Whoso therefore shall swear by the altar, sweareth by it, and by all things thereon.*

*21 And whoso shall swear by the temple, sweareth by it, and by him that dwelleth therein.*

*22 And he that shall swear by heaven, sweareth by the throne of God, and by him that sitteth thereon.*

*23 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye pay tithe of mint and anise and cummin, and have omitted the weightier matters of the law, judgment, mercy, and faith: these ought ye to have done, and not to leave the other undone.*

*24 Ye blind guides, which strain at a gnat, and swallow a camel.*

*25 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye make clean the outside of the cup and of the platter, but within they are full of extortion and excess.*

*26 Thou blind Pharisee, cleanse first that which is within the cup and platter, that the outside of them may be clean also.*

*27 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye are like unto whited sepulchres, which indeed appear beautiful outward, but are within full of dead men's bones, and of all uncleanness.*

*28 Even so ye also outwardly appear righteous unto men, but within ye are full of hypocrisy and iniquity.*

*29 Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! because ye build the tombs of the prophets, and garnish the sepulchres of the righteous,*

<!-- post 10748 · 2023-06-16 07:38 · study -->

*30 And say, If we had been in the days of our fathers, we would not have been partakers with them in the blood of the prophets.*

*31 Wherefore ye be witnesses unto yourselves, that ye are the children of them which killed the prophets.*

*32 Fill ye up then the measure of your fathers.*

*33 Ye serpents, ye generation of vipers, how can ye escape the damnation of hell?*

<!-- post 10749 · 2023-06-16 07:41 · study -->

They devour widows houses. They make merchandise of the people. They make them twice the child of hell as themselves. They are as whited sepecultres, full of death. 

These are the workers of iniquity whom Jesus says on the day of judgement will call out "Lord, Lord, didn't we do many wonderful works?" and He will say to them "Depart from me, I never knew you."

Jesus and Paul taught the same thing, because God's Gospel has always been the same, and opposed by the same forces. Chasing after the error of Cain. Trusting in their works. Hypocrites condemning others while excusing themselves.

<!-- post 10750 · 2023-06-16 08:04 · study -->

And so, going back to Romans 3, having the full context of things, and an understanding of the conflict between these two visions; God's revelation of His righteousness, and the filthy rags of man's, we proceed. 

*Now we know that what things soever the law saith, it saith to them who are under the law: that every mouth may be stopped, and all the world may become guilty before God. Therefore by the deeds of the law there shall no flesh be justified in his sight: for by the law is the knowledge of sin.*

Now we know. Everyone under the law should know that they all break the law. That all the world is guilty before God. That is the purpose of the law, to serve to humble us and reveal the holiness of God, that we may come to call upon His name to be saved. 

(If a man were perfect under the law, they would be saved, but the exceeding holiness that is God, only the Son could achieve this in the flesh)

Therefore by the deeds, the doing, the works, none shall be saved. 

It is impossible for man to work their way to Heaven. We cannot attain the righteousness of God. 

And so what does this mean for us? Where is the good news? How can we attain righteousness if not by our works? 

*But now the righteousness of God without the law is manifested, being witnessed by the law and the prophets; Even the righteousness of God which is by faith of Jesus Christ unto all and upon all them that believe: for there is no difference: For all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God; Being justified freely by his grace through the redemption that is in Christ Jesus: Whom God hath set forth to be a propitiation through faith in his blood, to declare his righteousness for the remission of sins that are past, through the forbearance of God; To declare, I say, at this time his righteousness: that he might be just, and the justifier of him which believeth in Jesus.*

Read that again. And again. 

Jesus Christ is the propitiation, that is a sacrifice that is wholly pleasing to God, that is the blood of Christ serves to impart His righteousness to us, by faith, to show us the righteousness of God in our forgiveness. We are justified freely by His grace. Even the righteousness of God, **that is by faith**, upon **all them that believe**.

That's the good news. This is the Gospel. This is the reason Romans is used to evangelize, because it is the clearest and most succinct summary of the Gospel. 

All have sinned. 
All deserve death.
Jews under the law. Gentiles without the law.
But God's grace is such that all who believe that His Son, Christ Jesus, the lamb without blemish, was given to be the propitiation for our sin, and that grace is given freely to all who believe. 

*Where is boasting then? It is excluded. By what law? of works? Nay: but by the law of faith.* 

This is the end of the argument. There is no one who reads this and says "yeah, but you have to do works" who is seeking truth. They are blind in their own pride.

*Therefore we conclude that a man is justified by faith without the deeds of the law.* 

Read that again. Say it out loud. Three chapters of building argument against the judaizers, the works-based false gospel. The workers of iniquity. 

*Therefore we conclude that a man is justified by faith without the deeds of the law.* 

Furthermore*:*

*Is he the God of the Jews only? is he not also of the Gentiles? Yes, of the Gentiles also: Seeing it is one God, which shall justify the circumcision by faith, and uncircumcision through faith.*
*Do we then make void the law through faith? God forbid: yea, we establish the law.*

By understanding that salvation comes only by faith, we actually establish the law. By believing God's word, by placing our trust in the sacrifice of Jesus, that in His blood we are washed clean, forgiven of sins, we then understand the nature and purpose of the law, and are free to become doers of the law. To seek to obtain the holiness of God out of love. 

To be holy because He is holy. 

To reveal His righteousness to the world by our forgiveness in His grace.

<!-- post 10751 · 2023-06-16 08:09 · study -->

I know that is a lot of Bible, and that these posts were long, but I implore those reading along to take the time and really understand what's being given to us to reveal God's will. 

If you've already called upon the name of the Lord, and are saved, I hope this helps make it easier for you to explain the Gospel to those who aren't. To separate yourselves to the Gospel of God.

And to those of you who are still trusting in your works, who are still dependent on the works of the law to be justified. Who are under the yoke of the workers of iniquity, the blind guides who *bind heavy burdens and grievous to be borne, and lay them on men's shoulders; but they themselves will not move them with one of their fingers*, I hope this helped stir you to be convicted by the Spirit, to repentance from dead works and towards God. 

Amen

**Romans 3**

*1 What advantage then hath the Jew? or what profit is there of circumcision?*

*2 Much every way: chiefly, because that unto them were committed the oracles of God.*

*3 For what if some did not believe? shall their unbelief make the faith of God without effect?*

*4 God forbid: yea, let God be true, but every man a liar; as it is written, That thou mightest be justified in thy sayings, and mightest overcome when thou art judged.*

*5 But if our unrighteousness commend the righteousness of God, what shall we say? Is God unrighteous who taketh vengeance? (I speak as a man)*

*6 God forbid: for then how shall God judge the world?*

*7 For if the truth of God hath more abounded through my lie unto his glory; why yet am I also judged as a sinner?*

*8 And not rather, (as we be slanderously reported, and as some affirm that we say,) Let us do evil, that good may come? whose damnation is just.*

*9 What then? are we better than they? No, in no wise: for we have before proved both Jews and Gentiles, that they are all under sin;*

*10 As it is written, There is none righteous, no, not one:*

*11 There is none that understandeth, there is none that seeketh after God.*

*12 They are all gone out of the way, they are together become unprofitable; there is none that doeth good, no, not one.*

*13 Their throat is an open sepulchre; with their tongues they have used deceit; the poison of asps is under their lips:*

*14 Whose mouth is full of cursing and bitterness:*

*15 Their feet are swift to shed blood:*

*16 Destruction and misery are in their ways:*

*17 And the way of peace have they not known:*

*18 There is no fear of God before their eyes.*

*19 Now we know that what things soever the law saith, it saith to them who are under the law: that every mouth may be stopped, and all the world may become guilty before God.*

*20 Therefore by the deeds of the law there shall no flesh be justified in his sight: for by the law is the knowledge of sin.*

*21 But now the righteousness of God without the law is manifested, being witnessed by the law and the prophets;*

*22 Even the righteousness of God which is by faith of Jesus Christ unto all and upon all them that believe: for there is no difference:*

*23 For all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God;*

*24 Being justified freely by his grace through the redemption that is in Christ Jesus:*

*25 Whom God hath set forth to be a propitiation through faith in his blood, to declare his righteousness for the remission of sins that are past, through the forbearance of God;*

*26 To declare, I say, at this time his righteousness: that he might be just, and the justifier of him which believeth in Jesus.*

*27 Where is boasting then? It is excluded. By what law? of works? Nay: but by the law of faith.*

*28 Therefore we conclude that a man is justified by faith without the deeds of the law.*

*29 Is he the God of the Jews only? is he not also of the Gentiles? Yes, of the Gentiles also:*

*30 Seeing it is one God, which shall justify the circumcision by faith, and uncircumcision through faith.*

*31 Do we then make void the law through faith? God forbid: yea, we establish the law.*
