## Romans 4  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-18

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10777

<!-- post 10777 · 2023-06-18 05:28 · scripture -->

**Romans 4**
*What shall we say then that Abraham our father, as pertaining to the flesh, hath found?*

*2 For if Abraham were justified by works, he hath whereof to glory; but not before God.*

*3 For what saith the scripture? Abraham believed God, and it was counted unto him for righteousness.*

*4 Now to him that worketh is the reward not reckoned of grace, but of debt.*

*5 But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness.*

*6 Even as David also describeth the blessedness of the man, unto whom God imputeth righteousness without works,*

*7 Saying, Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered.*

*8 Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not impute sin.*

*9 Cometh this blessedness then upon the circumcision only, or upon the uncircumcision also? for we say that faith was reckoned to Abraham for righteousness.*

*10 How was it then reckoned? when he was in circumcision, or in uncircumcision? Not in circumcision, but in uncircumcision.*

*11 And he received the sign of circumcision, a seal of the righteousness of the faith which he had yet being uncircumcised: that he might be the father of all them that believe, though they be not circumcised; that righteousness might be imputed unto them also:*

*12 And the father of circumcision to them who are not of the circumcision only, but who also walk in the steps of that faith of our father Abraham, which he had being yet uncircumcised.*

*13 For the promise, that he should be the heir of the world, was not to Abraham, or to his seed, through the law, but through the righteousness of faith.*

*14 For if they which are of the law be heirs, faith is made void, and the promise made of none effect:*

*15 Because the law worketh wrath: for where no law is, there is no transgression.*

*16 Therefore it is of faith, that it might be by grace; to the end the promise might be sure to all the seed; not to that only which is of the law, but to that also which is of the faith of Abraham; who is the father of us all,*

*17 (As it is written, I have made thee a father of many nations,) before him whom he believed, even God, who quickeneth the dead, and calleth those things which be not as though they were.*

*18 Who against hope believed in hope, that he might become the father of many nations, according to that which was spoken, So shall thy seed be.*

*19 And being not weak in faith, he considered not his own body now dead, when he was about an hundred years old, neither yet the deadness of Sarah's womb:*

*20 He staggered not at the promise of God through unbelief; but was strong in faith, giving glory to God;*

*21 And being fully persuaded that, what he had promised, he was able also to perform.*

*22 And therefore it was imputed to him for righteousness.*

*23 Now it was not written for his sake alone, that it was imputed to him;*

*24 But for us also, to whom it shall be imputed, if we believe on him that raised up Jesus our Lord from the dead;*

*25 Who was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.*

<!-- post 10778 · 2023-06-18 05:49 · study -->

What shall we say then? Paul, having concluded that all are under sin, all being unrighteous, and the grace of God given to all who believe on the Son, and that no man may be justified by the works of the law but only faith, now begins the application of this knowledge. The implications of this law of faith, given us by God. And he uses scripture and the promises to Abraham to explain the Old Testament in light of the New. 

Abraham, the physical father of the Hebrews, including the remnant of Jews from the Children of Israel in Rome, what was Abraham's experience? A story every Jew would be familiar with. 

Paul starts by saying, if Abraham were justified by works, he hath whereof to glory. Abraham could brag in his works, his decades of service to God that includes willingness to sacrifice his only begotten son, but we know that there are none righteous. That boasting is excluded when we are judged by grace, as all the glory belongs to God, and the righteousness of Christ.

So Paul writes, if it were by works, Abraham could glory, boast, brag that it was due him being so righteous. But not before God, being in the flesh, a sinner. (Which we have already stipulated in chapter 3)

What then? 

Well, scripture says in **Genesis 15:6**; *And he believed in the LORD; and he counted it to him for righteousness*. Paul quotes this and argues that if it were of works, that means God would be indebted to us. To pay us for our works. That we were owed. 

And so the reward would be not reckoned, or accounted for, by grace. Because grace is an unmerited and unearned gift or favor. Debt is deserved. Grace is undeserved. 

*But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness.*

*Abraham believed God and it was counted unto him for righteousness*.

<!-- post 10779 · 2023-06-18 05:58 · study -->

Abraham is the example Paul uses, given us in scripture as a lesson in justification by faith to God, by grace not of debt. That the glory in our salvation is God's alone. 

But what about before other men?

A man may say that faith without works is dead, being alone. That, to men, justification isn't seen of faith but by a man's works. 

**James 2:18-23**
*Yea, a man may say, Thou hast faith, and I have works: shew me thy faith without thy works, and I will shew thee my faith by my works. Thou believest that there is one God; thou doest well: the devils also believe, and tremble. But wilt thou know, O vain man, that faith without works is dead? Was not Abraham our father justified by works, when he had offered Isaac his son upon the altar? Seest thou how faith wrought with his works, and by works was faith made perfect? And the scripture was fulfilled which saith, Abraham believed God, and it was imputed unto him for righteousness: and he was called the Friend of God.*

*A man may say...* The overlooked predicate for the argument James makes in his epistle, written to brethren, the saved, to encourage them to good works. A man may say that Abraham's faith was shown in his works and thus he was justified to others. 

But we know that scripture says it was faith that justified Abraham to God. 

*For if Abraham were justified by works, he hath whereof to glory; but not before God. For what saith the scripture? Abraham believed God, and it was counted unto him for righteousness.*

By comparing scripture with scripture we see that there is no contradiction, only context. That Paul is writing of justification before God in grace, while James is writing of justification before man, by doing good works in faith. 

The rhetorical question asked by a rhetorical man, "Was not Abraham justified by works?" is answered by the holy Spirit in **Romans 4** and **Genesis 15**. Not before God, or he would have to glory. The boasting wouldn't be excluded. The law of grace would become one of debt. 

The misuse of **James 2** to teach a false gospel of works is one of the great stumbling blocks to salvation and faith. But by comparison, context and understanding we see that there is no contradiction found, anywhere in the Bible.

<!-- post 10780 · 2023-06-18 06:04 · study -->

*Now to him that worketh is the reward not reckoned of grace, but of debt. But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness.*

But to him that worketh not, but believes, his faith is counted for righteousness just as Abraham's. Just as all who believe. Works has nothing to do with salvation. God doesn't owe us salvation. 

Our works, no matter how good we think they are, can never offset our sin, and the wages thereof. 

When someone says their salvation or their ability to stay saved is contingent on their works, they are saying they are good enough to please God. That they are owed salvation. That the glory is theirs. That their boastings before God are justified. That they don't need grace. 

**Hebrews 11:6**
*But without faith it is impossible to please him: for he that cometh to God must believe that he is, and that he is a rewarder of them that diligently seek him.*

Their lack of faith in Christ to be the propitiation for our sin, the sole sacrifice pleasing and acceptable to God, means their works are dead. They have no grace and no works. They are incapable of pleasing God.

<!-- post 10781 · 2023-06-18 06:16 · study -->

**Hebrews 6:1**
*Therefore leaving the principles of the doctrine of Christ, let us go on unto perfection; not laying again the foundation of repentance from dead works, and of faith toward God*

Repentance from dead works and to faith towards God. Turning from means turning towards. From trusting in our works of righteousness to faith in God. This is the foundation that is built on the rock that is Christ. 

And so, reckoned of faith, the man who does no works but believes, is saved by that faith alone. Paul quotes Davin from **Psalm 32**
*Blessed is he whose transgression is forgiven, Whose sin is covered. Blessed is the man unto whom the LORD imputeth not iniquity, And in whose spirit there is no guile*.

The Lord will not impute sin, our ledger is clean, our transgressions forgiven. 

For what is sin?

**1 John 3:4**
*Whosoever committeth sin transgresseth also the law: for sin is the transgression of the law.*

But we who believe not only are forgiven of sins past, but God will not impute sin to us. Our transgression is forgiven. This is a promise of God.

**Hebrews 10:17**
*And their sins and iniquities will I remember no more.*

<!-- post 10782 · 2023-06-18 06:37 · study -->

So this is the promise made by God that if we believe on the Son, we are saved by His grace. Our sins forgiven. No sin even being imputed upon us. Whether we work or not, because work is debt not grace. 

Paul continues using Abraham's example;

*Cometh this blessedness then upon the circumcision only, or upon the uncircumcision also? for we say that faith was reckoned to Abraham for righteousness. How was it then reckoned? when he was in circumcision, or in uncircumcision? Not in circumcision, but in uncircumcision.*

Abraham did not receive the ordinance of circumcision until decades after his believing God's promise, and being saved by that faith. He was blessed with the accounting of righteousness when he did not know circumcision. So this promise, predating this rite of the Jews, means it does not only apply to the circumcised, but also to the uncircumcised. And so the promise of grace doesn't require circumcision, but faith alone. 

This point is both specific to the issue of circumcision being introduced by the Judaizers in Paul's day, but also generally speaking, that there is no rite or ordinance that is required for salvation. Not baptism. Not any ritual or keeping of tradition. No works of the law. 

*And he received the sign of circumcision, a seal of the righteousness of the faith which he had yet being uncircumcised: that he might be the father of all them that believe, though they be not circumcised; that righteousness might be imputed unto them also*.

Abraham as the father of all who believe, the circumcision of the spirit, the generation of believers are partakers of the promise, that the righteousness of God is imputed upon us in faith. 

*For the promise, that he should be the heir of the world, was not to Abraham, or to his seed, through the law, but through the righteousness of faith. For if they which are of the law be heirs, faith is made void, and the promise made of none effect: Because the law worketh wrath: for where no law is, there is no transgression.*

The promise of God that Abraham believed was that through his faith, he would be made father to many nations, and inherit the kingdom of God. This promise was made possible in Christ. 

**Galatians 3:16**
*Now to Abraham and his seed were the promises made. He saith not, And to seeds, as of many; but as of one, And to thy seed, which is Christ*.

Unbelieving Jews felt they were owed the kingdom by God for being Abraham's seed under the law. That the inheritance was by blood, and the promise made to them after the flesh, and not in the spirit. 

This is the basis for Zionism and the false doctrine of dispensationalism. 

But the Bible teaches the exact opposite. There is no difference between Jew and Gentile. All are under sin. It is by faith only that we are made heirs. That the seed of the promise is Christ manifest in the flesh. 

**Matthew 3:9**
*And think not to say within yourselves, We have Abraham to our father: for I say unto you, that God is able of these stones to raise up children unto Abraham.*

It is not an inheritance by the law but by faith. Like Esua and Jacob, the inheritance went to the younger. It was taken from the elder, who with tears sought repentance from God, but none was found. 

**Luke 13:25-30**
*When once the master of the house is risen up, and hath shut to the door, and ye begin to stand without, and to knock at the door, saying, Lord, Lord, open unto us; and he shall answer and say unto you, I know you not whence ye are: Then shall ye begin to say, We have eaten and drunk in thy presence, and thou hast taught in our streets. But he shall say, I tell you, I know you not whence ye are; depart from me, all ye workers of iniquity. There shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth, when ye shall see Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, and all the prophets, in the kingdom of God, and you yourselves thrust out. And they shall come from the east, and from the west, and from the north, and from the south, and shall sit down in the kingdom of God. And, behold, there are last which shall be first, and there are first which shall be last.*

<!-- post 10783 · 2023-06-18 06:37 · study -->

The kingdom of God was taken from the Jew in their unbelief and given to the nations of people bringing forth the fruit thereof. Who had faith. 

There is no plan for the Jews after the flesh and those teaching dispensationalism to claim there is are teaching heresy.

<!-- post 10784 · 2023-06-18 06:48 · study -->

*Because the law worketh wrath: for where no law is, there is no transgression. Therefore it is of faith, that it might be by grace; to the end the promise might be sure to all the seed; not to that only which is of the law, but to that also which is of the faith of Abraham; who is the father of us all, (As it is written, I have made thee a father of many nations,) before him whom he believed, even God, who quickeneth the dead, and calleth those things which be not as though they were.*

Because the law brings wrath, because we are all under sin, it isn't by the law we are saved. It is impossible for all have sinned. 

As James writes in the parts of his epistles ignored-by-works salvationists;

*For whosoever shall keep the whole law, and yet offend in one point, he is guilty of all.*

Remember, there are none righteous. Knowledge of the law brings wrath. But faith, the grace of God. 

We access His mercy and forgiveness through faith alone. Plus nothing, minus nothing.

And in grace we receive the promise given Abraham, and count as his seed in the spirit. Adopted into the kingdom of God. Made sons of God. Abraham is the father of us all who believe. 

 *(As it is written, I have made thee a father of many nations,) before him whom he believed, even God, who quickeneth the dead, and calleth those things which be not as though they were.*

God, who can see all things, the future which is not to us, and yet it appears as it were to God, who quickens the dead, who breathes life into our dead spirit, and who made this eternal promise to all who believe. 

The Jew has no claim to Abraham, outside of faith. None. And to none of the promises made, for they all are fulfilled in Christ. 

If it feels like I am beating a dead horse, understand that it is God's word that stresses this over and over again, because it is the Gospel, the good news, that is the entire point of scripture. To bring us into the promise through faith and the imputed righteousness of God.

<!-- post 10785 · 2023-06-18 06:53 · study -->

Paul continues to show that despite the unlikely nature of some of the specific promises to him made of God, the hope against hope that he father a child with Sarah in their old age, that they would through Isaac become many nations, to people more numerous than the sand of the shore, the stars of heaven. 

*And being fully persuaded that, what he had promised, he was able also to perform. And therefore it was imputed to him for righteousness.*

But was this for Abraham alone? No. 

This was written for our sake as well, that we may KNOW we have eternal life and the imputed righteousness of Jesus Christ if we believe on the promise of God. 

*Now it was not written for his sake alone, that it was imputed to him; But for us also, to whom it shall be imputed, if we believe on him that raised up Jesus our Lord from the dead; Who was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.*

Amen, amen, amen!

<!-- post 10786 · 2023-06-18 07:00 · study -->

I pray to the Lord that this series continues to be a blessing to others and that my understanding grows from presenting it. That the undeniable meaning of the theology of God found in scripture changes hearts with the power of His word. Have a blessed Sunday, and remember to thank father Abraham on this father's day. 

For he is the father of all who believe. 

In Jesus name, amen

## Romans 4  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2023-10-21

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/12317

<!-- post 12317 · 2023-10-21 05:59 · scripture -->

**Romans 4**
*What shall we say then that Abraham our father, as pertaining to the flesh, hath found?*

*2 For if Abraham were justified by works, he hath whereof to glory; but not before God.*

*3 For what saith the scripture? Abraham believed God, and it was counted unto him for righteousness.*

*4 Now to him that worketh is the reward not reckoned of grace, but of debt.*

*5 But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness.*

*6 Even as David also describeth the blessedness of the man, unto whom God imputeth righteousness without works,*

*7 Saying, Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered.*

*8 Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not impute sin.*

*9 Cometh this blessedness then upon the circumcision only, or upon the uncircumcision also? for we say that faith was reckoned to Abraham for righteousness.*

*10 How was it then reckoned? when he was in circumcision, or in uncircumcision? Not in circumcision, but in uncircumcision.*

*11 And he received the sign of circumcision, a seal of the righteousness of the faith which he had yet being uncircumcised: that he might be the father of all them that believe, though they be not circumcised; that righteousness might be imputed unto them also:*

*12 And the father of circumcision to them who are not of the circumcision only, but who also walk in the steps of that faith of our father Abraham, which he had being yet uncircumcised.*

*13 For the promise, that he should be the heir of the world, was not to Abraham, or to his seed, through the law, but through the righteousness of faith.*

*14 For if they which are of the law be heirs, faith is made void, and the promise made of none effect:*

*15 Because the law worketh wrath: for where no law is, there is no transgression.*

*16 Therefore it is of faith, that it might be by grace; to the end the promise might be sure to all the seed; not to that only which is of the law, but to that also which is of the faith of Abraham; who is the father of us all,*

*17 (As it is written, I have made thee a father of many nations,) before him whom he believed, even God, who quickeneth the dead, and calleth those things which be not as though they were.*

*18 Who against hope believed in hope, that he might become the father of many nations, according to that which was spoken, So shall thy seed be.*

*19 And being not weak in faith, he considered not his own body now dead, when he was about an hundred years old, neither yet the deadness of Sarah's womb:*

*20 He staggered not at the promise of God through unbelief; but was strong in faith, giving glory to God;*

*21 And being fully persuaded that, what he had promised, he was able also to perform.*

*22 And therefore it was imputed to him for righteousness.*

*23 Now it was not written for his sake alone, that it was imputed to him;*

*24 But for us also, to whom it shall be imputed, if we believe on him that raised up Jesus our Lord from the dead;*

*25 Who was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.*

<!-- post 12318 · 2023-10-21 06:27 · study -->

Building upon the conclusions reached in Chapter 3, we see in **Romans 4**, Paul uses scripture to flesh out the ideas and demonstrate the truth of his argument. The veil lifted, so that by the reading of Moses a believer may understand God's word. 

He begins with the understanding that Abraham was not justified by his works or by circumcision, but by his faith. Abraham believed God and it was counted for righteousness. 

And when Abraham believed, it was decades before the institution of the carnal ordinance of circumcision. A practice giving an outward sign for the continuation of the people chosen to bear Abraham's seed, which is Christ.

While **Romans 4** is frequently cited to demolish the heretical argument that we are saved in any capacity by our own works, it is primarily an argument against the Judaizers who think to themselves to say they have Abraham as their father. 

But as John the Baptist says in **Matthew 3:9**;

*And think not to say within yourselves, We have Abraham to our father: for I say unto you, that God is able of these stones to raise up children unto Abraham.*

It wasn't circumcision that saved Abraham, but faith. It wasn't even his willingness to sacrifice Isaac. All the great works of faith by Abraham may be reason to glory among men, but not before God. They may be reckoned for reward out of debt, but not of grace which is freely given to those who believe, to the glory of God. 

As David wrote, Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not impute sin. Those who do no works, but have believed on Christ, that faith is counted for righteousness. 

It is an accounting. The only thing that is reckoned for forgiveness of sin and salvation from death, is faith.

And so, if this blessing came by faith and not circumcision, there is no profit in circumcision. 

And if the righteousness of Abraham came by faith, there is no profit in being able to call Abraham your father after the flesh. 

The promise of God, that through his seed all nations shall be blessed, and that seed should be made heir to the whole world, was not made to Abraham, or to his seed through the law. Meaning his physical descendants, who under the law are inheritors. 

The promise to be made heir was through the righteousness of faith. 

This excludes unconditionally any claim Jews would have on the Abrahamic covenant, except by faith in Christ. 

*For if they which are of the law be heirs, faith is made void, and the promise made of none effect*

If dispensationalist Zionism were true, that by the law Jews are heirs to Abraham's promise, *then faith is made void and the promises made of none effect.* 

Saved Christians who believe that Jews are the inheritors by this promise, are like Esau, selling the promise for pottage. Despising their inheritance.

<!-- post 12319 · 2023-10-21 06:35 · study -->

Abraham believed. And in that faith, received the inheritance, which in Christ is given to all who believe. Whether they were circumcised or uncircumcised. And the righteousness and promises of faith given Abraham was not only to him, but to all who believe the Gospel of Grace, again recounted here by Paul;

*Now it was not written for his sake alone, that it was imputed to him; But for us also, to whom it shall be imputed, if we believe on him that raised up Jesus our Lord from the dead; Who was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.*

This is the faith that imputed righteousness on Abraham and upon we who believe. Plus nothing and minus nothing. 

The same faith that saved Abraham saved us today, and will until the day of the Lord comes, and we are gathered unto Him. Jew or Gentile. At the great harvest of God.

<!-- post 12320 · 2023-10-21 06:40 · study -->

So, in 4 chapters we see that there is no difference between Jew and Gentile, all are under sin, all condemned, with the law or without. 

We see that all who are saved from that condemnation are saved by faith in the blood of Christ Jesus. By believing God's promise. By the imputed righteousness of Jesus, our own sins are not counted against us. Without works. Not by the law. Not by circumcision. Not by being physical heirs of Abraham, but spiritual heirs in faith. 

There is no replacement theology, only the continuation of the covenant made to Abraham, which is to all nations. 

There was no other dispensation given. There is no future dispensation to be had.

<!-- post 16679 · 2025-09-06 07:04 · study -->

Unlearned and unstable people confuse the means by which God delivered us, Jesus' life, death, burial and resurrection, with the means of receiving the promise of God, belief. 

Abraham believed God's promise to him, by faith, without knowing the exact means by which He would make good on the promise, **only that He would**. And it was counted for righteousness. 

We have the mystery of the means revealed to us, by the post-resurrection teachings of Christ to the Apostles, and that faith handed down, believer to believer in an unbroken line by which we can say that Abraham and the OT saints are our brothers in Christ, through they did not fully understand the mystery that was Christ. 

This is what the Bible teaches.

<!-- post 16680 · 2025-09-06 07:07 · study -->

I am saved because I believe God, and it is counted for righteousness. If God told us we would be saved by any other means or manner, believing His word would still be why we have His imputed righteousness. 

We know as New Testament Christians that the only means and manner of salvation is by the blood of Christ.

## Romans 5  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-19

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10789

<!-- post 10789 · 2023-06-19 07:19 · scripture -->

**Romans 5**
*Therefore being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ:*

*2 By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.*

*3 And not only so, but we glory in tribulations also: knowing that tribulation worketh patience;*

*4 And patience, experience; and experience, hope:*

*5 And hope maketh not ashamed; because the love of God is shed abroad in our hearts by the Holy Ghost which is given unto us.*

*6 For when we were yet without strength, in due time Christ died for the ungodly.*

*7 For scarcely for a righteous man will one die: yet peradventure for a good man some would even dare to die.*

*8 But God commendeth his love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.*

*9 Much more then, being now justified by his blood, we shall be saved from wrath through him.*

*10 For if, when we were enemies, we were reconciled to God by the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we shall be saved by his life.*

*11 And not only so, but we also joy in God through our Lord Jesus Christ, by whom we have now received the atonement.*

*12 Wherefore, as by one man sin entered into the world, and death by sin; and so death passed upon all men, for that all have sinned:*

*13 (For until the law sin was in the world: but sin is not imputed when there is no law.*

*14 Nevertheless death reigned from Adam to Moses, even over them that had not sinned after the similitude of Adam's transgression, who is the figure of him that was to come.*

*15 But not as the offence, so also is the free gift. For if through the offence of one many be dead, much more the grace of God, and the gift by grace, which is by one man, Jesus Christ, hath abounded unto many.*

*16 And not as it was by one that sinned, so is the gift: for the judgment was by one to condemnation, but the free gift is of many offences unto justification.*

*17 For if by one man's offence death reigned by one; much more they which receive abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness shall reign in life by one, Jesus Christ.)*

*18 Therefore as by the offence of one judgment came upon all men to condemnation; even so by the righteousness of one the free gift came upon all men unto justification of life.*

*19 For as by one man's disobedience many were made sinners, so by the obedience of one shall many be made righteous.*

*20 Moreover the law entered, that the offence might abound. But where sin abounded, grace did much more abound:*

*21 That as sin hath reigned unto death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life by Jesus Christ our Lord.*

<!-- post 10790 · 2023-06-19 07:44 · study -->

Therefore. 

When we see this word we have to ask ourselves "what's it there for?" What does it refer back to?

Well, 4 chapters of Paul logically and with irrefutable proof from scripture that all are under sin and need salvation, and that salvation is through faith in Jesus Christ, by the grace of God. 

Therefore. Therefore what?

*Being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ.*

As the heavenly hosts proclaimed in the hills of Bethlehem at the birth of the man Christ Jesus;

*Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will toward men.*

The peace and goodwill on earth was come from God to man. Not the peace of men not warring. Not goodwill between tribes. But the enmity of wrath in the transgressions of man made justified by the peace offering of His only begotten Son. 

We are justified by faith alone. There is nothing more noted. We have already concluded that by the works of the law no man shall be justified. We already know that by faith, Abraham became father to all who believe. We already know that the judgment of God is upon Jew and Greek alike. And that Jesus as propitiation for our sin has given us salvation from the wrath we deserve. 

*Therefore being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ.* 

What's more; By faith we have access into the grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God. 

Believing, we are given grace, and by grace, God is given the glory for our salvation. Note, "wherein we stand." This isn't the future, this isn't a process. This is now, at the time of our repentance towards belief in Christ as our redeemer we then, at that moment, stand in grace. And rejoice. 

And we not only rejoice in the glory of God's grace that provides us with salvation through faith, because it shows the mercy and judgment and righteousness of God (which remember is one way His glory is revealed to man from Heaven), we also glory in tribulations here on earth, knowing they are a refining fire; that worketh patience, which brings experience, and experience brings hope. Hope builds faith.

*Now faith is the substance of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen*.

Hope is the sure knowledge that a thing will be that is not yet. The object of faith. Tribulation in our lives, not only persecution for our faith, but just general troubles like health, finance, whatever things common to all men, saved or unsaved, all of it gives opportunity to glorify the Lord, as it makes our faith sharper as we are brought out of it by His mercy and blessings, better for having experienced it. 

And our hope is fufifiled in the love of God, by the comforter, the Holy Ghost, which is given us, who indwells with us, quickening our spirit and leading us to that peace which passes all understanding, even in turmoil and crisis. 

Our faith in man, traditions, our own works, all will leave us ashamed at some point. But the hope we have in Christ Jesus will never bring us shame. God never lets us down.

<!-- post 10791 · 2023-06-19 07:59 · study -->

And how do we know that God's word is good, that our hope is true? Because 

*But God commendeth his love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us*.

That when we were still enemies, working inequities, dead in sin, God reconciled us through the death of His Son. God loved His enemies, and offered the free gift of salvation by the shedding of blood of His only begotten Son. 

Think about this, for in it is the righteousness of God. 

And if He offered us such a gift, if a righteous man gave His life for us while we were enemies, dying for us while we were dead in sin, then how much more shall He do for us whom have been reconciled to Him in faith by the death of His Son? Who stand in His grace? We shall be saved by His life in the resurrection. 

And not only so, that we shall know we will be saved out of tribulation by His resurrection and the promise of God to us likewise for eternal life, but we also joy, rejoice, in God through our Lord Jesus Christ by whom we have now received the atonement. 

Our hope is that, today we who believe are spared wrath for our sins, adopted to the promise, and that in tribulation on this earth we will be saved by God's love for His sons, because of the atonement of Christ Jesus that we holdfast to in faith. Amen

<!-- post 10792 · 2023-06-19 08:08 · study -->

The first law was to Adam, and it was simple. Do not eat of the fruit of the tree in the midst of the garden. For you will surely die. 

And Adam broke the single law in paradise. And by his sin, death came up on the world. Paradise was denied us, and the way of the world, short and brutish lives. And sin multiplied, and death. All through one man. 

This is not original sin, btw. There is no sin that follows man from birth, but only the understanding that it is in our nature, even under perfect conditions as Eden, that we still fall short. 

And so the law came, to Noah, to Abraham, to Moses, and by Sinai, so detailed and expanded, much more than the single commandment of the Garden, so that man had to know that the holiness of God was unattainable by their works, and that faith in His grace alone was our only hope. 

And with the law, sin abounded much the more, that God's grace may abound to His glory. 

So that by one man, the man Christ Jesus, and His righteousness and His willingness to die for us, and not only those who believe, but for every man and all sin, he gave His life that we may enter into peace with the Father by faith, in His grace. To His glory. That the righteousness of God be shown the world.

<!-- post 10793 · 2023-06-19 08:15 · study -->

It becomes impossible for those who maintain that "vicarious atonement isn't found in scripture," that Jesus wasn't the sacrifice for our sins, once we have reached Romans 5. The propitiation that is Christ is the atonement we have. The reconciliation is in His blood. Our hope is in His life from death. 

When we were righteous? No, as sinners. Did we earn it through works, the keeping of the law? No, that would be debt, it is the imputed righteousness from Christ that justifies us in faith. It is the law of grace. We don't deserve it. 

It is the free gift given by grace, which is by one man, Jesus Christ. The gift of His righteousness that we may be justified through Him to God. By His obedience to the end, and the death on the cross for all men, that in His resurrection we may enter into grace by faith. 

*That as sin hath reigned unto death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life by Jesus Christ our Lord.*

<!-- post 10794 · 2023-06-19 08:20 · study -->

What shall we say then? Do you continue in the vain works of the law seeking to justify yourselves? To put God in debt to you for your own righteousness? 

Well, we have concluded by logical analysis that this is impossible. 

If you are counting on your works to justify you before God in any way, you do not have faith in Christ, you call God a liar, and you reject His free gift. 

If this is you, and you have been told all your life by men that you have to deserve grace to be saved or to keep your salvation, you cannot read the epistle to the Romans and not see the folly of that false gospel. Repent. 

Trust God, and enter into peace with Him through Christ. Have faith in the hope of the free gift of eternal life.

## Romans 5  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2022-04-29

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/606

**Romans 5**

*2 By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.*

*8 But God commendeth his love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.*

*9 Much more then, being now justified by his blood, we shall be saved from wrath through him.*

*10 For if, when we were enemies, we were reconciled to God by the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we shall be saved by his life.*

*11 And not only so, but we also joy in God through our Lord Jesus Christ, by whom we have now received the atonement.*

*12 Wherefore, as by one man sin entered into the world, and death by sin; and so death passed upon all men, for that all have sinned:*

*13 (For until the law sin was in the world: but sin is not imputed when there is no law.*

*14 Nevertheless death reigned from Adam to Moses, even over them that had not sinned after the similitude of Adam's transgression, who is the figure of him that was to come.*

*15 But not as the offence, so also is the free gift. For if through the offence of one many be dead, much more the grace of God, and the gift by grace, which is by one man, Jesus Christ, hath abounded unto many.*

*16 And not as it was by one that sinned, so is the gift: for the judgment was by one to condemnation, but the free gift is of many offences unto justification.*

*17 For if by one man's offence death reigned by one; much more they which receive abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness shall reign in life by one, Jesus Christ.)*

*18 Therefore as by the offence of one judgment came upon all men to condemnation; even so by the righteousness of one the free gift came upon all men unto justification of life.*

*19 For as by one man's disobedience many were made sinners, so by the obedience of one shall many be made righteous.*

*20 Moreover the law entered, that the offence might abound. But where sin abounded, grace did much more abound:*

*21 That as sin hath reigned unto death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life by Jesus Christ our Lord.*

**Romans 5**
*Therefore being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ:*

*2 By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.*

*3 And not only so, but we glory in tribulations also: knowing that tribulation worketh patience;*

*4 And patience, experience; and experience, hope:*

*5 And hope maketh not ashamed; because the love of God is shed abroad in our hearts by the Holy Ghost which is given unto us.*

*6 For when we were yet without strength, in due time Christ died for the ungodly.*

*7 For scarcely for a righteous man will one die: yet peradventure for a good man some would even dare to die.*

*8 But God commendeth his love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.*

*9 Much more then, being now justified by his blood, we shall be saved from wrath through him.*

*10 For if, when we were enemies, we were reconciled to God by the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we shall be saved by his life.*

*11 And not only so, but we also joy in God through our Lord Jesus Christ, by whom we have now received the atonement.*

*15 But not as the offence, so also is the free gift. For if through the offence of one many be dead, much more the grace of God, and the gift by grace, which is by one man, Jesus Christ, hath abounded unto many.*

*16 And not as it was by one that sinned, so is the gift: for the judgment was by one to condemnation, but the free gift is of many offences unto justification.*

*17 For if by one man's offence death reigned by one; much more they which receive abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness shall reign in life by one, Jesus Christ.)*

*18 Therefore as by the offence of one judgment came upon all men to condemnation; even so by the righteousness of one the free gift came upon all men unto justification of life.*

<!-- post 12325 · 2023-10-22 06:20 · study -->

I wasn't going to take detours in this exercise of Dispensationalism in light of the book of Romans, but I cannot overlook the beauty of chapter 5 and the understanding that everything is in Christ Jesus; *By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.*

Note this carefully. The Calvanist says the special boys chosen by God have access to faith by grace, but the Bible says the exact opposite. We access His grace, which abounds and is to all men, by faith. Just as Adam accessed death by the choice of sin, we access life in Jesus by the same free will choice. 

*Therefore as by the offence of one judgment came upon all men to condemnation; even so by the righteousness of one, the free gift came upon all men unto the justification of life.* 

Salvation is a free gift. Justification is of Christ. Offered to ALL MEN, by grace. Free. A gift, given without works, without being reckoned of debt. Without our having the glory. Without boasting, which is excluded. 

*Therefore being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ.* 

This is the Gospel. And it is to all men. With the law or without. While we were yet sinners, Christ died for us. 

*That as sin hath reigned unto death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life by Jesus Christ our Lord.*

<!-- post 12326 · 2023-10-22 06:33 · study -->

Again, it is impossible to read "*Therefore being justified by faith..."* and come away with "it's faith plus works." 

Just as it's impossible to read *"to all men*" and say it's only for special boys, chosen because of their unique wonderfulness, "Hey, don't question a sovereign God. He just thinks I am super special and awesome." 

God is not a respecter of persons. 

It is impossible to say the Mosaic law did anything other than show man our own sinful nature, just as Adam transgressed under one commandment, then so too man transgressed all the more abundantly under the 600 or so contained in the law.

There is no fair reading of Romans that leads to these heresies. It is only by being a heretic first, and then searching for whatever scripture they can wrest with to torture into confirming their own vain imagination. 

There are some who say the complex sentences and thoughts can be difficult for some to understand, but luckily for us God is not the author of confusion, and has Paul in the Spirit provide clear and concise summaries of each point as he concludes each argument. 

"There is neither Jew nor Greek...."

"Therefore by the deeds of the law there shall no flesh be justified in his sight..."

"To him that worketh not but believes..."

"Therefore being justified by faith..."

<!-- post 14362 · 2024-06-17 06:58 · study -->

The Lord tells us love our enemies. That we who are saved, that have received the atonement through Jesus Christ, Who died for us while we were yet sinners, who were His enemies now reconciled in the blood of the lamb, because God is kind to the unthankful and to the evil, should judge our enemies with the same spirit of grace, longsuffering and mercy that God has shown us. 

His mercy is such that we are promised by the Lord Jesus Christ that though we suffer loss, are despised for His name's sake, will be blessed. We are poor, and ours is the kingdom of God. We hunger, but we shall be filled. We weep, but we shall laugh. And when we are persecuted, we should leap for joy, because we are rewarded in Heaven. 

And here on earth, our generosity, our putting others needs before our own, even to the thankless and those that seek to do evil, we shall be rewarded greatly, here on earth as well as in Heaven. *For with the same measure that ye mete withal it shall be measured to you again.*

It can be hard to not lose sight of the mercy of God that has been given to us. To take for granted so great a gift as Christ. To forget the pattern of prayer Jesus taught us, asking the Father to *forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors*. Why?

*For if ye forgive men their trespasses, your heavenly Father will also forgive you: But if ye forgive not men their trespasses, neither will your Father forgive your trespasses.*

<!-- post 14363 · 2024-06-17 07:00 · study -->

Today, I am grateful for the forgiveness and mercy of the Father, and His love towards me, through the sacrifice of Christ. I pray for those that have made me their enemies, that they may find peace and forgiveness in Jesus. Amen.

CHRISTIAN THEOLOGY
the reconciliation of God and humankind through Jesus Christ.

Propitiation, atonement, justification. These are the principles by which Biblical doctrines of salvation rest. 

Jesus is the sole propitiation for our sin, the atonement that makes us justified before God. 

Not by deeds of the law. Not by works, least any man should boast. By faith are we saved. 

*Therefore leaving the principles of the doctrine of Christ, let us go on unto perfection; not laying again the foundation of repentance from dead works, and of faith toward God.*

## Romans 6  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-20

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10801

<!-- post 10801 · 2023-06-20 05:10 · scripture -->

**Romans 6**
*What shall we say then? Shall we continue in sin, that grace may abound?*

*2 God forbid. How shall we, that are dead to sin, live any longer therein?*

*3 Know ye not, that so many of us as were baptized into Jesus Christ were baptized into his death?*

*4 Therefore we are buried with him by baptism into death: that like as Christ was raised up from the dead by the glory of the Father, even so we also should walk in newness of life.*

*5 For if we have been planted together in the likeness of his death, we shall be also in the likeness of his resurrection:*

*6 Knowing this, that our old man is crucified with him, that the body of sin might be destroyed, that henceforth we should not serve sin.*

*7 For he that is dead is freed from sin.*

*8 Now if we be dead with Christ, we believe that we shall also live with him:*

*9 Knowing that Christ being raised from the dead dieth no more; death hath no more dominion over him.*

*10 For in that he died, he died unto sin once: but in that he liveth, he liveth unto God.*

*11 Likewise reckon ye also yourselves to be dead indeed unto sin, but alive unto God through Jesus Christ our Lord.*

*12 Let not sin therefore reign in your mortal body, that ye should obey it in the lusts thereof.*

*13 Neither yield ye your members as instruments of unrighteousness unto sin: but yield yourselves unto God, as those that are alive from the dead, and your members as instruments of righteousness unto God.*

*14 For sin shall not have dominion over you: for ye are not under the law, but under grace.*

*15 What then? shall we sin, because we are not under the law, but under grace? God forbid.*

*16 Know ye not, that to whom ye yield yourselves servants to obey, his servants ye are to whom ye obey; whether of sin unto death, or of obedience unto righteousness?*

*17 But God be thanked, that ye were the servants of sin, but ye have obeyed from the heart that form of doctrine which was delivered you.*

*18 Being then made free from sin, ye became the servants of righteousness.*

*19 I speak after the manner of men because of the infirmity of your flesh: for as ye have yielded your members servants to uncleanness and to iniquity unto iniquity; even so now yield your members servants to righteousness unto holiness.*

*20 For when ye were the servants of sin, ye were free from righteousness.*

*21 What fruit had ye then in those things whereof ye are now ashamed? for the end of those things is death.*

*22 But now being made free from sin, and become servants to God, ye have your fruit unto holiness, and the end everlasting life.*

*23 For the wages of sin is death; but the gift of God is eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord.*

<!-- post 10802 · 2023-06-20 05:27 · study -->

**Romans 6** builds on the conclusion of the doctrine of the law of grace, and the implications for that liberty which we have in Christ. That we are no longer dead in sin with the judgment against us in unrighteousness, but born again a new man in the imputed righteousness of Jesus Christ. 

Having proven this, that we are justified before God by faith, and standfast in His grace, what then?

Shall we sin that grace may abound? If it glorified God to be forgiven our sin, would it be to His glory that we sin all the more? (Again, as some slanderously reported about the apostles, and those who continue this smear today, calling the glory of God "cheap grace" and "a license to sin")

Again, *God forbid. How should we, that are dead to sin, love any longer therein?* 

We are free, in Christ, that when we are baptized as believers, it is into death with Christ, just as when we rise up from the water, it is a picture of our rising with Him into eternal life. 

Even so, we should walk in newness of life. 

If we are picturing the image of Christ in our baptism, death, burial and resurrection, then our old man, our sinful nature after the flesh should be considered nailed to the cross with Him. *That the body of sin might be destroyed.* 

Being dead in Christ we are risen with Him, and risen, death has no more dominion over Him, or us through Him. We are dead to sin but alive unto God through Jesus Christ our Lord. Paul is really hammering this point home because it is not automatic that we who are saved turn from our sins. That the new man's presence doesn't preclude the old man's lusts of the flesh from warring in our members. Our eyes and tongue betray us. Our feet are swift to sin. 

If we automatically became holy by our salvation, this wouldn't have to be taught us, and it wouldn't be given us as a reminder in nearly every epistle. If we were given to "show fruit" to prove we are saved, as Lordshippers and other hypocrites suggest, it wouldn't be necessary to explain the old man vs the new.

<!-- post 10803 · 2023-06-20 05:45 · study -->

*Being then made free from sin, ye became the servants of righteousness.*

We are free to serve God being pardoned by His grace. To work for righteousness, as its servant out of love for Him that justified the ungodly, while we were yet sinners. 

We who deserved death now are servants of life, unto God and should act like it. Note also in his appeal to walk in the new man, we are never told that we should lose the grace we standfast in. Rather, that we have our fruit unto holiness and the end everlasting life. We now earn rewards in the kingdom heaven, vs earning the wages of sin which is death. 

*For the wages of sin is death; but the gift of God is eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord.*

Gift vs wage. We do not earn gifts. A wage is debt owed. In sin, our debt is paid by death and hell, but in Christ we are given grace, a free gift, salvation and liberty that we may serve Him. And in that service we earn holiness, towards the righteousness of God, rewards. 

**1 Corinthians 3:9-15**
*For we are labourers together with God: ye are God's husbandry, ye are God's building. According to the grace of God which is given unto me, as a wise masterbuilder, I have laid the foundation, and another buildeth thereon. But let every man take heed how he buildeth thereupon. For other foundation can no man lay than that is laid, which is Jesus Christ. Now if any man build upon this foundation gold, silver, precious stones, wood, hay, stubble; Every man's work shall be made manifest: for the day shall declare it, because it shall be revealed by fire; and the fire shall try every man's work of what sort it is. If any man's work abide which he hath built thereupon, he shall receive a reward. If any man's work shall be burned, he shall suffer loss: but he himself shall be saved; yet so as by fire.*

We see here that as laborers for God, working as husbandmen in His vineyard, servants of His righteousness, that we will be judged as laborers, and earn rewards for our works. Each deed we do that is built up on the foundation of Christ will be judged by fire, and those of no spiritual importance will burn like wood, hay and stubble, but those of lasting impact, especially the salvation of souls, will be manifest as gold, silver and precious stones. And though some of our effort is burned by fire, as a loss, not being of real value, we ourselves are still saved, yet so as by fire. The same fire that reveals the fruit of our labors will be to us who believe a refining fire, but to them without hope, destruction in eternal torment. 

*For the scripture saith, Thou shalt not muzzle the ox that treadeth out the corn. And, The labourer is worthy of his reward.*

God repays. Be it the wages of sin with death or rewards in the kingdom to come.

## Romans 7  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-22

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10819

<!-- post 10819 · 2023-06-22 05:12 · scripture -->

**Romans 7**
*Know ye not, brethren, (for I speak to them that know the law,) how that the law hath dominion over a man as long as he liveth?*

*2 For the woman which hath an husband is bound by the law to her husband so long as he liveth; but if the husband be dead, she is loosed from the law of her husband.*

*3 So then if, while her husband liveth, she be married to another man, she shall be called an adulteress: but if her husband be dead, she is free from that law; so that she is no adulteress, though she be married to another man.*

*4 Wherefore, my brethren, ye also are become dead to the law by the body of Christ; that ye should be married to another, even to him who is raised from the dead, that we should bring forth fruit unto God.*

*5 For when we were in the flesh, the motions of sins, which were by the law, did work in our members to bring forth fruit unto death.*

*6 But now we are delivered from the law, that being dead wherein we were held; that we should serve in newness of spirit, and not in the oldness of the letter.*

*7 What shall we say then? Is the law sin? God forbid. Nay, I had not known sin, but by the law: for I had not known lust, except the law had said, Thou shalt not covet.*

*8 But sin, taking occasion by the commandment, wrought in me all manner of concupiscence. For without the law sin was dead.*

*9 For I was alive without the law once: but when the commandment came, sin revived, and I died.*

*10 And the commandment, which was ordained to life, I found to be unto death.*

*11 For sin, taking occasion by the commandment, deceived me, and by it slew me.*

*12 Wherefore the law is holy, and the commandment holy, and just, and good.*

*13 Was then that which is good made death unto me? God forbid. But sin, that it might appear sin, working death in me by that which is good; that sin by the commandment might become exceeding sinful.*

*14 For we know that the law is spiritual: but I am carnal, sold under sin.*

*15 For that which I do I allow not: for what I would, that do I not; but what I hate, that do I.*

*16 If then I do that which I would not, I consent unto the law that it is good.*

*17 Now then it is no more I that do it, but sin that dwelleth in me.*

*18 For I know that in me (that is, in my flesh,) dwelleth no good thing: for to will is present with me; but how to perform that which is good I find not.*

*19 For the good that I would I do not: but the evil which I would not, that I do.*

*20 Now if I do that I would not, it is no more I that do it, but sin that dwelleth in me.*

*21 I find then a law, that, when I would do good, evil is present with me.*

*22 For I delight in the law of God after the inward man:*

*23 But I see another law in my members, warring against the law of my mind, and bringing me into captivity to the law of sin which is in my members.*

*24 O wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me from the body of this death?*

*25 I thank God through Jesus Christ our Lord. So then with the mind I myself serve the law of God; but with the flesh the law of sin*.

<!-- post 10820 · 2023-06-22 05:13 · study -->

**Galatians 5**
*16 This I say then, Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfil the lust of the flesh.*

*17 For the flesh lusteth against the Spirit, and the Spirit against the flesh: and these are contrary the one to the other: so that ye cannot do the things that ye would.*

*18 But if ye be led of the Spirit, ye are not under the law.*

*19 Now the works of the flesh are manifest, which are these; Adultery, fornication, uncleanness, lasciviousness,*

*20 Idolatry, witchcraft, hatred, variance, emulations, wrath, strife, seditions, heresies,*

*21 Envyings, murders, drunkenness, revellings, and such like: of the which I tell you before, as I have also told you in time past, that they which do such things shall not inherit the kingdom of God.*

*22 But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, longsuffering, gentleness, goodness, faith,*

*23 Meekness, temperance: against such there is no law.*

*24 And they that are Christ's have crucified the flesh with the affections and lusts.*

*25 If we live in the Spirit, let us also walk in the Spirit.*

*26 Let us not be desirous of vain glory, provoking one another, envying one another.*

<!-- post 10821 · 2023-06-22 05:16 · study -->

**1 Corinthians 15**
*42 So also is the resurrection of the dead. It is sown in corruption; it is raised in incorruption:*

*43 It is sown in dishonour; it is raised in glory: it is sown in weakness; it is raised in power:*

*44 It is sown a natural body; it is raised a spiritual body. There is a natural body, and there is a spiritual body.*

*45 And so it is written, The first man Adam was made a living soul; the last Adam was made a quickening spirit.*

*46 Howbeit that was not first which is spiritual, but that which is natural; and afterward that which is spiritual.*

*47 The first man is of the earth, earthy; the second man is the Lord from heaven.*

*48 As is the earthy, such are they also that are earthy: and as is the heavenly, such are they also that are heavenly.*

*49 And as we have borne the image of the earthy, we shall also bear the image of the heavenly.*

*50 Now this I say, brethren, that flesh and blood cannot inherit the kingdom of God; neither doth corruption inherit incorruption.*

*51 Behold, I shew you a mystery; We shall not all sleep, but we shall all be changed,*

*52 In a moment, in the twinkling of an eye, at the last trump: for the trumpet shall sound, and the dead shall be raised incorruptible, and we shall be changed.*

*53 For this corruptible must put on incorruption, and this mortal must put on immortality.*

*54 So when this corruptible shall have put on incorruption, and this mortal shall have put on immortality, then shall be brought to pass the saying that is written, Death is swallowed up in victory.*

*55 O death, where is thy sting? O grave, where is thy victory?*

*56 The sting of death is sin; and the strength of sin is the law.*

*57 But thanks be to God, which giveth us the victory through our Lord Jesus Christ.*

<!-- post 10822 · 2023-06-22 05:32 · study -->

And so, building on the implications of our faith, the law and grace, Paul comes to the matter of our flesh, in this world before the resurrection, but after our salvation and the quickening of our spirit in Christ. 

Christ, in whose death, we die also to the law, freed then to the Spirit, married to Him in grace. Just as the woman described by Paul is free from her marriage vows at the death of her husband, we are free from sin and death with Jesus. And baptism is a like figure representing this. We are born again, in the Spirit.

But not in the flesh. That's the lively hope we have, Jesus being the first fruits of the resurrection with the same promise to us who believe. That we will shed this corruptible body for incorruptible. The dead in Christ and those that are alive and remain at His coming, all will receive glorified bodies that know not sin. Chapter 8 of Romans will delve a bit more into this, but then the question for Christians is; in the meantime, what?

Our new man, inside, spirit quickened by the Holy Ghost, sealed unto the day of redemption, knows not sin. It can't, being dead to the law. 

For as Paul explains, with the commands of God to tell us what is righteous and holy, there is no sin. 

He says he was alive once without the law, we all have been. Innocent children who don't know there is a right from wrong, do no sin. But when the knowledge of the law comes, and they in the flesh choose sin, the spirit dies to the law. 

Had Adam and Eve not had the commandment in the garden, they wouldn't have sinned in eating the apple. Had the laws of God not come onto Noah and then Moses, showing us His light, we wouldn't know our flesh of the old man leads us to darkness. The law itself is holy and true. And being saved, our spirit is willing but our flesh is weak. 

The old man still reigns in our members. Our flesh. 

Sin takes advantage of the law to show we lust in the flesh. Not that the law is evil, but we are. 

And like Paul, we do what we would not. We don't do what we should, despite wanting to. Without the newness of spirit we can't. 

*For I know that in me (that is, in my flesh,) dwelleth no good thing: for to will is present with me; but how to perform that which is good I find not.*

As Jesus admonished the Apostles who were to keep watch as He prayed at Gethsemane, who fell asleep multiple times;

**Matthew 26:41**
*Watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation: the spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak.*

<!-- post 10823 · 2023-06-22 05:40 · study -->

It is important to know as a Christian, we are not perfect and sinless perfection isn't possible in our flesh. 

We all are sinners. We all deserve death and Hell but in Christ we are given His righteousness that we may be given justification before God. 

We sin, every day. But unlike the unsaved (which we were and should never forget the mercy we were shown, that we not think too highly of ourselves before the unsaved), we have the Spirit within us, and our own spiritual new man cries out to do good.

But to the flesh, we are called to sin. 

What do we do? The Bible says if we walk in the spirit, feeding that new man, praying, studying scripture, going to church to be edified with the congregation, practicing true religion which is care for the poor, widows, fatherless, and most importantly sharing the Gospel with others that they may be comforted as we are by the promise, that the flesh becomes easier to mortify. To subdue.

<!-- post 10824 · 2023-06-22 05:47 · study -->

And, if we walk in the spirit we will also learn more about the holiness of God and His law, and in that knowledge come to see more things that are sinful. It is impossible to feed the new man with the things of God and not come to be convicted by the Spirit that things we thought not sin, actually are. But the great mystery of that dichotomy is that, despite the knowledge increasing our occasion for the flesh, we have the grace and power to escape from it. 

**1 Corinthians 10:13**
*There hath no temptation taken you but such as is common to man: but God is faithful, who will not suffer you to be tempted above that ye are able; but will with the temptation also make a way to escape, that ye may be able to bear it.*

It is a refining process. One that removes thorns in our flesh we didn't know we had. This process of sanctification makes us more valuable vessels for God's will. It makes us better able to serve His Gospel. To have real fruits of the spirit. To win others to Christ, and further, to instruct and guide babes in Christ to maturity that they may be able to better serve God as well. 

This is the point of our Christian walk. 

It's not about you. It's not about finding yourself, or happiness or whatever new age and pagan selfish nonsense our flesh comes up with to lead us away from the spirit. 

We are saved already, and justified by the perfect work of Christ Jesus that we believe on. Rejoice. In His blood we have overcome. 

Our sanctification isn't about salvation. It is about becoming acceptable tools in God's shed. To be workmen who aren't ashamed. Good and faithful servants.

<!-- post 10825 · 2023-06-22 05:55 · study -->

*O wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me from the body of this death?*

Some false prophets use Romans 7 to teach a works-based, repent of your sins salvation. They use similar verses to teach that if you have sin you aren't saved. They will even claim they themselves are without sin.

**1 John 1:8**
*If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us.*

**Revelation 22**
*But the fearful, and unbelieving, and the abominable, and murderers, and whoremongers, and sorcerers, and idolaters, and all liars, shall have their part in the lake which burneth with fire and brimstone: which is the second death.*

These people are vipers. They have a false gospel. They produce no fruit, just bitter people unable to ever come to perfection, and trusting in their own works to save them. 

Or worse, they make hypocrites, "fruit inspectors," who spend their time being holier than thou and touting their own righteousness, and glorying in their separation, but who never do the actual works of the spirit. Who can't win someone to Christ. At best, if they believed at one point, they create nothing but wood, hay and stubble. 

But most aren't believers and just bring filthy rags before the judgment seat of Christ, saying *Lord, Lord, have we not prophesied in thy name? and in thy name have cast out devils? and in thy name done many wonderful works?*

And He will say to them, depart from me I never knew you.

<!-- post 10826 · 2023-06-22 05:57 · study -->

And so we wait patiently for the reward of eternal life, and glorified bodies that know not sin. 

We who are yet carnal, have a mind for God being renewed in the Spirit. We still have occasion to sin as long as we are in the flesh. 

*But thanks be to God, which giveth us the victory through our Lord Jesus Christ.*

<!-- post 10827 · 2023-06-22 06:02 · study -->

**Romans 7**, like most of the chapters leads directly into the next, the theology building upon each argument. Chapter 7 sets up the arguments made in 8, just as it rests on the foundation of the previous chapters. We are blessed that we have the whole word of God. Preserved for all time. That we may study to be approved. Comparing scripture with scripture. 

Because, by doing so we are given power over our flesh that we may restrain the lusts of our members, and do what's right. That we may have access to the fruit of the Spirit;

**Galatians 5:22-23**
*But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, longsuffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, Meekness, temperance: against such there is no law.*

Amen

<!-- post 10828 · 2023-06-22 07:14 · study -->

And just one more point that I've touched on here but needs to seriously be stressed; the fruits of the Spirit don't come automatically.. Just because we are saved doesn't mean that we will do good. Just as it doesn't mean we will stop sinning. 

Paul, who many consider the greatest Christian of all time for his understanding of God's word, writes of himself here in Romans chapter 7;

*But I see another law in my members, warring against the law of my mind, and bringing me into captivity to the law of sin which is in my members.*

Writing in **1 Timothy 1:15**
*This is a faithful saying, and worthy of all acceptation, that Christ Jesus came into the world to save sinners; of whom I am chief.*

Paul and the other authors of the epistles write to be edifying to the brethren, that they *should* do good, that they *should not* do evil. That we *should* walk in the spirit that we do not fulfill the lusts of the flesh. 

This is a choice. As Jesus says;

**Luke 9:23**
*And he said to them all, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross daily, and follow me.*

It's a daily choice. It is on us to reject the temptations of the flesh by walking in the spirit. 

But it is not automatic. Even Paul struggled with sin. 

So many people beat themselves up for failing to stop sin in their life. For falling short. But what does the Bible tells us? That all fall short. 

**1 John 1:9**
*If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness*.

And so we are instructed; *Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfil the lust of the flesh.*

And when we stumble, confess to God who has adopted us as sons, and He will forgive us and help us with our infirmities. 

Don't allow the men of this world to judge your walk, and in doing so place stumbling blocks and snares for you. Those hypocrites have their reward. Instead, every day make a commitment to walk in the spirit, do for others, what you can. And give glory to God for His righteousness and mercy, in Christ Jesus. The rest will sort itself out.
