## Romans 7  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2023-10-22

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/12330

<!-- post 12330 · 2023-10-22 07:25 · scripture -->

**Romans 7**
*Know ye not, brethren, (for I speak to them that know the law,) how that the law hath dominion over a man as long as he liveth?*

*2 For the woman which hath an husband is bound by the law to her husband so long as he liveth; but if the husband be dead, she is loosed from the law of her husband.*

*3 So then if, while her husband liveth, she be married to another man, she shall be called an adulteress: but if her husband be dead, she is free from that law; so that she is no adulteress, though she be married to another man.*

*4 Wherefore, my brethren, ye also are become dead to the law by the body of Christ; that ye should be married to another, even to him who is raised from the dead, that we should bring forth fruit unto God.*

*5 For when we were in the flesh, the motions of sins, which were by the law, did work in our members to bring forth fruit unto death.*

6 *But now we are delivered from the law, that being dead wherein we were held; that we should serve in newness of spirit, and not in the oldness of the letter.*

<!-- post 12331 · 2023-10-22 07:28 · study -->

Addressing the Jew, brethren after the flesh, those knowing the law, Paul continues his argument that the law is the ministry of death, while the gospel of grace is unto life. 

But to buttress the point, Paul uses the law to demonstrate how the law is made null by the death of Christ, just as a marriage is annulled by the death of the husband. The oldness of the letter is done away with for the newness of the Spirit.

Paul is again dismantling the idea of salvation by the law. The same law that Judaizers sought to bring to the new believers of the Gentiles. The same law that dispensationalists claim was the means of salvation for the Jews, and that it will be implemented again. As if a widow should take her dead husband again as a spouse. 

 *O wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me from the body of this death? I thank God through Jesus Christ our Lord. So then with the mind I myself serve the law of God; but with the flesh the law of sin.*

Amen 🙏🏻

## Romans 8  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-23

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10832

<!-- post 10832 · 2023-06-23 07:01 · scripture -->

**Romans 8**
*There is therefore now no condemnation to them which are in Christ Jesus, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.*

*2 For the law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus hath made me free from the law of sin and death.*

*3 For what the law could not do, in that it was weak through the flesh, God sending his own Son in the likeness of sinful flesh, and for sin, condemned sin in the flesh:*

*4 That the righteousness of the law might be fulfilled in us, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.*

*5 For they that are after the flesh do mind the things of the flesh; but they that are after the Spirit the things of the Spirit.*

*6 For to be carnally minded is death; but to be spiritually minded is life and peace.*

*7 Because the carnal mind is enmity against God: for it is not subject to the law of God, neither indeed can be.*

*8 So then they that are in the flesh cannot please God.*

*9 But ye are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit, if so be that the Spirit of God dwell in you. Now if any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of his.*

*10 And if Christ be in you, the body is dead because of sin; but the Spirit is life because of righteousness.*

*11 But if the Spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead dwell in you, he that raised up Christ from the dead shall also quicken your mortal bodies by his Spirit that dwelleth in you.*

*12 Therefore, brethren, we are debtors, not to the flesh, to live after the flesh.*

*13 For if ye live after the flesh, ye shall die: but if ye through the Spirit do mortify the deeds of the body, ye shall live.*

*14 For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, they are the sons of God.*

*15 For ye have not received the spirit of bondage again to fear; but ye have received the Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry, Abba, Father.*

*16 The Spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God:*

*17 And if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together.*

*18 For I reckon that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed in us.*

*19 For the earnest expectation of the creature waiteth for the manifestation of the sons of God.*

*20 For the creature was made subject to vanity, not willingly, but by reason of him who hath subjected the same in hope,*

*21 Because the creature itself also shall be delivered from the bondage of corruption into the glorious liberty of the children of God.*

*22 For we know that the whole creation groaneth and travaileth in pain together until now.*

*23 And not only they, but ourselves also, which have the firstfruits of the Spirit, even we ourselves groan within ourselves, waiting for the adoption, to wit, the redemption of our body.*

*24 For we are saved by hope: but hope that is seen is not hope: for what a man seeth, why doth he yet hope for?*

*25 But if we hope for that we see not, then do we with patience wait for it.*

*26 Likewise the Spirit also helpeth our infirmities: for we know not what we should pray for as we ought: but the Spirit itself maketh intercession for us with groanings which cannot be uttered.*

*27 And he that searcheth the hearts knoweth what is the mind of the Spirit, because he maketh intercession for the saints according to the will of God.*

*28 And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose.*

*29 For whom he did foreknow, he also did predestinate to be conformed to the image of his Son, that he might be the firstborn among many brethren.*

*30 Moreover whom he did predestinate, them he also called: and whom he called, them he also justified: and whom he justified, them he also glorified.*

*31 What shall we then say to these things? If God be for us, who can be against us?*

*32 He that spared not his own Son, but delivered him up for us all, how shall he not with him also freely give us all things?*

*33 Who shall lay any thing to the charge of God's elect? It is God that justifieth.*

<!-- post 10833 · 2023-06-23 07:01 · scripture -->

*34 Who is he that condemneth? It is Christ that died, yea rather, that is risen again, who is even at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us.*

*35 Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?*

*36 As it is written, For thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter.*

*37 Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us.*

*38 For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come,*

*39 Nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.*

<!-- post 10834 · 2023-06-23 07:03 · study -->

**Psalms 90:10** 
*The days of our years are threescore years and ten; and if by reason of strength they be fourscore years, yet is their strength labour and sorrow; for it is soon cut off, and we fly away.*

**Psalms 91:16** 
*With long life will I satisfy him, and shew him my salvation.*

**Proverbs 10:27**
*The fear of the LORD prolongeth days: but the years of the wicked shall be shortened.*

**1 Corinthians 15:53-55**
*For this corruptible must put on incorruption, and this mortal must put on immortality. So when this corruptible shall have put on incorruption, and this mortal shall have put on immortality, then shall be brought to pass the saying that is written, Death is swallowed up in victory. O death, where is thy sting? O grave, where is thy victory?*

<!-- post 10835 · 2023-06-23 07:23 · study -->

*O wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me from the body of this death?I thank God through Jesus Christ our Lord. So then with the mind I myself serve the law of God; but with the flesh the law of sin.*

**Romans 7** explains the war within the saved between the spirit and the flesh, that the flesh worketh the law of sin, while the Spirit worketh the law of God. That our flesh has us do what we would not. And not do what we would. That our temporal bodies are in a battle with the new man, the quickened spirit within us, the Spirit of God that resides in our hearts. 

But that if we walk after the Spirit, we will do the things of God. But if we walk after the flesh, we will go after the lusts of the flesh. 

But we are delivered from this mortal body. The promise of God is that as we receive the Spirit as the earnest of our adoption, that the day of redemption is when it is fulfilled with a glorified body, unto eternal life and without the temptations of the flesh. 

**Ephesians 1:12-14**
*That we should be to the praise of his glory, who first trusted in Christ. In whom ye also trusted, after that ye heard the word of truth, the gospel of your salvation: in whom also after that ye believed, ye were sealed with that holy Spirit of promise, Which is the earnest of our inheritance until the redemption of the purchased possession, unto the praise of his glory.*

Earnest is money, sometimes called a “good faith deposit,” is a sum that is included with your offer to purchase something to show your seriousness of intent to go through with the purchase, which is also called redemption. The Spirit is the earnest of God, given in Christ, sealing us as His until the resurrection, whereby we are redeemed. 

**1 Corinthians 6:20**
*For ye are bought with a price: therefore glorify God in your body, and in your spirit, which are God's.*

<!-- post 10836 · 2023-06-23 07:30 · study -->

And so, back to Romans, Paul is offering the hope we have, while yet in sinful flesh, that we have been purchased with a price, the blood of Christ, and adopted by God as heirs in faith. To be delivered upon at the day of His glorious coming. 

Therefore, brethren, we are debtors, not to the flesh, to live after the flesh...The Spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the Children of God. 

If we are children, then heirs of God, joint heirs with Christ. The kingdom is ours, as princes of it. Wherein are many mansions prepared for us. Glorified bodies that have no want of need or corruption. The streets, paved with gold. Given rewards for our faithful service. 

But, here on earth, we still are in the flesh. And if we walk not in the Spirit, the lusts of the flesh will we do. And that is often frustrating and depressing for we who are saved. 

O wretched men that we are. 

But Paul, writing in the Spirit says hey, it's ok. We will be saved out of this condition. You have the Spirit testifying within you. You know you are saved. Sealed. And that the sufferings of this life are nothing in comparison to the glory of the life to come in Christ. If we are tempted and successful in mortifying our members, if the Spirit working in us brings us from sin, we may suffer for God, but to glory everlasting. 

And if we fail, and sin and the flesh dies, we still have eternity in the same glory, that this temporary condition will be no more. So rejoice, rather than despair. We are not like those that don't believe. We know for certain our final condition. And so we walk in the Spirit that we may not do the things of the flesh, that we may serve God and store up treasure in Heaven through works worthy of those adopted sons.

<!-- post 10837 · 2023-06-23 07:43 · study -->

*For we know that the whole creation groaneth and travaileth in pain together until now. And not only they, but ourselves also, which have the firstfruits of the Spirit, even we ourselves groan within ourselves, waiting for the adoption, to wit, the redemption of our body. For we are saved by hope: but hope that is seen is not hope: for what a man seeth, why doth he yet hope for?*
*But if we hope for that we see not, then do we with patience wait for it.*

All of creation travailing in pain, as the pangs of birth, waiting for the day of our redemption. Not only they, but ourselves also. 

There is a day to come where we will be redeemed and the Bible says that all of creation is like a woman giving birth, a new thing to be born, and that we ourselves are also in travail, having the firstfruits of the Spirit within us, that earnest expectation, sealed with that promise unto that day of redemption. This is our hope. 

Hope is an earnest expectation of what is yet to come. It is the object of our faith. It isn't seen. It isn't present, it is what is to come. Having that deposit, we know we will be purchased. But we groan in ourselves waiting for that day. Stuck in the flesh. 

But the Comforter, that is the Spirit of God as He resides within us, He does the will of God and works. 

*Likewise the Spirit also helpeth our infirmities: for we know not what we should pray for as we ought: but the Spirit itself maketh intercession for us with groanings which cannot be uttered. And he that searcheth the hearts knoweth what is the mind of the Spirit, because he maketh intercession for the saints according to the will of God.*

The Spirit, if we let Him, works on our infirmities, helps us with removing the thorns in our hearts that otherwise lead us to sin. He heals us as we walk in the Spirit. 

But more, the Spirit is an intercessor, bringing our needs before the Father, even that which we don't know we have, that His will be done through us. 

Thus we are conformed to His will, and in all things that glorify God and shape us as worthy vessels, as we walk in the Spirit and feed that new man inside.

<!-- post 10838 · 2023-06-23 08:13 · study -->

**Ephesians 1:3-6** 
*Blessed be the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, who hath blessed us with all spiritual blessings in heavenly places in Christ: According as he hath chosen us in him before the foundation of the world, that we should be holy and without blame before him in love: Having predestinated us unto the adoption of children by Jesus Christ to himself, according to the good pleasure of his will, To the praise of the glory of his grace, wherein he hath made us accepted in the beloved*.

So what is this that is predestined? The saved? Or Christ Jesus by whom we are saved? 

Calvinists read this and **Romans 8**, and place the emphasis on themselves, rather than Christ, and so, they claim, that God picks who will believe. This doctrine of devils, like all false gospels, is based on putting themselves before the Lord. Just as works salvation is predicated on the person's own righteousness, not Christ's, the special boy doctrine makes it that they are the elect, not of Jesus perfect work, the lamb slain before the foundation of the world, that all who believe on Him will be saved, but by God's "sovereign will."

But we know that God's will is not for a chosen few.

**1 Timothy 2:3-4**
*For this is good and acceptable in the sight of God our Saviour; Who will have all men to be saved, and to come unto the knowledge of the truth.*

If God exerted His sovereign will, we would all be saved. 

As Jesus says, 

*And this is the will of him that sent me, that every one which seeth the Son, and believeth on him, may have everlasting life: and I will raise him up at the last day.*

Every one. 

Limited atonement is a doctrine of devils. It is based on pride. 

So we know that all who believe are predestinated in Christ that they will have eternal life, sealed by the Spirit, and if they walk in tha Spirit, will be conformed to the use of God, that we may become separated, to be servants of the Gospel. 

And so continuing the theme of Romans 8, the context of which is the works of the Spirit.

That while we are yet in the flesh, knowing the day of redemption will free us from that wretched state, that the Spirit helps heal our infirmities that led us to sin and conforms us to His will, so long as we walk after the Spirit, not feeding the flesh with the things of this world. That the Spirit within us acts also as an intercessor to bring our needs before the Father, so that His will be done in us. 

And so, the good news;

*And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose.*

Who is called? Who is drawn to faith in God's promise? 

**John 12:32-33**
*And I, if I be lifted up from the earth, will draw all men unto me. This he said, signifying what death he should die.*

All men. But few will answer this call, and seek to be transformed to fulfill His intended purpose for us. The harvest is great but the laborers few. Strait is the gate and narrow the way and few there be that find it. Every name of every person born of water upon this earth is in the book of life. But their names are striken from it when they die in sin, unbelieving. (Or reprobates, twice-dead)

No where in the Bible do we see names added, only erased. God's foreknowledge, knowing of the end from the beginning, means He knows who will answer the call. It doesn't mean He selects them.

<!-- post 10839 · 2023-06-23 09:07 · study -->

*And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose. For whom he did foreknow, he also did predestinate to be conformed to the image of his Son, that he might be the firstborn among many brethren. Moreover whom he did predestinate, them he also called: and whom he called, them he also justified: and whom he justified, them he also glorified. What shall we then say to these things? If God be for us, who can be against us? He that spared not his own Son, but delivered him up for us all, how shall he not with him also freely give us all things?*

And so, being justified by faith, being given the promise of eternal life, having our spirit quickened, being sealed by the earnest expectation of the day of redemption by the Spirit, testifying within us, we are promised that in our cries, in our gutteral utterances and incomprehensible groanings as we travail in this life, awaiting another, as the flesh frustrates our will to do that which we should, God promises that His Spirit as intercessor, and that all we ask for or need He will know and provide, as it conforms to His will. 

The Father who gave us His only begotten Son as a sacrifice for our sin, that we may be adopted heirs into His Kingdom, will not be slack concerning His promises to us. He will give us all we need. And all is good, even tribulation, because we love God and have faith in His righteousness. 

If we walk in the spirit, we shouldn't worry about the things of this world. Money, politics, persecution, the various temptations that Satan and his children leave for us as snares. These are temporary. Material. Worldly. 

Instead we should focus on the eternal. The will of the Father. Servants to the Gospel. The just will live by faith.

<!-- post 10840 · 2023-06-23 09:13 · study -->

*Who shall lay any thing to the charge of God's elect? It is God that justifieth.*

Who can say to us that are justified by the imputed righteousness of Jesus Christ, predestined in Him to be heirs of God, that our sin in the flesh has disqualified our spirit and the promise? 

Who can tell you you've been taken from Him, if God's word is that we cannot lose what he has given us?

**John 10:28-30**
*And I give unto them eternal life; and they shall never perish, neither shall any man pluck them out of my hand. My Father, which gave them me, is greater than all; and no man is able to pluck them out of my Father's hand. I and my Father are one*.

The eternal security of salvation is the purpose of the Spirit indwelling us. That we may KNOW we have eternal life. That this wretched condition of the flesh will end. And the new man inside us will take on incorruption at the redemption. 

Let no man tell you you can lose your salvation. Let no man lay change against you who are in Christ. Let no man lead you back to the yoke of the law, which is death. For we have eternal life with the Father through the Son, testified by the Spirit.

*Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword? As it is written, For thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter. Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us. For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come, Nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.*

Amen, amen, amen!

## Romans 8  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2023-10-22

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/12332

<!-- post 12332 · 2023-10-22 07:35 · scripture -->

**Romans 8**
*There is therefore now no condemnation to them which are in Christ Jesus, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.*

*2 For the law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus hath made me free from the law of sin and death.*

*3 For what the law could not do, in that it was weak through the flesh, God sending his own Son in the likeness of sinful flesh, and for sin, condemned sin in the flesh:*

*4 That the righteousness of the law might be fulfilled in us, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.*

*5 For they that are after the flesh do mind the things of the flesh; but they that are after the Spirit the things of the Spirit.*

*6 For to be carnally minded is death; but to be spiritually minded is life and peace.*

*7 Because the carnal mind is enmity against God: for it is not subject to the law of God, neither indeed can be.*

*8 So then they that are in the flesh cannot please God.*

*9 But ye are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit, if so be that the Spirit of God dwell in you. Now if any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of his.*

*10 And if Christ be in you, the body is dead because of sin; but the Spirit is life because of righteousness.*

*11 But if the Spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead dwell in you, he that raised up Christ from the dead shall also quicken your mortal bodies by his Spirit that dwelleth in you.*

*12 Therefore, brethren, we are debtors, not to the flesh, to live after the flesh.*

*13 For if ye live after the flesh, ye shall die: but if ye through the Spirit do mortify the deeds of the body, ye shall live.*

*14 For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, they are the sons of God.*

*15 For ye have not received the spirit of bondage again to fear; but ye have received the Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry, Abba, Father.*

*16 The Spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God:*

*17 And if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together.*

<!-- post 12333 · 2023-10-22 07:50 · study -->

Paul is transitioning from the previous arguments, to build upon them that salvation is by faith. By the Spirit and not the words or by the deeds of the flesh. But again, in contrasting the law of the flesh with the law of faith, Paul shows how the promises and inheritance are to those that believe. That believers are adopted sons of God, crying Abba, Father. That the Spirit itself bears witness to this adoption. That we are the children of God, and those without Christ are none of His. 

We are heirs of God, joint-heirs with Christ. Not co-religionists with Jews. Not co-heirs with them. But with Christ. And unbelieving Jews have no claim to the promise. 

Unbelieving Jews living by the law of the flesh die after the flesh. It is only by the Spirit of Christ that the deeds of the body are mortified, made dead. To no effect. Because we live, made alive by the Spirit. 

But Jews are dead in their flesh. Not of His. Not children of God having not the Spirit of adoption. Not heirs of God or co-heirs with Christ, because they reject Him. They are carnal. They have the spirit of bondage. They are of the flesh. 

*So then they that are in the flesh cannot please God.* 

Similar to **Hebrews 11:6**; 
*But without faith it is impossible to please him...*

The unregenerate Jews are of the flesh and have not faith. They cannot please God. The Bible says it is impossible. The wrath abides on them to the uttermost. 

But the dispensationalist will say they will come to believe. They will come to faith in the end. "God isn't through with His people." 

And yet the same dispensationalism they profess claims they will be reinstituting the works of the law. The sacrifices and the ordinances, which we know is the ministration of death. It is the law of the flesh, and by this law it is impossible to please God. 

The contradiction of their heresy cannot stand up to the scrutiny of the word. *Let God be true and every man a liar.*

<!-- post 12334 · 2023-10-22 07:54 · study -->

*Likewise the Spirit also helpeth our infirmities: for we know not what we should pray for as we ought: but the Spirit itself maketh intercession for us with groanings which cannot be uttered. And he that searcheth the hearts knoweth what is the mind of the Spirit, because he maketh intercession for the saints according to the will of God.*

Another side note: It is the Spirit that makes intercession for the saints, it is not the saints who make intercession for people praying to them. But Catholicism inverts this truth. 

Saints are all believers, the righteousness of the saints is Christ's.

<!-- post 12335 · 2023-10-22 08:03 · study -->

*What shall we then say to these things? If God be for us, who can be against us? He that spared not his own Son, but delivered him up for us all, how shall he not with him also freely give us all things? Who shall lay any thing to the charge of God's elect? It is God that justifieth. Who is he that condemneth? It is Christ that died, yea rather, that is risen again, who is even at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us.* 

*Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword? As it is written, For thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter. Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us.*

*For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come, Nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.*

Another side note, from another clear and concise conclusion to another argument, made by Paul: We who believe are adopted into the faith that is without repentance. Eternal life of the gift. The Spirit creates a new man in the believers and seals us to the day of redemption, where we look forward to being raised up in glorified bodies. Our hope that we do not see but know is to come, the same Spirit testifying within us as the surety of the promise. 

And nothing can separate us from God once we have received the Spirit, which is by faith, through grace. 

We may know we have eternal life and that life is in Jesus. 

Once saved always saved is not just a slogan, it is the truth of the Gospel and the crux of the Gospel, without which you cannot claim to have faith. You cannot claim to believe on Christ and at the same time maintain that it can be lost.

<!-- post 12336 · 2023-10-22 10:25 · study -->

Really thinking about this and when when we talk about the Gospel, we of course talk about what one is trusting in to be saved. That God so loved the world that He gave His only begotten Son that whosoever believes on Him shall not perish but have everlasting life. 

Each part of that is true. Each part of it is important to believe. Without any aspect, you're missing the picture of what our faith is held in. 

We not only believe that there is a God. That out of love for His creation, He sent His only begotten Son to die for us. That Jesus existed. That faith in His work not only saves us from Hell, but gives us eternal life. 

It is that last bit that is our blessed hope. It is the unseen thing that we wait patiently for. That we are called to be longsuffering towards as we wait. 

We seek the resurrection. To be shed of the sinful flesh and to fully be the new man. That is our hope. We await Jesus' return because that is the hour of our redemption. Where we are changed in a twinkling of an eye. Where corruption puts on incorruption.

If you aren't trusting God that that's a sure thing for believing Jesus died and rose again, when His word tells us again and again that that is the payoff for that faith, then you don't have a hope in Hell.

<!-- post 766 · 2022-05-02 05:44 · scripture -->

*34 Who is he that condemneth? It is Christ that died, yea rather, that is risen again, who is even at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us.*

*35 Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?*

*36 As it is written, For thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter.*

*37 Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us.*

*38 For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come,*

*39 Nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.*

<!-- post 767 · 2022-05-02 06:05 · study -->

Paul has for the past 7 chapters laid out the factual, Biblical case for *conditional election,* that **we must believe to be saved,** starting with the testimonial of God in creation, the lack of excuse by the unbelieving, the refusal to accept the knowledge of God of the reprobate, and the surety of our salvation of placing our faith in God's promise. 

Here, tying together these themes, he speaks of the carnal mind that the saved still have, exhorting the saved by faith to live in the spirit, not the flesh, because in that righteousness given us is adoption into the promise; we become heirs of Christ, we call out to our Father. In verse 9, he makes it clear that this is a **conditional** state of being. You aren't adopted without first believing.

Then, Paul begins building onto this foundational theme of being adopted as sons in the spirit by explaining how God's foreknowledge that we (the saved) then be used to His purpose. (Note the order of operations in verse 29, calvanists reverse it, and thus get none of it).

The glory of being joint heirs with Christ, in Christ. The hope, which is an earnest expectation for the thing we believe to come (not a long-shot wish as some use the term) being shared with all of creation, waiting for that kingdom to come. That we are blameless to charge by any because we are justified in our faith in Christ. That, in faith, none can stand before us as God gives us all things, according to His will. 

Having laid out the first faith and its terms and rewards, Paul is making the case to the saved to understand what their faith has opened them up to, what God's gifts mean to us if we now walk in the spirit, quickened by the Holy Ghost, that all things are for good to us for loving God, if we simply trust further in the word of God, building faith upon faith.

<!-- post 768 · 2022-05-02 06:11 · study -->

Sanctification becomes a post hoc form of works salvation, wishing it be enough. Rather than giving hope, it cruelly removes it. 

Wicked

**Romans 9**
 *I say the truth in Christ, I lie not, my conscience also bearing me witness in the Holy Ghost,*

*2 That I have great heaviness and continual sorrow in my heart.*

*3 For I could wish that myself were accursed from Christ for my brethren, my kinsmen according to the flesh:*

*4 Who are Israelites; to whom pertaineth the adoption, and the glory, and the covenants, and the giving of the law, and the service of God, and the promises;*

*5 Whose are the fathers, and of whom as concerning the flesh Christ came, who is over all, God blessed for ever. Amen.*

*6 Not as though the word of God hath taken none effect. For they are not all Israel, which are of Israel:*

*7 Neither, because they are the seed of Abraham, are they all children: but, In Isaac shall thy seed be called.*

*8 That is, They which are the children of the flesh, these are not the children of God: but the children of the promise are counted for the seed.*

*9 For this is the word of promise, At this time will I come, and Sarah shall have a son.*

*10 And not only this; but when Rebecca also had conceived by one, even by our father Isaac;*

*11 (For the children being not yet born, neither having done any good or evil, that the purpose of God according to election might stand, not of works, but of him that calleth;)*

*12 It was said unto her, The elder shall serve the younger.*

*13 As it is written, Jacob have I loved, but Esau have I hated.*

*14 What shall we say then? Is there unrighteousness with God? God forbid.*

*15 For he saith to Moses, I will have mercy on whom I will have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I will have compassion.*

*16 So then it is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy.*

*17 For the scripture saith unto Pharaoh, Even for this same purpose have I raised thee up, that I might shew my power in thee, and that my name might be declared throughout all the earth.*

*18 Therefore hath he mercy on whom he will have mercy, and whom he will he hardeneth.*

*19 Thou wilt say then unto me, Why doth he yet find fault? For who hath resisted his will?*

*20 Nay but, O man, who art thou that repliest against God? Shall the thing formed say to him that formed it, Why hast thou made me thus?*

*21 Hath not the potter power over the clay, of the same lump to make one vessel unto honour, and another unto dishonour?*

*22 What if God, willing to shew his wrath, and to make his power known, endured with much longsuffering the vessels of wrath fitted to destruction:*

*23 And that he might make known the riches of his glory on the vessels of mercy, which he had afore prepared unto glory,*

*24 Even us, whom he hath called, not of the Jews only, but also of the Gentiles?*

*25 As he saith also in Osee, I will call them my people, which were not my people; and her beloved, which was not beloved.*

*26 And it shall come to pass, that in the place where it was said unto them, Ye are not my people; there shall they be called the children of the living God.*

*27 Esaias also crieth concerning Israel, Though the number of the children of Israel be as the sand of the sea, a remnant shall be saved:*

*28 For he will finish the work, and cut it short in righteousness: because a short work will the Lord make upon the earth.*

*29 And as Esaias said before, Except the Lord of Sabaoth had left us a seed, we had been as Sodoma, and been made like unto Gomorrha.*

*30 What shall we say then? That the Gentiles, which followed not after righteousness, have attained to righteousness, even the righteousness which is of faith.*

*31 But Israel, which followed after the law of righteousness, hath not attained to the law of righteousness.*

*32 Wherefore? Because they sought it not by faith, but as it were by the works of the law. For they stumbled at that stumblingstone;*

*33 As it is written, Behold, I lay in Sion a stumblingstone and rock of offence: and whosoever believeth on him shall not be ashamed.*

<!-- post 10857 · 2023-06-24 08:00 · study -->

Having established all things regarding salvation by faith, and the implications to man: believers unto righteousness found in Christ Jesus, the new man, the Spirit, and the promise of the incorruptible that comes at the redemption (as well as the sin of the flesh in the meantime), unbelief unto death, and reprobates, twice-dead, hearts hardened, Paul now moves on to addressing the Jews after the flesh, considering these settled arguments from before. 

If all these things are true, and the gospel is by faith in Christ, which the prophets who came before all testified, and the law was unto death (not because of the unrighteousness of the law, but rather the infirmities of the people), and if all are under sin, Jew or Gentile, and all require faith in Christ to be saved, for there is no difference, what then?

Paul begins by expressing his sorrow that his kinsmen, the unbelieving children of Israel after the flesh are cut off. That they have had the Kingdom taken from them, as Jesus said. 

*Israelites; to whom pertaineth the adoption, and the glory, and the covenants, and the giving of the law, and the service of God, and the promises; Whose are the fathers, and of whom as concerning the flesh Christ came, who is over all, God blessed for ever. Amen.*

All of these blessings, and the seed pertaining to the promise that is Christ Jesus (concerning the flesh, note the distinction, especially those "mother of God" heretics), and yet Israel are cut off. 

*They which are the children of the flesh, these are not the children of God*.

(Dispensationalists should read that again and again until it sinks in)

...*but the children of the promise are counted for the seed. For this is the word of promise, At this time will I come, and Sarah shall have a son. And not only this; but when Rebecca also had conceived by one, even by our father Isaac*.

The promise was of the seed, the seed being Christ in the flesh, who came of the line of Jacob, born second but given the inheritance and the blessing over Esau. 

**Malachi 1:2-3**
*I have loved you, saith the Lord. Yet ye say, Wherein hast thou loved us? Was not Esau Jacob's brother? saith the Lord: yet I loved Jacob, And I hated Esau, and laid his mountains and his heritage waste for the dragons of the wilderness.*

This is pertaining to the seed, the promise of Christ, knowing Edom would be made desolate, the election is of Jacob/Israel, that Jesus would come from Judah, the root of Jesse, from the throne of David. 

This is not about individual salvation of Esau or Jacob. The election of the seed is in Christ. And it served God that the older served the younger in the promise,  in the same way that the Jew made way for the Gentile. 

But the law says the older shall have the inheritance , so what then, is God unrighteous? 

*God forbid. For he saith to Moses, I will have mercy on whom I will have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I will have compassion.*

We trust the judgment of God. Who knows beginning from end. That all are chosen in Christ, not unto belief. Not given faith by God, but predestined in Christ to be given the blessing, the promise and the inheritance, to all who believe. 

And so God, in His wisdom, shows mercy to who He will show mercy. And compassion on whom He will. 

And harden the hearts of those fitted for destruction. All to His glory. Because as we have established; the righteousness of God is revealed both in His mercy towards us, and the love that gave His only begotten Son for us, but in His wrath against those who hold the truth in unrighteousness.

<!-- post 10858 · 2023-06-24 08:06 · study -->

And so, as Paul in the Spirit testifies, he wishes that his brothers after the flesh would come to the faith. But they are cut off. Just as Esau become Edom, and they were made desolate, Jews were cut off and consumed. 

Is God's judgment unrighteous? No. Is His word unfaithful? Again no, for a remnant remains, those saved by faith as is Paul, and to believing Jews whom he writes to in Rome. But the unbelieving Jew, hearts hardened, had their chance, and their destruction served to show the glory of God. 

*For the scripture saith unto Pharaoh, Even for this same purpose have I raised thee up, that I might shew my power in thee, and that my name might be declared throughout all the earth.*

Just as Pharaoh was made reprobate, so too are the Jews after the flesh. 

God's promises were kept in Christ. The remnant is in Christ. All Israel was saved, in Christ. The Kingdom, the land that was promised, is through Christ. The blessings were to the seed, and that seed is singular; Christ.

<!-- post 10859 · 2023-06-24 08:10 · study -->

Both Calvanism and Zionism are about making the promises and the blessings, the election and our shaping to God's purpose, about man. They are special boys. 

And as special boys, their pride takes the glory of God and makes it their own. Just as works-based salvation makes their righteousness the center of their religion. Just as Catholicism makes it about their earthly church. 

All heresy is based on taking the focus away from the glory of God through Christ Jesus and making it man's. The creature putting themselves above the Creator, who is blessed forever amen.

<!-- post 10860 · 2023-06-24 08:19 · study -->

Is God then unrighteous? Is it unfair that some are saved and others burned, that those who are elected in faith through Christ are shaped to His purpose, while those who reject the faith and deny the truth have their hearts eventually hardened, cut off and burned?

*Thou wilt say then unto me, Why doth he yet find fault? For who hath resisted his will? Nay but, O man, who art thou that repliest against God? Shall the thing formed say to him that formed it, Why hast thou made me thus? Hath not the potter power over the clay, of the same lump to make one vessel unto honour, and another unto dishonour?*

Who are we to judge God? 

*But we are sure that the judgment of God is according to truth*...

It is His will to be done. That Jesua would be born of a woman, live, be crucified and raised again, to be made Lord as His son, and that all who believe be given eternal life, sealed by the Spirit and given hope to the resurrection, heirs to the Kingdom. Made sons of God. 

This is the predestination of God. Not picking and choosing who believes, but the elected in Christ, using those who do to His purpose. 

And if His purpose is to show His glory and righteousness through those vessels of wrath, then so be it. 

*We are sure that the judgment of God is according to truth against them which commit such things.*

<!-- post 10861 · 2023-06-24 08:28 · study -->

*What if God, willing to shew his wrath, and to make his power known, endured with much longsuffering the vessels of wrath fitted to destruction: And that he might make known the riches of his glory on the vessels of mercy, which he had afore prepared unto glory, Even us, whom he hath called, not of the Jews only, but also of the Gentiles?*

As the prophets spake, moved by the Spirit, God promised to bring men from all nations to be His. And as the prophets warned the Jews, that except for the seed, being Jesus, He would have destroyed Israel sooner, they would have been as Sodom. But being fitted to the purpose, He showed mercy, until establishing Jesus in Zion, a sure foundation for the believers, Jew and Gentile, but for the unbelieving Jews, a stumblingblock. A rock of offense. 

And this is the argument Paul is making. It isn't about calvanism or Jews after the flesh, it is about Christ. Whatever twisted doctrines can be wrung out of Romans 9, the context is clear:

*What shall we say then? That the Gentiles, which followed not after righteousness, have attained to righteousness, even the righteousness which is of faith. But Israel, which followed after the law of righteousness, hath not attained to the law of righteousness. Wherefore? Because they sought it not by faith, but as it were by the works of the law. For they stumbled at that stumblingstone*.

Gentiles, through Christ have found righteousness. Jews, through the law, death. Why? Because they sought their own righteousness through works, rather than seek the imputed righteousness of Jesus, by faith. 

It is the same argument Paul has been making since chapter 1. 

We are saved by faith. It is God's righteousness, not our own. It is for His glory, not ours. Boasting is excluded. We are but clay to be shaped. The Creator is greater than the creation.

<!-- post 827 · 2022-05-03 06:18 · study -->

Here Paul begins dealing with the JQ. While Calvinism uses these verses, which are about the use of nations, rulers, sinners and believers to His purpose, as being about our salvation, they do so ignoring the scriptural, Spiritual arguments that have been building throughout the epistle. 

Having established God's word, His grace, our faith, the doctrine of Christ and the condition of the saved being heirs to the promise, adopted sons, he turns to explaining the unrepentant Jew's position with God, relative to the old covenant and salvation in Christ. Replacement theology is the theme. Dispies hate this. 

Note what the stumblingstone preventing the Jews from being partakers of the promise; faith. They seek righteousness by the works of the law, not faith. 

But WHOSOEVER believeth on Jesus Christ shall not be ashamed for trusting God's promise.

<!-- post 828 · 2022-05-03 06:24 · study -->

Verse 19 and 20 of Romans 9 addresses the foolish predestination argument that God's will is such that none can resist, "so how can He judge?" But it's God's foreknowledge crafting vessels for use, to his glory. This again is tied into the context of nations and people, not righteousness (He uses Pharaoh for example).

<!-- post 829 · 2022-05-03 06:25 · study -->

As for the "remnant shall be saved;" dispensationalism heresy would have that mean in the future. That from the modern state calling itself Israel, God will select them to inherit blessings and the kingdom. But Paul is speaking of the Jews of the flesh, like himself, who are saved in Christ by faith.

<!-- post 830 · 2022-05-03 06:57 · study -->

The problem with taking an implication from a partial bit of scripture and forming a Doctrine around it is obvious when you apply it to something like this. 

God gives us statements that are clear regarding doctrine. He doesn't leave us to create doctrine from implication in isolation. God is not the author of confusion. 

So if someone offers a Doctrine that's based on a partial verse taken out of context that appears to contradict clearly stated Doctrine we shouldn't then hammer it till it fits but discard it as being incorrect.

**Romans 10**
*Brethren, my heart's desire and prayer to God for Israel is, that they might be saved.*

*6 But the righteousness which is of faith speaketh on this wise, Say not in thine heart, Who shall ascend into heaven? (that is, to bring Christ down from above:)*

*7 Or, Who shall descend into the deep? (that is, to bring up Christ again from the dead.)*

*8 But what saith it? The word is nigh thee, even in thy mouth, and in thy heart: that is, the word of faith, which we preach;*

*9 That if thou shalt confess with thy mouth the Lord Jesus, and shalt believe in thine heart that God hath raised him from the dead, thou shalt be saved.*

*10 For with the heart man believeth unto righteousness; and with the mouth confession is made unto salvation.*

*11 For the scripture saith, Whosoever believeth on him shall not be ashamed.*

*12 For there is no difference between the Jew and the Greek: for the same Lord over all is rich unto all that call upon him.*

*13 For whosoever shall call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.*

*14 How then shall they call on him in whom they have not believed? and how shall they believe in him of whom they have not heard? and how shall they hear without a preacher?*

*15 And how shall they preach, except they be sent? as it is written, How beautiful are the feet of them that preach the gospel of peace, and bring glad tidings of good things!*

*16 But they have not all obeyed the gospel. For Esaias saith, Lord, who hath believed our report?*

*17 So then faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the word of God.*

*18 But I say, Have they not heard? Yes verily, their sound went into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*

*19 But I say, Did not Israel know? First Moses saith, I will provoke you to jealousy by them that are no people, and by a foolish nation I will anger you.*

*20 But Esaias is very bold, and saith, I was found of them that sought me not; I was made manifest unto them that asked not after me.*

*21 But to Israel he saith, All day long I have stretched forth my hands unto a disobedient and gainsaying people.*

*Behold, your house is left unto you desolate.*

<!-- post 10872 · 2023-06-25 09:50 · study -->

Following the end of the argument regarding Israel after the flesh and the utter rejection of the idea that they are saved by their kinship to Abraham in chapter 9, Paul begins chapter 10 of Romans by lamenting this sad fact; that his own people, Israel after the flesh have shut themselves out of the kingdom by seeking their own righteousness by works of the law rather than the righteousness of God by faith.

*Brethren, my heart's desire and prayer to God for Israel is, that they might be saved. For I bear them record that they have a zeal of God, but not according to knowledge.*

This same prayer and sad refrain, a testimony of ignorant zeal that is preventing those trusting in their works from obeying the law of faith, could apply to so many zealous Catholics and Orthodox, Lordshippers and Repent-of-your-sins types. Those who place their works, their turning from sin, pursuit of their own righteousness to be made worthy, rather than the humility to understand that none are righteous, that they may obtain God's grace and mercy through faith. They have a zeal for God, but go about to set up their own righteousness to their destruction. Just like Israel after the flesh. 

*For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to every one that believeth.*

They lack the understanding that by the end of the law for righteousness, actually establishes the law. **Romans 3**:

*Do we then make void the law through faith? God forbid: yea, we establish the law.*

God's judgment reveals His holy righteousness through His mercy and His wrath. His law is the instrument by which we understand it, and our failings and the need for salvation by grace through faith. And by submission to the righteousness of God, we become able to truly fulfill the law. Without the spectre of death and hell, but rather with charity and love through faith.

<!-- post 10873 · 2023-06-25 10:00 · study -->

And so, Paul using Deuteronomy, familiar scripture to the believing Jews receiving his epistle to the churches at Rome, shows that the receiving of the law through Moses was the understanding of Christ. 

And that understanding of Christ is put into one of the most beautiful passages of scripture, testifying to the salvation that is by faith on Jesus, and the truth of the nature of the Gospel.

*But what saith it? The word is nigh thee, even in thy mouth, and in thy heart: that is, the word of faith, which we preach; That if thou shalt confess with thy mouth the Lord Jesus, and shalt believe in thine heart that God hath raised him from the dead, thou shalt be saved. For with the heart man believeth unto righteousness; and with the mouth confession is made unto salvation. For the scripture saith, Whosoever believeth on him shall not be ashamed. For there is no difference between the Jew and the Greek: for the same Lord over all is rich unto all that call upon him. For whosoever shall call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.*

That's the simplicity of the Gospel. That's all of the law of faith. This is the righteousness of God that we obtain through Christ by faith, summed up. Complete. 

There is no other way to Him except by this. There are no works, no special boy status, no secret traditions, ordinances, sacraments or other deeds of the law. And knowing this, then we must preach this glad tiding, this good news, that others may by the power of God's word, hear, believe and confess unto salvation. 

*How then shall they call on him in whom they have not believed? and how shall they believe in him of whom they have not heard? and how shall they hear without a preacher? And how shall they preach, except they be sent? as it is written, How beautiful are the feet of them that preach the gospel of peace, and bring glad tidings of good things!*

<!-- post 10874 · 2023-06-25 10:17 · study -->

Our entire purpose as vessels fit for righteousness, is spreading and sending others to spread the word of God, that He may have the increase. That the harvest, which is plentiful have laborers to bring in the sheaves. That we be servants of the Gospel. Ministers of His word. 

**1 Corinthians 3:5-8**
*Who then is Paul, and who is Apollos, but ministers by whom ye believed, even as the Lord gave to every man? I have planted, Apollos watered; but God gave the increase. So then neither is he that planteth any thing, neither he that watereth; but God that giveth the increase. Now he that planteth and he that watereth are one: and every man shall receive his own reward according to his own labour.*

These are the works that matter. That have eternal value. That, unlike the wood, hay and stubble of our own vain works and filthy rags of our own righteousness which burn up, the winning of souls and edification of others that they may win souls are the precious stones, silver and gold that remain. The eternal value of those things we build up on the foundation that is Christ. 

We must be sent, into the highways and hedges, preaching the Gospel to every creature, so that they may hear the word of God, and by hearing, believe, and by their faith, confess the truth with their mouths, that they too may be sent. This is the way. 

Note the power is in the word we preach. The word of God found in scripture. 

*So then faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the word of God.*

The power to save men's souls is in God's word. Not our logical arguments, not in our boasts or testimonials. Certainly not in our selfish acts of following the letter of the law, the vain traditions of men, washing the outside of the cup while the inside is filthy. But by scripture.

*Faith cometh by hearing and hearing by the word of God*, preached by those that believe, sent to gather for the harvest. 

*For the word of God is quick, and powerful, and sharper than any twoedged sword, piercing even to the dividing asunder of soul and spirit, and of the joints and marrow, and is a discerner of the thoughts and intents of the heart.*

This is an important idea for saved Christians to grasp. That for all the efforts made to send people tracts, pocket New Testaments, door hangers and invitations to church, and our own attempts to get people to believe by our rephrasing and paraphrasing and using logic, none of it saves.

*Faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the word of God.*

The power is in the word. The Spirit works through the word, given by believers to bring men to faith. And so we should not hide that light under a bushel. We should shout it from the rooftops. 

*For I am not ashamed of the gospel of Christ: for it is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth*.

<!-- post 10875 · 2023-06-25 10:22 · study -->

But back to Paul's lament; Israel has heard. They had the advantage of the oracles of God. They had the law. They had the testimony of Moses and the prophets, the warnings and appeals made by a longsuffering and faithful God, Who would have gathered them up as His, but they would not. They killed the prophets, they killed Christ Jesus, and are contrary to all men. 

And so they are shut out of the kingdom and are hurled into outer darkness with wailing and gnashing of teeth. Tearfully seeking repentance, but there is none to be found.

<!-- post 899 · 2022-05-04 05:27 · study -->

Verse 1, Paul prays that his fellow Israelites of the flesh be saved, by what? Faith. 

Verse 2, because of their zeal for the Lord which he testifies is real. Many unsaved Israelites loved and wanted to serve God, zealously. 

*But not according to knowledge*. Knowledge of what? The Gospel of Christ which is faith.

I feel this same way for all those who I know, in real life and online, that I work with and am friends with, who zealously serve God the way they know, but *not according to knowledge,* doing so with vain works of the law, and so, *are not saved.* 

Why? Because Christ is the end of the law of righteousness, to EVERYONE who believes on Him. Once you obey the Gospel, that is believe on Jesus Christ and His perfect work on the cross, death, burial and resurrection, you do not have to rely on works of the law to try and obtain righteousness. 

Paul says stop wondering who is going to heaven or hell based on their adherence to the law, because doing so denies that we are made righteous *in* Christ, by faith, and so denies His work that we are counting on.

And what is the word of faith that we preach? It's right there in your heart if you're saved, and the Spirit puts it on your tongue to be shared;

*That if thou confess with thy mouth the Lord Jesus and shalt believe in thine heart that God raised him from the dead, THOU SHALL BE SAVED.*

Period. This is the Gospel. To obey the Gospel is to believe this one truth. There's nothing else regarding righteousness and eternal life to know. 

There is no secret trick, no hidden gnostic secret. There is no catch. No strings. No being worthy, because no one is worthy of the righteousness of Christ. Just believe. And whosoever believes is saved. Not a chosen people. Not a limited few, picked to believe. Not those who do works, indulge in sacraments, keep Sabbaths, go to church...but those who believe the report given, by whom all prophets testified, given by Jesus Christ in the flesh, and shared by His disciples straight through unto this day.

<!-- post 900 · 2022-05-04 05:35 · study -->

For scripture says "*Whosoever* believeth on Him shall not be ashamed." 

And because there is no difference between "Jew and Greek," there is no "chosen people." Dispensationalism rests entirely on the idea that there is, and so they deny the word of God. 

*For whosoever call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.* 

Adam believed. Able believed. Seth called upon His name. Enoch believed. Noah believed. Abraham believed and it was counted upon him for righteousness. Sara, Issac, Joseph, Moses, Joshua..all OT saints, they all are said to have believed and called upon the name of the Lord. 

That's it. There is no "and..."

There is no church you must join, sacrament you must have, no works of the law, no special boy status, no hypocritical "fruit inspection."

 No other act, regardless of zeal and sincere hope in those acts, can save anyone.

<!-- post 901 · 2022-05-04 05:39 · study -->

That the purpose of the believer is to preach the Gospel, using God's word so that the unsaved hear, and by hearing believe, and believing, call upon the name of the Lord. 

And then sending them to do the same.

<!-- post 902 · 2022-05-04 05:41 · study -->

Verse 16 says it plainly: The Jews (and all unsaved, Jew or Greek), have not obeyed the Gospel, *because they have not believed our report*.

<!-- post 903 · 2022-05-04 06:00 · study -->

The Bible says those works are hay and stubble. They burn up, and only the work God has made us to do, in serving Him is giving the report of the good news by which we have hope unto eternal life to others that they may be saved.

Works without faith in Christ as the sole means of salvation are dead and cannot save anyone. Faith, even to him that worketh not, saves.

God doesn't hear the prayers of those who don't depend on faith alone for righteousness, or care about their works:

**Matthew 7**
*21 Not every one that saith unto me, Lord, Lord, shall enter into the kingdom of heaven; but he that doeth the will of my Father which is in heaven.*

*22 Many will say to me in that day, Lord, Lord, have we not prophesied in thy name? and in thy name have cast out devils? and in thy name done many wonderful works?*

*23 And then will I profess unto them, I never knew you: depart from me, ye that work iniquity.*

Please do not be like the Jews Paul is writing about. Do not be among those counting on their works before Christ. 

If you believe that God so loved you that he sent His only begotten Son, Jesus Christ, to live, die and be raised again so that all who believe on Him have eternal life, that can never be taken away or lost, never earned or deserved, given in grace, then confess with thy mouth, call upon the name of the Lord and be saved.

Love worketh no ill to his neighbour: therefore love is the fulfilling of the law. And that, knowing the time, that now it is high time to awake out of sleep: for now is our salvation nearer than when we believed.*

**Romans 11**
*I say then, Hath God cast away his people? God forbid. For I also am an Israelite, of the seed of Abraham, of the tribe of Benjamin.*

*2 God hath not cast away his people which he foreknew. Wot ye not what the scripture saith of Elias? how he maketh intercession to God against Israel saying,*

*3 Lord, they have killed thy prophets, and digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life.*

*4 But what saith the answer of God unto him? I have reserved to myself seven thousand men, who have not bowed the knee to the image of Baal.*

*5 Even so then at this present time also there is a remnant according to the election of grace.*

*6 And if by grace, then is it no more of works: otherwise grace is no more grace. But if it be of works, then it is no more grace: otherwise work is no more work.*

*7 What then? Israel hath not obtained that which he seeketh for; but the election hath obtained it, and the rest were blinded.*

*8 (According as it is written, God hath given them the spirit of slumber, eyes that they should not see, and ears that they should not hear;) unto this day.*

*10 Let their eyes be darkened, that they may not see, and bow down their back alway.*

*12 Now if the fall of them be the riches of the world, and the diminishing of them the riches of the Gentiles; how much more their fulness?*

*13 For I speak to you Gentiles, inasmuch as I am the apostle of the Gentiles, I magnify mine office:*

*14 If by any means I may provoke to emulation them which are my flesh, and might save some of them.*

*15 For if the casting away of them be the reconciling of the world, what shall the receiving of them be, but life from the dead?*

*17 And if some of the branches be broken off, and thou, being a wild olive tree, wert grafted in among them, and with them partakest of the root and fatness of the olive tree;*

*18 Boast not against the branches. But if thou boast, thou bearest not the root, but the root thee.*

*19 Thou wilt say then, The branches were broken off, that I might be grafted in.*

*20 Well; because of unbelief they were broken off, and thou standest by faith. Be not highminded, but fear:*

*21 For if God spared not the natural branches, take heed lest he also spare not thee.*

*22 Behold therefore the goodness and severity of God: on them which fell, severity; but toward thee, goodness, if thou continue in his goodness: otherwise thou also shalt be cut off.*

*23 And they also, if they abide not still in unbelief, shall be grafted in: for God is able to graft them in again.*

*24 For if thou wert cut out of the olive tree which is wild by nature, and wert grafted contrary to nature into a good olive tree: how much more shall these, which be the natural branches, be grafted into their own olive tree?*

*25 For I would not, brethren, that ye should be ignorant of this mystery, lest ye should be wise in your own conceits; that blindness in part is happened to Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles be come in.*
