<!-- post 10887 · 2023-06-26 06:14 · study -->

And so, Paul having settled the matter regarding Israel after the flesh, that none are saved without faith in Christ, same as the Gentiles, dead under sin just as the Gentiles are, that any zeal they show towards God, without faith, is out of ignorance and going about to establish their own righteousness, all together vain; He continues stating his fervent desire that his brothers after the flesh repent, and be saved. 

This chapter is abused into teaching a false doctrine that all Jews, even the modern "Jews" (who say they are Jews but do lie), are still God's chosen people and thus still are going to be saved, despite being apostate and denying Christ Jesus. They will derisively dismiss the idea that non-believing Jews are cut off and cast out, ears dulled and hearts waxen, unable to believe, as "Replacement Theology." 

But the epistle to the Romans demonstrates with finality that all who don't believe are cut off, and that it is one continuation of faith that saves. The same for Jew and Greek. It isn't replacement it is continual, in the election of grace. 

Old Testament Israel and New Testament Israel. Hebrew of Gentiles, all were saved by faith. The offer to the Greek came after Christ was lifted up, but it is the same offer. The same gift of eternal life in Christ through faith. Being chosen to be God's people by our belief, not by seed. Wild olive branches grafted in to the root that is found in Israel, as unbelieving Jews are cut off and burned. 

Paul begins the chapter giving a rhetorical question to the conclusions he reached regarding apostate Jews in the previous chapters. *Hath God cast away his people? God forbid.* Why? Because he himself is of the Jews and a believer. So were most of the early churches. The remnant of those elect in grace, predestined in Christ (not as Calvanism teaches, but by our faith through Christ, not the other way around). If he, Paul is saved then not all of Israel is cast away. God hath not cast away his people who he foreknew. Again, those that are saved and in whom He works to make vessels fitted to His use. It is the same foreknowledge of the elect, those that would believe, not saying every Jew.

The prophet Elijah cursing Israel for their unbelief, saying "Lord, they have killed thy prophets, and digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life," is reminded by God that He has a remnant of believers still. As wicked and apostate as Israel got, there were still 7,000 who had not bowed the knee to Baal. Paul using this to say that even then, there were Jews who believed and got saved. That all of them were not cast out. 

*Even so then at this present time also there is a remnant according to the election of grace.*

The remnant is according to what? The election of grace. Which is as he already established, being saved by faith calling upon the name of the Lord. This is a conditional statement. Dispensationalism always ignores conditional statements regarding the Jews to make these things apply to all of them. 

And, as an aside he makes yet another statement of salvation by faith and not of works;
*And if by grace, then is it no more of works: otherwise grace is no more grace. But if it be of works, then it is no more grace: otherwise work is no more work*.

There is no grace if you worked for it. There is no working for it if it is of grace. 

What then? If only a remnant remains of those who believed, then Israel as a nation has not obtained righteousness (they seek to establish their own), but the election in grace, believers, have obtained righteousness by faith. 

And the rest were blinded. Which brings us back to chapter 1 and the reprobate doctrine. God chooses to blind those who will not believe, giving them up and giving them over. 

*According as it is written, God hath given them the spirit of slumber, eyes that they should not see, and ears that they should not hear*.

As Jesus said in **Matthew 13**, eyes closed, dull of hearing, hearts waxed gross, they cannot hear the word of God and believe.

<!-- post 10888 · 2023-06-26 06:14 · study -->

As David wrote, cursing Israel in **Psalm 69**; *Let their eyes be darkened, that they may not see, and bow down their back alway.*

(Note all the cursing of Israel in the prophets and Jesus' preaching. They were not afraid of being cursed for it. The "bless them that bless thee, curse them that curse thee" being fufiled in Jesus, not anyone calling themselves Jew) 

Paul hopes that, as God said repeatedly in the OT through the prophets, He will provoke them to jealousy by the salvation of the Gentiles. By giving the Kingdom to another people, they will repent. 

Paul says that if God is glorified in the salvation of the Gentiles by the destruction of the Jews, imagine how much more glory God would have if the Jews as a nation, would repent and believe?

<!-- post 10889 · 2023-06-26 07:06 · study -->

Before getting back into the chapter itself, I want to reiterate that whatever else they are, Modern Jews are not the sons of Judah from the Old Testament. 

Whether they are Edomites or Khazarians, or some other people, regardless of what Kaiser Sose origin story and Jewish fables, We know they are not the descendants of the 12 tribes of Israel. To the extent they may have some of Abraham's blood in them, So does most of the world. 

The one defining characteristic of all modern Jews, and by modern I mean going back to the first few centuries AD, or the foundation of Judaism 400 years after Christ, all Jews hold as their primary identity the rejection of Jesus. Be they Ashkenazi, Arabic, Turkish, Polish, Reformed, orthodox, atheist, conservative or liberal... Every single Jew is an antichrist. And God will make of them a synagogue of Satan. 

Reprobate silver will men call them. For they have been given up and rejected by God.

I make this caveat because as we know, dispensationalism is about modern Jews, not the 12 tribes of Israel which no longer exist and haven't existed since 70 AD. 

When they say that they think that God isn't finished with the Jews or that all the Jews will end up being saved at the second coming of Christ, they are talking about this reprobate group that the Bible says aren't actually Jews. They aren't even Israel after the flesh let alone spiritual Israel, which are all believers. 

And God has cursed them.

<!-- post 10890 · 2023-06-26 08:17 · study -->

Continuing the thought, Paul uses a parable, a picture of what has happened, that the natural root of salvation came through Israel, and though some of those branches were broken off and burned, the Gentiles were grafted in. All by grace through faith. 

And Paul warns the Gentiles not to think too highly of themselves, that it is by God's grace and to His purpose that this has happened. 

We aren't naturally supposed to be there, being wild olive trees, and the tree of the vineyard that God has grown with care is pruned of the branches that aren't producing fruit, and grafted us in that will produce in an abundance, but again the glory is His. 

*Boast not against the branches. But if thou boast, thou bearest not the root, but the root thee.* 

We shouldn't fall to the same arrogance as the unbelieving Jews. To think we are special boys. There is no Jew or Greek in Christ. Keep humility about our situation. We all deserve Hell and Death. We are there simply by the grace and the purpose of God.

*Thou wilt say then, The branches were broken off, that I might be grafted in.*
*Well; because of unbelief they were broken off, and thou standest by faith. Be not highminded, but fear: For if God spared not the natural branches, take heed lest he also spare not thee.*

Well there means , good. Correct. You are right. The Jews were cut off that we may be grafted in. So true. 

But be not high-minded, or proud, because of God did that to the nation he tended for thousands of years, He could easily do that to the Gentiles. The other nations.

<!-- post 10891 · 2023-06-26 08:34 · study -->

*Behold therefore the goodness and severity of God: on them which fell, severity; but toward thee, goodness, if thou continue in his goodness: otherwise thou also shalt be cut off. And they also, if they abide not still in unbelief, shall be grafted in: for God is able to graft them in again*.

Again, note the conditional statements. IF we as a people continue in belief, we will remain in His goodness, and IF the Jew in unbelief repents, they will be graft back in. It is entirely conditioned on faith. 

*For I would not, brethren, that ye should be ignorant of this mystery, lest ye should be wise in your own conceits; that blindness in part is happened to Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles be come in.*

So many miss the point of this statement, which is a warning to the nations not to assume they deserve the blessings of grace through faith, as the unregenerate Jew had done. They get caught up in the "fullness of the Gentiles come in." 

It is a curious turn of phrase that, because it points to a future event, many take and wrest with, trying to pinpoint what this means eschatologically. But it simply means when all those that are going to believe, have, the harvest comes. The day of the Lord, the rapture and the wrath. It's just a poetic way of saying this. Fullness being synonymous with the harvest. The fruit coming in. It isn't some esoteric hidden meaning. 

Paul says that blindness *in part* (there's a remnant that believed, remember) has happened until the harvest of the nations is ready. 

**Revelation 14:15**
*And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to him that sat on the cloud, Thrust in thy sickle, and reap: for the time is come for thee to reap; for the harvest of the earth is ripe.*

God likens all of this to a vineyard throughout scripture. The spring feasts and fall feasts point to the first and second coming, the sowing and the harvest. 

The fruits we produce are saved Christians we've led to Christ. The increase, the bounty of the harvest is God's. We are servants, husbandmen. He is the Lord of the harvest. And when the fullness of the Gentiles has come in, He will reap.

<!-- post 10892 · 2023-06-26 08:40 · study -->

And so, *As concerning the gospel, they are enemies for your sakes: but as touching the election, they are beloved for the father's sakes.*

God would that they believe and be saved. He would glory in the older being brought back into the Kingdom with the younger. But it can only happen through faith, and those who refuse to believe are cut off and made reprobate. 

*For as ye in times past have not believed God, yet have now obtained mercy through their unbelief: Even so have these also now not believed, that through your mercy they also may obtain mercy. For God hath concluded them all in unbelief, that he might have mercy upon all.*

It's all about faith. We are all in sin and grace is offered all of us. 

*O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and knowledge of God! how unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past finding out!*

Many find it unfair that some are cut off, they refuse to believe the Reprobate Doctrine, they doubt the judgment of God. They think He must have some secret plan to win them back. A plan B. But the only plan is Christ. And Him crucified. And it is not for us to question the judgment and mercy of God. He will have mercy on whom He has mercy. It's not up to us to tell Him, but to humbly submit to His will.

*For who hath known the mind of the Lord? or who hath been his counsellor? Or who hath first given to him, and it shall be recompensed unto him again? For of him, and through him, and to him, are all things: to whom be glory for ever. Amen.*

<!-- post 10893 · 2023-06-26 08:44 · study -->

And so we can see that the plain meaning of this section of Romans is the application, not the twisting and out of context bending of scripture that the Zionists have to use to deceive the unlearned. 

It's easily understandable if you just read exactly what it says in the order that it's given. Following along with the logical arguments offered. Comparing scripture with scripture. 

When we do that, rather than trusting in study guides and footnotes, we see the clear meaning. God is not the author of confusion. He is reasonable.

## Romans 11  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2023-10-26

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/12365

<!-- post 12365 · 2023-10-26 06:41 · scripture -->

**Romans 11**
*I say then, Hath God cast away his people? God forbid. For I also am an Israelite, of the seed of Abraham, of the tribe of Benjamin.*

*2 God hath not cast away his people which he foreknew. Wot ye not what the scripture saith of Elias? how he maketh intercession to God against Israel saying,*

*3 Lord, they have killed thy prophets, and digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life.*

*4 But what saith the answer of God unto him? I have reserved to myself seven thousand men, who have not bowed the knee to the image of Baal.*

*5 Even so then at this present time also there is a remnant according to the election of grace.*

*6 And if by grace, then is it no more of works: otherwise grace is no more grace. But if it be of works, then it is no more grace: otherwise work is no more work.*

*7 What then? Israel hath not obtained that which he seeketh for; but the election hath obtained it, and the rest were blinded.*

*8 (According as it is written, God hath given them the spirit of slumber, eyes that they should not see, and ears that they should not hear;) unto this day.*

*9 And David saith, Let their table be made a snare, and a trap, and a stumblingblock, and a recompence unto them:*

*10 Let their eyes be darkened, that they may not see, and bow down their back alway.*

*11 I say then, Have they stumbled that they should fall? God forbid: but rather through their fall salvation is come unto the Gentiles, for to provoke them to jealousy.*

*12 Now if the fall of them be the riches of the world, and the diminishing of them the riches of the Gentiles; how much more their fulness?*

*13 For I speak to you Gentiles, inasmuch as I am the apostle of the Gentiles, I magnify mine office:*

*14 If by any means I may provoke to emulation them which are my flesh, and might save some of them.*

*15 For if the casting away of them be the reconciling of the world, what shall the receiving of them be, but life from the dead?*

*16 For if the firstfruit be holy, the lump is also holy: and if the root be holy, so are the branches.*

*17 And if some of the branches be broken off, and thou, being a wild olive tree, wert grafted in among them, and with them partakest of the root and fatness of the olive tree;*

*18 Boast not against the branches. But if thou boast, thou bearest not the root, but the root thee.*

*19 Thou wilt say then, The branches were broken off, that I might be grafted in.*

*20 Well; because of unbelief they were broken off, and thou standest by faith. Be not highminded, but fear:*

*21 For if God spared not the natural branches, take heed lest he also spare not thee.*

*22 Behold therefore the goodness and severity of God: on them which fell, severity; but toward thee, goodness, if thou continue in his goodness: otherwise thou also shalt be cut off.*

*23 And they also, if they abide not still in unbelief, shall be grafted in: for God is able to graft them in again.*

*24 For if thou wert cut out of the olive tree which is wild by nature, and wert grafted contrary to nature into a good olive tree: how much more shall these, which be the natural branches, be grafted into their own olive tree?*

*25 For I would not, brethren, that ye should be ignorant of this mystery, lest ye should be wise in your own conceits; that blindness in part is happened to Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles be come in.*

*26 And so all Israel shall be saved: as it is written, There shall come out of Sion the Deliverer, and shall turn away ungodliness from Jacob:*

*27 For this is my covenant unto them, when I shall take away their sins.*

*28 As concerning the gospel, they are enemies for your sakes: but as touching the election, they are beloved for the father's sakes.*

*29 For the gifts and calling of God are without repentance.*

*30 For as ye in times past have not believed God, yet have now obtained mercy through their unbelief:*

*31 Even so have these also now not believed, that through your mercy they also may obtain mercy.*

*32 For God hath concluded them all in unbelief, that he might have mercy upon all.*

<!-- post 12366 · 2023-10-26 06:41 · scripture -->

*33 O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and knowledge of God! how unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past finding out!*

*34 For who hath known the mind of the Lord? or who hath been his counsellor?*

*35 Or who hath first given to him, and it shall be recompensed unto him again?*

*36 For of him, and through him, and to him, are all things: to whom be glory for ever. Amen.*

<!-- post 12367 · 2023-10-26 06:45 · study -->

**Romans 11**, the culmination of many of the arguments made throughout the epistle, definitively shows that unbelieving Jews have been cut off, to the benefit of the Gentiles, for the glory of God. 

And yet there are some who cherry pick certain isolated statements, out of context, to teach the opposite; that unbelieving Jews, trusting their works and lineage, and not of grace and faith, are still heirs. 

So let's break it down.

<!-- post 12368 · 2023-10-26 07:01 · study -->

Paul had just concluded that they are a disobedient and gainsaying people. God tried. 

As Christ put it in **Matthew 23:37**;

*O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, thou that killest the prophets, and stonest them which are sent unto thee, how often would I have gathered thy children together, even as a hen gathereth her chickens under her wings, and ye would not!*

So, what then? Are they all cut off? God forbid, for Paul is *an Israelite, of the seed of Abraham, of the tribe of Benjamin.* They cannot be all cut of simply by being Israel after the flesh, or Paul wouldn't be saved. 

*God has not cast away his people which he foreknew.* We have already seen that Paul has already written in **Romans 8** that those who believe, God foreknew. That same foreknowledge of believers. This is a conditional statement. It's not "God has not cast away His people," but His people that are saved. 

And to make this point clear, Paul quotes Elijah, who was cursing Israel for their unbelief and seeking the prophet's life. God's reply to Elijah is that there was a remnant of Israel who were saved by faith, who had no bent the knee to Baal. Who were still faithful. 

By comparing his present time to Elijah's, Paul deliberately specifies that there is a remnant, just as he himself is a remnant, according to the election of grace. 

That is, a chosen people by grace, believers. Even of the Jews at that present time, as he himself is proof of.

*And if by grace, then is it no more of works: otherwise grace is no more grace. But if it be of works, then it is no more grace: otherwise work is no more work.* 

With this aside, Paul lays the final nail in the coffin of the idea of works based salvation. The election of grace is to the glory of God, boasting excluded. Unmerited, but accessed by faith, as we have shown in earlier chapters.

<!-- post 12369 · 2023-10-26 07:16 · study -->

What then? 

Israel hath not obtained that which he seeketh for.

But the election hath obtained it. 

The rest were blinded.

Way back in **Romans 1** Paul firmly establishes that the Glory of God is revealed from heaven by the mercy He has shown on the believer, and His wrath on those who hold the truth in unrighteousness. That His abundant grace is shown by the forgiveness of sin, even to the Gentile, the same as with the Jew, and that forgiveness in grace reveals His glory from Heaven, so that none have any excuse. 

That mercy, through us calls others to believe. That wrath on the unbelieving, calls others to believe. All to His glory. 

Verse 11:11 relies on this established understanding. Have they stumbled **that** they should fall? No. God forbid. But rather they have stumbled that Gentiles be saved. Why? To provoke the unbelieving Jews to jealousy. 

Because it all serves to reveal the glory of God. It's not about us. It's not about the Jews. It is about God being glorified through His grace, revealed through His mercy and wrath.

<!-- post 12370 · 2023-10-26 07:29 · study -->

So consider this context as we read the next verse; 

*Now if the fall of them be the riches of the world, and the diminishing of them the riches of the Gentiles; how much more their fulness?*

Paul says he is speaking to the Gentiles here. Asking them a question as the apostle to them; if the world is made rich in Christ because the unregenerate Jews fell, can you imagine how much more the glory of God would be revealed by their coming to believe?

Recall that Paul's still talking about his own wish, that his brethren after the flesh be saved. He has already concluded that only a remnant are, and that blindness has come on the rest. That only by being part of the election of grace, given to believers, that they can be saved and made heirs in Christ. God is not a respecter of persons. There is no Jew or Greek in Christ. 

In that context, we can see Paul's wish for them expressed by the rhetorical question he asks the reader; if their fall was to bring salvation to the world, how much greater would it be if the salvation of the world brought them back? 

*If by any means I may provoke to emulation then which are of my flesh, and might save some of them.* 

This is the point of the jealousy God hopes to provoke in them. That some might be saved. 

If...may...might...some. 

Many dispensationalists will twist this series of conditions to mean "will" and "all."

<!-- post 12371 · 2023-10-26 07:55 · study -->

*For if the casting away of them be the reconciling of the world, what shall the receiving of them be, but life from the dead?*

This is the entire point of Paul's arguments about the Jews. They are cast off. They are blinded. They are reprobate, just as Jeremiah preached against Israel;

*Reprobate silver shall men call them, because the Lord hath rejected them.*

But wouldn't it be wonderful if the veil were lifted that they may come to believe on Christ? 

Now the dispensationalist will say that in the end, on the day of redemption, they will look up and see Jesus coming in the clouds and believe and be saved. But that is taught nowhere in scripture. 

Paul gives us the actual means by which *some may be saved*. Provoked to jealousy. Provoked to emulation. By what? The mercy and grace shown believers by God. By His glory revealed in the believing Gentiles. The people who were not a people. 

This theme runs throughout the epistle. There is no glory but God's. And this is how it is revealed. Mercy and wrath. 

It is not up to us to ask why some are fitted to mercy and others to wrath. 

This isn't God picking and choosing, but providing the means of salvation, and foreknowledge of who would avail themselves, all elect in Christ Jesus. All who are predestinated by faith in Him to be conformed to Him and His likeness. All who believe become elect and because God predestinated all who believe to be elect and adoption and promises and blessings and to be the seed of Abraham, the father of faith, whether Jew or Gentile. It's the process that was put in place, by the lamb slain before the foundation of the world. 

And so knowing this, and having shown the Gentiles of the sorry state of the fallen Jews, Paul warns against thinking Gentiles are some kind of special boys, falling victim to their own conceits the way the Jews have. 

*Well; because of unbelief they were broken off, and thou standest by faith. Be not highminded, but fear: For if God spared not the natural branches, take heed lest he also spare not thee.*

If generations of Christians began taking for granted the grace given to them, and assuming it is because they are better than the unregenerate Jews in terms of their own righteousness, they too could be cut off. Blinded just the same. 

Some take Paul's caution to the Gentiles to mean something entirely different, that the Jews are saved by some other means. But we have systematically proved by going through Romans that that isn't the case. 

Some will say the holy root are the Jews, and that while some branches are cut off and others grafted in, they still are the chosen of God by genetics. 

But the true vine is Jesus. It is entirely by grace. There is no difference. 

*If they abide not still in unbelief, shall be grafted in: for God is able to graft them in again.*

Simple as. 

All this is to encourage mercy for their enemies for the Gospels sake, that Christians can maybe convert some by believing on Christ. That some of the Judaizers might get saved. 

*For as ye in times past have not believed God, yet have now obtained mercy through their unbelief: Even so have these also now not believed, that through your mercy they also may obtain mercy. For God hath concluded them all in unbelief, that he might have mercy upon all.*

Israel was to be a light unto the unbelieving world. Now the believing world is to be a light to unbelieving Jews. All to God's glory. 

*O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and knowledge of God! how unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past finding out! For who hath known the mind of the Lord? or who hath been his counsellor?*
*Or who hath first given to him, and it shall be recompensed unto him again? For of him, and through him, and to him, are all things: to whom be glory for ever. Amen.*

<!-- post 12372 · 2023-10-26 08:07 · study -->

*For I would not, brethren, that ye should be ignorant of this mystery, lest ye should be wise in your own conceits; that blindness in part is happened to Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles be come in.* 

*And so all Israel shall be saved: as it is written, There shall come out of Sion the Deliverer, and shall turn away ungodliness from Jacob: For this is my covenant unto them, when I shall take away their sins.*

Here is the crux of the dispensationalist heresy. Their main proof text. "All Israel shall be saved."

But we know this is not Israel of the flesh. That not all Israel which are of Israel. 

*Not as though the word of God hath taken none effect. For they are not all Israel, which are of Israel:  Neither, because they are the seed of Abraham, are they all children: but, In Isaac shall thy seed be called. That is, They which are the children of the flesh, these are not the children of God: but the children of the promise are counted for the seed.*

And we see the conditional terms of how all Israel is saved; that a deliverer comes out of Zion, to turn away ungodliness in Jacob. Who will take away their sins. 

That is Christ Jesus. The promise is made in and through Him. And so, as it is written, all Israel shall be saved. 

The fullness of the Gentiles is the day of redemption. The second coming of Christ Jesus. The day of the Lord. Where the dead in Christ rise and the elect are gathered, and God's wrath is poured out in the unbelieving world. 

Paul is saying that partial blindness, that veil over their hearts, is going to remain in place until the end of the world. But all Israel, the sons of God adopted by faith, who are the true seed of Abraham, shall be saved.

<!-- post 12373 · 2023-10-26 08:17 · study -->

Anyone who tries to justify Dispensationalism to defend their Zionist fever dreams and heretical end times doctrine has to rely on making each argument in a vacuum. Stripping proof texts from their surrounding meaning. By ignoring contradictions and clear verses, to make doctrine from non sequiturs. 

But when we compare scripture to scripture, their doctrines fall apart. 

That is why they will make sweeping proclamations and assertions and arguments that are only backed by the stripped-away proof text, and not by the whole context of Scripture. 

It is why their false teachers will discourage reading the Bible yourself. Studying to be approved before God. They will over complicate it, make you doubt the word. Use false translations, study guides and charts. Tell you you have to "God back to the Greek!" or Hebrew. Imply that if you don't understand it the way they present it, you must not be saved. 

And inevitably, they will use Judaizing to bring a false gospel of works to reinforce their own wicked doctrines.

Dispensationalism has crept in to good, KJV, Bible believing churches with the right Gospel, no doubt. And many saved Christians are misled by it.

But if you look at how far along the spectrum of Dispensationalism they are, the more damnable heresy creeps in. 

A little leaven leavens the whole lump.

<!-- post 950 · 2022-05-05 05:42 · study -->

Here in Romans 11, Paul is finishing the JQ begun in chapter 9 and 10, as it pertains to Israel after the flesh, the nation, now that Christ had come. 

This builds on the theme he has from the start; that all men are without excuse in condemnation, and further some men and nations condemn themselves by bringing God to reject them utterly, unto reprobation. That even with the law, the Jews were condemned and without it, so were the Gentiles. That the Gospel of faith alone in grace alone of the resurrection, into His kingdom, was the promise that faith was placed in. Without works. *To him that worketh not.* That this election of grace applies to the Gentile as much as the Jew. That there is no difference between Jew and the Greek, that if all are condemned, then all are saved in Jesus if they believe. It's flawless doctrine, relentlessly building on itself.

Here in Romans 11 Paul is finishing the thought of what to say about the physical Israel, the Jews, given all this doctrine. What does the Gospel of Christ mean pertaining to the old covenant? (God is a logical God, who wants us to *reason together* with Him) 

Dispensationalism rests primarily on vs 1-5, and, like most bad doctrine, isolating verses out of context to achieve it. They say "see, God's not done with Israel!"

But here Paul specifically is offering himself as the example, that the remnant is those who believe the Gospel. For we know from the prior chapters that there is a conditional state to salvation, belief. Obeying the Gospel. And so any Jew who believes will be that remnant. Same as the Greek who are grafted in to the promise by their faith. 

Verse 6 has God hammer again the point that salvation is not by works. It is 100% by faith, least it no more be of grace. Because it is a gift and the rewards for work is a debt owed. (Works-nibbas BTHO, again)

So, having established that the Jews after the flesh have to believe, Paul circles back to Romans 1 and reprobate doctrine, calling in mind the imprecatory prayer of David to blind the wicked who reject the truth of God, unto damnation.

However, Paul prays that the Gentiles receiving the blessing and promises, being grafted in, provoked his kinsmen to repentance. That they turn from stubborn unbelief to accepting the Gospel. That they who are believe would be sent, preaching the word, and by hearing, physical Israel believe and be grafted in again. That by his own example living for Christ he gets his nation to want to emulate him and be saved. 

This plea is a familiar lament to many Christians. We all have unsaved relatives, often close. Friends, coworkers. People for whom we earnestly pray accept the free gift of salvation but who have hardened their hearts. 

Paul's example of being worthy of emulation is a model for us, that we live the Gospel in a way that draws them to the light and softens their hearts that they may be willing to believe.

The warning to Gentiles against becoming arrogant regarding their position relative to grace and the reprobate silver nation of Jews should also be taken to heart. 

A Godly nation can easily turn from the truth and be burned in the fire, just as easily as recalcitrant Israel could have a revival in Christ and be graft back in. Prodigals restored as heirs. 

Dispies falsely make this an unconditional statement rather than an argument; that Jews **will** be graft in regardless of their own belief or wickedness. 

Paul has built his case, in the Spirit, that everything here is contingent on believing God's law and promises, whom all men know, and knowing are condemned without excuse, except they believe in the gospel, which is of faith in Christ's crucifixion and resurrection for the remission of all sins, that this is a gift available to everyone, without works, entirely of grace, but that God doesn't hold it out to be taken indefinitely, and that at some point He will cut off the unbelieving, reject and turn them over to reprobation. Individual salvation to eternal life, or a physical nation on earth, it's the same.

<!-- post 951 · 2022-05-05 05:42 · study -->

Romans is one thesis. It's one argument. Not context-free series of non sequiturs and fortune cookie verses, that seemingly contradicts others when taken out of context.

<!-- post 952 · 2022-05-05 05:48 · study -->

(This applies to the entire Bible. It's ONE testimony, God's, and one theme, His love for us)

<!-- post 953 · 2022-05-05 05:59 · study · has photo -->

This is a powerful and sobering thought. Knowing your dad or mom, sister or brother, son or daughter, reject the Gospel and on the road to reprobation, or who are trapped in their zeal without knowledge, working their way to Hell by not obeying the Gospel. 

We know God is perfectly just. We know in His love we are saved, undeserved, entirely by grace, because we have believed the report. But do we live in a way that these people who we care about can emulate? Would they want to? What advantage do they see in accepting salvation, in how you live?

Are you fearful? Do you stress from the news of the world, the fake and gay theater the beast system presents to keep the cattle in pens for slaughter?

Do you live in gratitude and prayer to God in Jesus name? 

Do you read your Bible and go to church? Do you boldly proclaim the Gospel to every creature, giving a ready and meek answer to the lively hope you have in Christ?

<!-- post 954 · 2022-05-05 06:05 · study -->

This is our calling; spread the Good News with the joy that comes with knowing. 

Not trying to obtain sinless perfection, (though we should flee sin). Not self-flagellation for sins God's already forgiven in Christ. Not trying to score points with works, chasing "merit" and subscription based "grace." Not smugly sitting in our vain conceit of being "chosen," and so being holier than thou "fruit inspectors."

None of that brings the saving knowledge of the Gospel to anyone. It's selfish, self-righteous and if you're sincerely saved by faith, it's squandering the gift you've been given.

<!-- post 955 · 2022-05-05 06:10 · study -->

And I ask this not as a perfect example myself. I fail at much of this too often, but it's what we are called to do. As an offender I am chief.

## Romans 12  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-06-27

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/10896

<!-- post 10896 · 2023-06-27 05:29 · scripture -->

**Romans 12**
*I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, that ye present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable unto God, which is your reasonable service.*

*2 And be not conformed to this world: but be ye transformed by the renewing of your mind, that ye may prove what is that good, and acceptable, and perfect, will of God.*

*3 For I say, through the grace given unto me, to every man that is among you, not to think of himself more highly than he ought to think; but to think soberly, according as God hath dealt to every man the measure of faith.*

*4 For as we have many members in one body, and all members have not the same office:*

*5 So we, being many, are one body in Christ, and every one members one of another.*

*6 Having then gifts differing according to the grace that is given to us, whether prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of faith;*

*7 Or ministry, let us wait on our ministering: or he that teacheth, on teaching;*

*8 Or he that exhorteth, on exhortation: he that giveth, let him do it with simplicity; he that ruleth, with diligence; he that sheweth mercy, with cheerfulness.*

*9 Let love be without dissimulation. Abhor that which is evil; cleave to that which is good.*

*10 Be kindly affectioned one to another with brotherly love; in honour preferring one another;*

*11 Not slothful in business; fervent in spirit; serving the Lord;*

*12 Rejoicing in hope; patient in tribulation; continuing instant in prayer;*

*13 Distributing to the necessity of saints; given to hospitality.*

*14 Bless them which persecute you: bless, and curse not.*

*15 Rejoice with them that do rejoice, and weep with them that weep.*

*16 Be of the same mind one toward another. Mind not high things, but condescend to men of low estate. Be not wise in your own conceits.*

*17 Recompense to no man evil for evil. Provide things honest in the sight of all men.*

*18 If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men.*

*19 Dearly beloved, avenge not yourselves, but rather give place unto wrath: for it is written, Vengeance is mine; I will repay, saith the Lord.*

*20 Therefore if thine enemy hunger, feed him; if he thirst, give him drink: for in so doing thou shalt heap coals of fire on his head.*

*21 Be not overcome of evil, but overcome evil with good.*

<!-- post 10897 · 2023-06-27 05:53 · study -->

Having established all things, Paul now begins the practical application of the doctrines he has laid out. My favorite sermons are those that really tear into the meat of the word and then give practical application, how that doctrine can change your life and make you more able to work for God. 

Knowledge and wisdom. 

It isn't enough to know the word or have understanding of how it all connects, without taking the implications and applying it to our lives. How to be doers of the word and not hearers only. 

Paul, in the letter to the Romans, lays out some heavy doctrine in dense language, the revelation of God, the reprobate doctrine, salvation by grace through faith alone, to the Greek and Jew alike, the understanding of God's grace and the purpose of our salvation. And the understanding of a spiritual Israel, not of the flesh. 

But what then? 

Chapter 12, in clear and concise language tells us. 

That we present our bodies as a loving sacrifice, without blemish, which is our service, separated to the Gospel. Be holy that we may be better able to be of service to God. Vessels fitted for glory. Blameless. 

This isn't an appeal to hit the gym and not eat poorly. Our bodies are corruptible, and flesh is going to lead us to sin. It is about walking in the spirit that we may not fulfil the lusts of that flesh, and by example bring others to Christ. 

He writes, *be not conformed to this world: but be ye transformed by the renewing of your mind*, which comes from walking in the spirit. To know the will of God. Proving, testing to see what is acceptable. 

We are to remain humble, understanding that to every man of faith God has shaped to a purpose. Not all have the same gifts, the same talents, but all are members of the same body, which is Christ. None more important or less important. A similar theme Found in **1 Corinthians 12**:

*But now are they many members, yet but one body. And the eye cannot say unto the hand, I have no need of thee: nor again the head to the feet, I have no need of you.*

And so each of us should seek to use the talents we have to edify the whole of the body of believers. 

*Having then gifts differing according to the grace that is given to us, whether prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of faith; Or ministry, let us wait on our ministering: or he that teacheth, on teaching; Or he that exhorteth, on exhortation: he that giveth, let him do it with simplicity; he that ruleth, with diligence; he that sheweth mercy, with cheerfulness.*

So, to our measure, we all make perfect the will of God. And we see too, that attitude is everything.

<!-- post 10898 · 2023-06-27 06:05 · study -->

*Let love be without dissimulation. Abhor that which is evil; cleave to that which is good.*

Dissimulation is deceit, dishonesty regarding intent. Concealing motives. Love without dissimilation requires also to abhor, to hate, that which is evil. 

Speaking the truth in love. There is no love in manipulation or in hidden agendas. There is no love without hate.

These two tendencies of the world, to see all relationships as transactional, and so what men call love is often manipulative and controlling. Toxic. And with this toxic false love comes the lack of judgment, calling nothing evil. Tolerating all manner of corruption and perversion. "Not judging" but allowing others to do as they will and calling it good. 

It is codependency, an allowance for other's sin that we then can remain in our own. This isn't love. 

God's love is without manipulation. It is freely given. We don't have to work for it. Unconditional. To avail ourselves of it, we have to believe He so loved us He gave His only begotten Son. But His love came first, while we were yet sinners. 

And God hates evil. Many falsely say "God is love" and ignore His holy nature and the revelation of wrath against the wicked. His word telling us of abominations to Him. For example:

**Proverbs 6:16-19**
*These six things doth the Lord hate: yea, seven are an abomination unto him: A proud look, a lying tongue, and hands that shed innocent blood, An heart that deviseth wicked imaginations, feet that be swift in running to mischief, A false witness that speaketh lies, and he that soweth discord among brethren*.

The wicked slogan of the prideful sodomites, "Love is Love," is simply putting evil for good, bitter for sweet. 

It is impossible to love without hate.

<!-- post 10904 · 2023-06-28 05:24 · scripture -->

**Romans 12**

*11 Not slothful in business; fervent in spirit; serving the Lord;*

*12 Rejoicing in hope; patient in tribulation; continuing instant in prayer;*

*17 Recompense to no man evil for evil. Provide things honest in the sight of all men.*

*18 If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men.*

*19 Dearly beloved, avenge not yourselves, but rather give place unto wrath: for it is written, Vengeance is mine; I will repay, saith the Lord.*

<!-- post 10905 · 2023-06-28 05:49 · study -->

As we covered yesterday in the first half of chapter 12, faced with the truth of the Gospel and having repented towards God, being changed by the Spirit, sealed unto redemption, with eternity awaiting and goodwill with the Father through adoption as His sons, then we should live as His. To His purpose and glory, not frustrating the grace of God. 

Not for our benefit; we don't sanctify ourselves and walk in the Spirit to save ourselves from the wages of sin, as we are justified by Christ's righteousness without works of the law, but rather being freed from that fear and the yoke we were under as servants to death, we can become servants to life. 

We establish the law.

*Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind. This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like unto it, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself. On these two commandments hang all the law and the prophets.* 

And so, continuing to wrest the implications of salvation, Paul writes;

*Be kindly affectioned one to another with brotherly love; in honour preferring one another*. As Jesus asked us to love our brethren in Him. We should put the profit of our brothers and sisters in Christ above our own. With humility and meekness, being ministering servants. 

*Not slothful in business; fervent in spirit; serving the Lord*.

This is not only in our service to God for His works; winning souls, edifying the brethren, ministering to the afflicted, having hospitality and a generous spirit, but literally too, at work. A Christian is an exemplary employee when they see their work is for God, and a reflection of His work in us. 

**Colossians 3:22-25**
*Servants, obey in all things your masters according to the flesh; not with eyeservice, as menpleasers; but in singleness of heart, fearing God; And whatsoever ye do, do it heartily, as to the Lord, and not unto men; Knowing that of the Lord ye shall receive the reward of the inheritance: for ye serve the Lord Christ. But he that doeth wrong shall receive for the wrong which he hath done: and there is no respect of persons.*

The Bible doesn't say we should serve our bosses if they are saved. If they are Godly. It makes no distinction, and would apply even as slaves. We work, we don't rob our employers, because we work for God. 

Note the similarity with what John the Baptist preached to the newly converted to Christ in **Luke 3**:

 *And the people asked him, saying, What shall we do then? He answereth and saith unto them, He that hath two coats, let him impart to him that hath none; and he that hath meat, let him do likewise. Then came also publicans to be baptized, and said unto him, Master, what shall we do? And he said unto them, Exact no more than that which is appointed you. And the soldiers likewise demanded of him, saying, And what shall we do? And he said unto them, Do violence to no man, neither accuse any falsely; and be content with your wages.*

It's the same message. 

If we love God and we understand eternity in the spirit, then this short time in the flesh is His. And if we love our neighbor, we should seek their profit above our own. 

And He will repay. **Matthew 6:19-21**

*Lay not up for yourselves treasures upon earth, where moth and rust doth corrupt, and where thieves break through and steal: But lay up for yourselves treasures in heaven, where neither moth nor rust doth corrupt, and where thieves do not break through nor steal: For where your treasure is, there will your heart be also.*

Get the picture here, if we are humble. Meek. Knowing we are saved. Knowing the love of God and the peace in Christ, act like it. 

Not in pride, dishonesty. Not taking more than we are due. Our work reflecting the Spirit of God. That we may, by example be part of that revelation from Heaven of His mercy, that His light be magnified in us, drawing others to Him.

<!-- post 10906 · 2023-06-28 05:58 · study -->

*Rejoicing in hope; patient in tribulation; continuing instant in prayer.*

Be cheerful. Not a doomer. You have the hope of Christ, act like it. Not fearful, but let that joy come off of you. It is contagious. 

Be patient in tribulation. This applies obviously to the persecution for our faith, in Christ. But it is more than that. Try not to let the petty frustrations of this world get to you, lead you to wrath, being a bad report to the Gospel we live by. 

Continuing in prayer, always. Talk to God throughout the day. Give Him thanks and praise. While we shouldn't make a show of prayer, as is the habit of some, we shouldn't be ashamed to ask God's blessings for others and praise for when He answers or blesses. 

Think of the Philippian Jailer, who was saved by Paul and Silas, and the church planted there that did great things for the Lord. It all came about because, despite being in chains and captive, they sang hymns and Psalms and cheerfully held themselves out as content even in tribulation. 

**Philippians 2:1-5**
*If there be therefore any consolation in Christ, if any comfort of love, if any fellowship of the Spirit, if any bowels and mercies, Fulfil ye my joy, that ye be likeminded, having the same love, being of one accord, of one mind. Let nothing be done through strife or vainglory; but in lowliness of mind let each esteem other better than themselves. Look not every man on his own things, but every man also on the things of others. Let this mind be in you, which was also in Christ Jesus*

<!-- post 10907 · 2023-06-28 06:07 · study -->

*Distributing to the necessity of saints; given to hospitality. Bless them which persecute you: bless, and curse not. Rejoice with them that do rejoice, and weep with them that weep.*

We see the simplicity of this love, repeated throughout the New Testament. Be generous. Charitable. Merciful. Hospitable. 

Bless them that curse you. Turn the other cheek. Trust in God to repay, for vengeance is His. As Jesus says in **Matthew 5**:

*And if any man will sue thee at the law, and take away thy coat, let him have thy cloke also. And whosoever shall compel thee to go a mile, go with him twain. Give to him that asketh thee, and from him that would borrow of thee turn not thou away.*

Don't let your pride, covetousness, wrath and offense at the inequities of the ungodly be a snare for your carnal mind. God has you. And if you suffer loss for Him, He will repay with rewards greater than you can imagine, and crush our enemies for His sake under His heel. This is the promise of the Gospel, as much as our redemption and eternal life. Every knee shall bow. We will rule and reign with Christ, and He has a rod of iron to correct the ungodly. 

Better we practice our faith, our religion that is, with this in mind. 

**James 1:27**
*Pure religion and undefiled before God and the Father is this, To visit the fatherless and widows in their affliction, and to keep himself unspotted from the world.*

As I pointed out during our study of Luke, Jesus gives us a mission statement to live by:

*The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me to preach the gospel to the poor; he hath sent me to heal the brokenhearted, to preach deliverance to the captives, and recovering of sight to the blind, to set at liberty them that are bruised, To preach the acceptable year of the Lord.*

Amen!

<!-- post 10908 · 2023-06-28 06:20 · study -->

*Be of the same mind one toward another. Mind not high things, but condescend to men of low estate. Be not wise in your own conceits.*

This admonition, to be of the same mind, is repeated in the epistles, that the churches which receive them understand that we all share one goal. Serve one Gospel. Look forward with certainty, the same hope. 

It is why one of the 7 things God hates is a sower of discord among the brethren. It is why church discipline exists. But a church that is of one mind can do great things for the Lord.

But more so, see that the same caution to be of one mind, not at variance and strife, is coupled with the warning to Mind not high things, to not put ourselves as better than others, but rather condensed to the lowly. To not be wise in our own conceits. 

Don't be a special boy.

If you have wisdom, if you have knowledge, it came from God and isn't yours. Be humble. If facing someone who isn't as far in their Christian walk as you, be patient and longsuffering and offer guidance, because that is why God gave you that knowledge. Don't be high-minded. Don't get caught up in theologies and intellectual pursuit, thinking you're better than the unlearned. 

Special boys, your Calvinists, reform types, trad Caths, etc, full of vanity and the wisdom of the world, hold themselves up as better than others, full of hypocrisy. 

But we shouldn't be. Rather knowing that we serve God and love others, seek their profit, not our own vainglory.

<!-- post 10909 · 2023-06-28 06:24 · study -->

This is also speaking of not being a respecter of persons in a material sense. Understand that the more lowly and sinful and broken a person is, there's a strong likelihood that they will be receptive to the Gospel. Don't think better of yourself because someone is poor, uneducated, simple, not of your class or status. They aren't beneath you. Be their servants, ministering the Gospel in love. 

Jesus Christ who is the holy Son of God, was made flesh, subject to the corruption of the world, made sin for our sakes, suffering the humiliation of the cross, pain and death, the fires of Hell, all for our benefit, while we were yet sinners.

Keep that in mind.

<!-- post 10910 · 2023-06-28 06:45 · study -->

*Recompense to no man evil for evil. Provide things honest in the sight of all men. If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men.*

You can see the theme building. And it reflects everything that Jesus taught in his earthly ministry. It is the serious implication for our faith. Love one another. Love your neighbor as yourself. It's the whole of the law. 

And you'll note also, as this list of commands given us goes on, The harder and harder it is for the carnal mind to accept. That is, when you walk in the spirit you understand these things and you're less likely to be brought into a snare of wrath or greed or dishonesty and corruption, manipulating others. 

Because you grow in faith in the certainty of your hope. Because you see God working in your life and all around you. His work revealed to us from the heavens in His wrath and in His love. 

We live faith to faith. Not just in that we bring others to belief, but that our faith grows. As it is written, the just shall live by faith. 

You're hospitable and generous to others because you know God gave you what you have and He'll provide again. 

Your patient and long-suffering with others because you know He was patient in long suffering with you. When you didn't deserve it. 


You are just in your judgments and fair and honest in your dealings because God was just in His judgment and merciful to you, in truth. 

And you don't seek vengeance because you know that that belongs to God. And you know that He is not slack when it comes to delivering just punishment on the wicked. Just as Sodom and Gomorrah are held up for an example. 

And so as much as possible, an important caveat that will cover more extensively in chapter 13, but as much as possible, we live peaceably with all men. 

This is the implication of our faith, and the understanding of our hope, and a meek and humble acknowledgment of our condition. To better be servants of God.

<!-- post 10911 · 2023-06-28 07:17 · study -->

*Dearly beloved, avenge not yourselves, but rather give place unto wrath: for it is written, Vengeance is mine; I will repay, saith the Lord.* 

*Therefore if thine enemy hunger, feed him; if he thirst, give him drink: for in so doing thou shalt heap coals of fire on his head. Be not overcome of evil, but overcome evil with good.*

And so just finishing up the chapter. We see that this doesn't just apply to brethren, It doesn't just apply to our neighbors, but it really does apply to our enemies as well. But not in the sense our detractors claim; the weakness that they perceive from this. 

But in truth. Its strength. 

Again, I'm going to cover this more tomorrow God willing. But we see that in our faith there is understanding of the nature of God. That He will repay. And in our well-doing, in our charity to those who persecute us, curse us, and who do evil to us, we heap hot coals upon their head. 

It enrages the truly wicked when we show, by our faith, kindness towards them in need. When we turn the other cheek. 

For those that aren't necessarily wicked but worldly, this provides an opportunity for them to see the love of God through us and wins many of them over. 9 times out of 10 when someone offends you and you turn the other cheek and offer it to them, which isn't an act of submission but of defiance, you will see that most people back down. Where they may have struck an anger, literally or metaphorically, they will be made to feel ashamed. It humbles them and often brings about peace. Making an enemy into a friend. 

But knowing the wicked, that it frustrates them being that they are implacable and unmerciful and full of all unrighteousness, we can take comfort in the fact that by our defiant and cheerful service to the gospel, by our faith in the Lord to repay with vengeance that is His, they are made more miserable. And God given more glory. 

They will be punished in this world and the next. While we will have an eternity with God and His love.

<!-- post 997 · 2022-05-06 05:40 · study -->

Having reached the culmination of his instruction to the saints in Rome on the doctrines implicit in the faith, Paul begins to close out his epistle to them with exhortation for practical living in that faith. What having that blessed assurance of forgiveness and eternal life then means. 

We are called to live blameless. Our bodies kept pure and acceptable. Not abused by our lusts, marred by our sins. Presentable in our service which is preaching the gospel. 

Being not caught up in the trends and immorality of this world, but renewing our mind with His word to prove all things by scripture and the spirit, to discern what's true. What's God's will. We don't have to guess or search. It's in the Bible. 

Paul then explains understanding your own individual calling, and pursuing it for God. 

That we are to love without dissimulation; pretense. Not hiding what we are or couching it in subtlety. Openly loving as a reflection of God's love to us, shining on others. And to love means hating evil and clinging to good, not just some feel good selfish emotional rationalizing and enabling. 
 
And in that love, live in the faith, being a good brother in Christ, example in the world, trusting God for Him to take vengence. To love your enemies. To overcome evil with good. 

This is setting up the next chapter, 13, and one of the doctrines most of "our guys" struggle with and we will look at tomorrow.

<!-- post 10964 · 2023-06-30 06:30 · scripture -->

**Romans 13**
*Let every soul be subject unto the higher powers. For there is no power but of God: the powers that be are ordained of God.*

*2 Whosoever therefore resisteth the power, resisteth the ordinance of God: and they that resist shall receive to themselves damnation.*

*3 For rulers are not a terror to good works, but to the evil. Wilt thou then not be afraid of the power? do that which is good, and thou shalt have praise of the same:*

*4 For he is the minister of God to thee for good. But if thou do that which is evil, be afraid; for he beareth not the sword in vain: for he is the minister of God, a revenger to execute wrath upon him that doeth evil.*

*5 Wherefore ye must needs be subject, not only for wrath, but also for conscience sake.*

*6 For for this cause pay ye tribute also: for they are God's ministers, attending continually upon this very thing.*

*7 Render therefore to all their dues: tribute to whom tribute is due; custom to whom custom; fear to whom fear; honour to whom honour.*

<!-- post 10965 · 2023-06-30 06:46 · study -->

Throughout the epistle to the Romans, we see Paul in the Spirit, building stone upon stone the doctrines of theology upon the foundation of Christ. The implications of our faith and the religion of following it. 

That God gave His only begotten Son Christ Jesus, that by faith we are saved from the grip of death and the wrath of Hell. Every man. Jew and Gentile. Great and lowly. God is not a respecter of persons. 

We are in the flesh, and sinful, even once we are saved, spirit quickened, and sealed by His Spirit, unto the day of redemption. But that being our hope, we know that in that day we will be raised, corruptible to incorruptible, into everlasting life. 

And so, being freed from the burden of sin and death, we then should live like it. As much as possible live peaceably with all men. Loving our neighbors. Loving our enemies. Seeking the profit of our brethren. Serving our masters in our work as if we are working for God himself, because we are. 

Showing mercy, as mercy has been shown us. Longsuffering as God has with us. Obedient, as Christ was obedient to the cross, even for our sakes. Even as we were enemies of God, that we may, in Him not only have peace with the Father, but become heirs to the Kingdom. Adopted brothers of Christ. That we may rule and reign with Him in the Millennial Kingdom. *Don't you know we shall judge angels?* 

And if heirs to a kingdom, princes. And if princes, then we are servants ministering to those we have rule over. 

And our ministry is determined by our willingness to sacrifice and serve here on Earth. We are given talents of silver by the master while He is gone. And some of us will seek its increase, and use the talents given us to bring forth more, that the Lord at His return can say *Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things*.

<!-- post 10966 · 2023-06-30 06:49 · study -->

**Galatians 3:21-29/4:1-7** 
*Is the law then against the promises of God? God forbid: for if there had been a law given which could have given life, verily righteousness should have been by the law. But the scripture hath concluded all under sin, that the promise by faith of Jesus Christ might be given to them that believe. But before faith came, we were kept under the law, shut up unto the faith which should afterwards be revealed.*

*Wherefore the law was our schoolmaster to bring us unto Christ, that we might be justified by faith. But after that faith is come, we are no longer under a schoolmaster. For ye are all the children of God by faith in Christ Jesus. For as many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. There is neither Jew nor Greek, there is neither bond nor free, there is neither male nor female: for ye are all one in Christ Jesus.*

*And if ye be Christ's, then are ye Abraham's seed, and heirs according to the promise. Now I say, That the heir, as long as he is a child, differeth nothing from a servant, though he be lord of all; But is under tutors and governors until the time appointed of the father. Even so we, when we were children, were in bondage under the elements of the world: But when the fulness of the time was come, God sent forth his Son, made of a woman, made under the law, To redeem them that were under the law, that we might receive the adoption of sons. And because ye are sons, God hath sent forth the Spirit of his Son into your hearts, crying, Abba, Father. Wherefore thou art no more a servant, but a son; and if a son, then an heir of God through Christ.*

<!-- post 10967 · 2023-06-30 07:10 · study -->

And so, knowing we are sons, heirs, princes to a Kingdom, a better country which we seek, seek it diligently. And if under tutors and governors, schoolmasters until the day appointed, then we are to learn to better serve when appointed in that day.

And we learn patience. We learn right judgment. We learn the law and the will of God that we may better serve those entrusted to us in the Kingdom, by serving those we are given in this world. 

We serve God. With our whole heart, soul and mind, we are to love Him. And if we love Him, obey His commandments. 

As children, we are given to obey our parents. Commanded so, but with promise that we will live long upon the earth if we do. Honoring them, not just with obedience but in caring for them when they are old. Supporting them in their end years. Paying honor. 

God doesn't say "if the parent is good." He doesn't say if they are believers, it is then saved or unsaved.

We are told to love our neighbors as ourselves. The whole of the law and commandments is fufiled in this. How? In service. With hospitality. Grace and charity. Putting their profit above our own. We learn to minister that we may rule justly. We are to be holy because He is holy. And how can he trust us with much when we squandered the talents we were given?

How can we rule others, when we are rebellious and wicked?

We become husbands, and expect wives to submit, as they should, but men are to submit to God, and love their wife as Christ loved us, who did so unto death. 

When men don't submit themselves to God, when they don't love their wives as if they were their own flesh (which they are, joined together by God), then wives no longer submit. 

Wives are to submit to the husband, and when they don't, their children see and learn to rebel. They no longer honor their parents. 

When a nation has rebellious and seditious men, unfaithful women, unruly children, then they are punished by God. 

And God uses the ministers of government to hand out His punishment, that the nation either repents, or is destroyed. 

And while the unregenerate people that make up the majority of the nation may wax worse and worse, we who are a light unto the world, are told to obey the higher powers. To seek to find ways to please even wicked kings, but always keeping the word of God as our final authority. And by doing so we may be punished. We may be persecuted. We may be thrown into a firey furnace. Or a lion's den. 

We may be like the son of Israel, Joseph, sold into captivity and then thrown in prison for being faithful when tempted by the Pharoh's wife. But these things are done so that the glory of God may be revealed, and more are drawn to the truth. And to the Lord goes the increase. 

As the prophets were slain. As the Apostles were martyred. As Christ Himself died that the glory of God could be revealed. 

And so we obey God. We love our wives. Our wives submitting, teach our children. Our children in honoring us learn to fear God. Fearing God and in faith they do great things. And if the evil spirits that rule this world, the principalities that we wrest with, not of flesh and blood, but spiritual, if they seek our lives for it, then rejoice. It will be to the glory of God, and our rewards will be greater than we can convince of.

<!-- post 10968 · 2023-06-30 07:25 · study -->

"But I don't like it. I don't like being told what to do. I am obviously better, morally, intellectually, in every way, than my parents, than my boss, than my rulers." 

The same spirit of rebellion that Satan losed into the world. 

God created earthly hierarchies for His purpose. To shape the clay as the potter sees fit. To make vessels fitted for destruction and fitted for honor. Who is the clay to question it?

Now if your father tells you to lie, or steal. Or to dishonor God, worship idols, to kill. If he tells you to disobey a higher authority, we have no obligation to. 

Like Daniel, we do have an obligation to show them an alternative way. God's way. 

*There hath no temptation taken you but such as is common to man: but God is faithful, who will not suffer you to be tempted above that ye are able; but will with the temptation also make a way to escape, that ye may be able to bear it.*

And so, with every authority. There's never an occasion to partake in another man's sin. There is always a way out, even if that way is to sacrifice yourself that another may not sin, or that those who witness understand the right path, and may be converted by your conversation; which is in the Bible, our behavior. 

*Likewise, ye wives, be in subjection to your own husbands; that, if any obey not the word, they also may without the word be won by the conversation of the wives; While they behold your chaste conversation coupled with fear*.

Paul and Silas, in Philippi, offended the Jews and were brought before magistrates, given stripes being scourged, and thrown into prison for the Gospel. 

*And at midnight Paul and Silas prayed, and sang praises unto God: and the prisoners heard them.*

And, in their chaste conversation, in their behavior that showed their faith, when an earthquake came and tare down the walls of the prison, they remained, obedient to the charge. 

And the Philippian Jailer, scared not only for his life had they escaped, but afraid of the God they held their faith in, brought them out, and said, *Sirs, what must I do to be saved?*

*And they said, Believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, and thou shalt be saved, and thy house. And they spake unto him the word of the Lord, and to all that were in his house. And he took them the same hour of the night, and washed their stripes; and was baptized, he and all his, straightway.*

They even remained in prison that the magistrates who had ordered them released in private, would have to come to release them publicly, to the magistrates' shame. 

And the church of the Philippians was born and grew, and did mighty things for God, because of the obedience of Paul and Silas. To God and to government, even the government that unjustly persecuted them.

<!-- post 10969 · 2023-06-30 07:37 · study -->

And this is a pattern we see, God using the persecution of believers, even of Christ, to give the increase. God uses the weak things of this world to confound the strong. And in doing so, they are often converted. 

Nebuchadnezzar believed, and repented of the evil He would do. Kings came around to free all Judah from captivity. 

Publicans, centurions, even some Pharisees, all men with power and authority came to believe, and in doing so were given the same spirit we are. To be good ministers, to rightly judge. 

Paul, despite his being able to be released when arrested in Jerusalem, rather went to Rome in chains that he may minister the Gospel to the house of Caesar. 

This world isn't ours and the things of it are at best wood, hay and stubble, but the treasure we seek is of Heaven and incorruptible. Things of eternal value. 

And when we look at the eternal, this mortal life is given perspective. And rendering to Caesar what is Caesar's is a trifling thing. Paying custom and tribute; taxes, is nothing. God provided us what we have and will again. Even from the mouth of a fish. 

And if we are defrauded, Jesus says to not only let it go, but offer more. They take your coat, give them your cloak also. If they force you to walk a mile, go two. If they strike you, give them your other cheek that they may see what they are doing for what it is, and make the decision in your defiance to either strike again, or in shame back down. And perhaps by your chaste conversation be converted. By your stripes they may be healed.

<!-- post 10970 · 2023-06-30 07:55 · study -->

And so, knowing all this, what then? What further hope have we than eternity? Laying up treasure in Heaven? Giving glory to God in our tribulation that more may benefit? What else could we possibly need? 

God. In His grace and love promises us one more thing; Vengeance. He will repay. And all who have wronged Him, who have persecuted His sons and daughters, will repay. For He is a consuming fire. 

We aren't simply "Christ-cucks" acting as punching bags. We aren't floor mats to be trampled on. We have the Spirit inside us, we have blessing upon blessing given us. We see God move in our lives in mighty ways, when we walk after the spirit, and we have felt the reproach of God, who scourges every son, when we walk after the flesh. 

The natural man receiveth not the things of God for they are spiritually discerned. 

They can't understand this. The worldly men are full of fear and without hope because they don't believe, and not having faith, they cannot see.

**1 Corinthians 1:25-29**
*Because the foolishness of God is wiser than men; and the weakness of God is stronger than men. For ye see your calling, brethren, how that not many wise men after the flesh, not many mighty, not many noble, are called: But God hath chosen the foolish things of the world to confound the wise; and God hath chosen the weak things of the world to confound the things which are mighty; And base things of the world, and things which are despised, hath God chosen, yea, and things which are not, to bring to nought things that are: That no flesh should glory in his presence.*

Don't be ashamed of the Gospel, for it is the power of God unto salvation and in it we will never be ashamed. God will always deliver.

<!-- post 10971 · 2023-06-30 08:14 · study -->

Damnation in this sense for the saved is physical, but in the broader sense it is a sin to revolt against things that aren't contradicted by the higher power, the highest being God.

**Romans 13**

*Let every soul be subject unto the higher powers. For there is no power but of God: the powers that be are ordained of God.*

*2 Whosoever therefore resisteth the power, resisteth the ordinance of God: and they that resist shall receive to themselves damnation.*

*3 For rulers are not a terror to good works, but to the evil. Wilt thou then not be afraid of the power? do that which is good, and thou shalt have praise of the same:*

*4 For he is the minister of God to thee for good. But if thou do that which is evil, be afraid; for he beareth not the sword in vain: for he is the minister of God, a revenger to execute wrath upon him that doeth evil.*

*5 Wherefore ye must needs be subject, not only for wrath, but also for conscience sake.*

*6 For for this cause pay ye tribute also: for they are God's ministers, attending continually upon this very thing.*

*7 Render therefore to all their dues: tribute to whom tribute is due; custom to whom custom; fear to whom fear; honour to whom honour.*

*8 Owe no man any thing, but to love one another: for he that loveth another hath fulfilled the law.*

*9 For this, Thou shalt not commit adultery, Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not steal, Thou shalt not bear false witness, Thou shalt not covet; and if there be any other commandment, it is briefly comprehended in this saying, namely, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself.*

*10 Love worketh no ill to his neighbour: therefore love is the fulfilling of the law.*

*11 And that, knowing the time, that now it is high time to awake out of sleep: for now is our salvation nearer than when we believed.*

*12 The night is far spent, the day is at hand: let us therefore cast off the works of darkness, and let us put on the armour of light.*

*13 Let us walk honestly, as in the day; not in rioting and drunkenness, not in chambering and wantonness, not in strife and envying.*

*14 But put ye on the Lord Jesus Christ, and make not provision for the flesh, to fulfil the lusts thereof.*

<!-- post 1103 · 2022-05-08 05:25 · study -->

Romans 13 continues the theme of how saved Christians are to behave. It begins telling us God's view of earthly authority. Parents, bosses, civic leaders, law enforcement, all authorities under God, wicked and cruel or righteous and kind, to serve His will. 

Of course we are to follow the higher law of God when earthly authority contradicts it. We are to obey God rather than man, but *if at all possible to live peaceably with all men*. 

Many of us bristle at this. It requires our subjection and faith that God will protect and deliver us. Paying custom and tribute to people we know are wicked, stupid, foolish, deceitful is a hard thing to swallow. Not going into reactionary spirals. Not acting from fear. Honoring God by honoring those he's placed in authority over you. Starting with parents for children, husbands for wives, bosses for husbands. 

When authority places you in conflict with God, meekly and in His name refuse, but attempt to offer a solution, even one that may harm your interests short term, that you may render what is acceptable to God and in doing so be rewarded long term.

This is so hard in our current year. But if it's fake and gay, can't be voted out, can't be fed posted, and God is with you anyways, what more can we do but live in Godly subjection? At worse be persecuted for His sake and be rewarded here and for eternity. At best be an example as to how God delivers His sons and daughters from tribulation so that others may believe, and in doing so change the nature of the people, turning them towards God. This is how the Bible teaches us we change who rules over us to a more benevolent or tolerable authority. It's how we win. 

Hard lesson in faith.

<!-- post 1104 · 2022-05-08 05:45 · study -->

It's why the beast gets us to put our faith in the process, our faith in the parties, in our vote, hope in men. It's why they keep us afraid and reacting. It's why they pit two evils against each other and tell us to select the lesser and in so doing become complicit in the resulting wickedness, even if "our candidate" loses. 

Or we have faith in God, be not afraid, and stand in His righteousness alone.

<!-- post 1108 · 2022-05-08 06:07 · study -->

Not participating in wickedness, even at a financial loss or loss of "freedoms" while maintaining faith in God to see you though, results in the wickedness passing you by and those pursuing it being undone, every time. 

It's often our choosing sides on fake and gay matters (things beyond first principles), that creates the conditions of our persecution. Using our wanting to maintain or achieve preferences against us is one of Satans oldest tricks.

<!-- post 1112 · 2022-05-08 06:31 · study -->

And owe no man anything, except the love that is fulfilment of the law. Pay your bills. Live within your means. Don't become indebted according to your own covetousness, be it financially or in obligation.

Far too many agree to terms of obligation in exchange for nothing but affirmation of bias and enabling support for sin. It's how grifters make merchandise of you. It's a mental and spiritual usury.

*Him that is weak in the faith receive ye, but not to doubtful disputations.*

*15 But if thy brother be grieved with thy meat, now walkest thou not charitably. Destroy not him with thy meat, for whom Christ died.*

*16 Let not then your good be evil spoken of:*

*18 For he that in these things serveth Christ is acceptable to God, and approved of men.*
