<!-- post 10989 · 2023-07-02 07:13 · study -->

**Ephesians 4:14-16**
*That we henceforth be no more children, tossed to and fro, and carried about with every wind of doctrine, by the sleight of men, and cunning craftiness, whereby they lie in wait to deceive; But speaking the truth in love, may grow up into him in all things, which is the head, even Christ: From whom the whole body fitly joined together and compacted by that which every joint supplieth, according to the effectual working in the measure of every part, maketh increase of the body unto the edifying of itself in love.*

<!-- post 10990 · 2023-07-02 07:54 · study -->

Romans chapter 14 is Paul giving particular application to these things regarding the consequences of our faith, turning it inwardly onto brothers in Christ; our role in edifying one another rather than debating and striving in doubtful disputations about things not clearly taught in scripture. 

Something we see ourselves in any church or fellowship with other churches or individual Christians. 

The Bible says we are to be a *whole body fitly joined together*, repeatedly stating we should be of one mind regarding the Gospel and the ministry of it. That if there are disputes between us, or trespasses against us, that we work it out within the congregation. Even unto church discipline. But in all things we are to strive together for the Gospel, and because of this, and loving one another, we shouldn't judge one another over things the Bible isn't firm on. 

Understand that the Bible is the final authority on all matters of faith and practice. All scripture is given by God that we may be throughly furnished to all good works. Perfect. Complete. Especially with the catch-all, love each other as you love yourself. Putting others needs before your own. And the profit of others before yours. As much as possible live peaceably with all men. 

And so, chapter 14 begins with a hypothetical situation to illustrate the point Paul is building to. 

*Him that is weak in the faith receive ye, but not to doubtful disputations.*

This caveat  is important to understand what he is proposing; A person weak in the faith may hold themselves to a higher standard than the Bible calls for, that we being more stable in the faith may see as silly. 

Right up front Paul is warning not to use the occasion to argue with them. Especially of they are so weak of faith, or babes, that either may dig in their heels at rebuke, and add to the Gospel their opinions. Doubtful disputations, arguments about unimportant things that ultimately rest on "if you disagree you aren't saved." 

Get the picture here. We can assume these are both saved Christians who simply disagree on a matter of separation and sanctification that aren't spelled out, explicitly or implicitly in scripture. Paul uses eating of meat vs vegetarians, but He also applies it to celebration of holidays, even the first day of the week as sacred. 

Other than the strong admonishment against eating meat sacrificed to idols in 1 Corinthians for example, even if you don't think the idol is anything but wood or molten metal, is clear. And the giving to us that all food is clean and good to eat, that is blessed in Christ by our thanksgiving, there is nothing that says we have to eat meat. Just as there's nothing against abstaining. 

But one weak in the faith and consumed by guilt at eating an animal, or who sees holidays as unnecessary or even wrong in their conscience, we should be patient with. We shouldn't push them to do what's wrong in their mind, to stumble. Even if you think they're fools. 

To love them, we shouldn't place stumbling blocks but rather edify them. Our job isn't to judge one another who believe and are not in open sin regarding clear teachings, over petty matters. 

An example may be something like *The woman shall not wear that which pertaineth unto a man*, and applying it to all things, even specifically women's attire that has a shared element to what the male version is. Tee-shirts. Sweatshirts. Hoodies. Taking it beyond not crossdressing and wearing modest apparel as scripture teaches, but extending it to a higher level. 

Just because they have a higher standard than the Bible calls for doesn't mean you should mock it or ridicule it, and by God if it is in your church and it is their standards and it isn't being placed as a work of righteousness, than not only remain quiet about it, but hold to it. Better to dress in the manner that is greater than the Bible calls for than to sow discord among the brethren and bring others to sin.

<!-- post 10991 · 2023-07-02 07:54 · study -->

And by the same token, those weaker in the faith should learn not to judge another's walk, if it is keeping with the spirit of the law, but maybe not to your liking. 

We are to build each other up. 

Stand fast for what's true, in love. If the Bible says it and they are against it. Correct them. 

If the Bible gives us liberty to determine for ourselves what's right (again outside clear teachings) then don't worry about others who don't see it yet. 

If you celebrate Christmas or Thanksgiving, and do it to Christ, giving thanks to God, and someone else doesn't, it shouldn't bother you. Or vice versa. 

We will give account to God. Don't judge His servants by your own terms. If some brothers see it as unclean and you don't, both being fully persuaded, let the matter settle, but the onus is on both to not give the other occasion to stumble at their own conscience.

<!-- post 10992 · 2023-07-02 08:09 · study -->

This can be very hard for many. Especially those who, despite being saved and understanding their righteousness is as filthy rags, take their sanctification in all things very seriously. They see some, in their mind, abuse the liberty we have been given in Christ. Those who do not strive as they strive. Who don't go to church as much as they do. Who don't read the Bible sufficiently enough for them. Who go soul winning sporadically, if at all when you're out there grinding. 

Those people need your encouragement. Not your scorn, least we think better of ourselves than we ought. Becoming hypocrites. Edify them and show them the blessings of the work that they may emulate you. 

People who take matters of this life and this world too seriously, who worry about money. Who worry about politics. Who, being weak in the faith feel like we have to be active in supporting candidates to keep "evil from gaining" or something. Who are prepping a bunker and are terrified of persecution, stocking up on ammo and freeze dried food. These to me are people weak in the faith, and I try not to judge. I have been there myself. 

And while it is important to be ready for things that may happen, and aware of what's going on, to be afraid and give in to the troubles of the world is wrong. But many weak in the faith do it and believe they are serving God when they do. We should receive them, but not to doubtful disputations. Not in strife. I am using examples of how to apply this, and if they aren't perfect don't judge me. 

*But speaking the truth in love, may grow up into him in all things...*

Try saying something like "I'm not judging you, but I can show you from the Bible what the word says." But don't argue. Save admonishment for actual clear doctrines, not your opinions, and not to lord over someone who isn't as far along in their Christian walk as you. (Or who. Being stronger in the faith, acting in that faith but to you, seems wrong)

**Romans 14** 

*Him that is weak in the faith receive ye, but not to doubtful disputations.*

*2 For one believeth that he may eat all things: another, who is weak, eateth herbs.*

*3 Let not him that eateth despise him that eateth not; and let not him which eateth not judge him that eateth: for God hath received him.*

*4 Who art thou that judgest another man's servant? to his own master he standeth or falleth. Yea, he shall be holden up: for God is able to make him stand.*

*5 One man esteemeth one day above another: another esteemeth every day alike. Let every man be fully persuaded in his own mind.*

*6 He that regardeth the day, regardeth it unto the Lord; and he that regardeth not the day, to the Lord he doth not regard it. He that eateth, eateth to the Lord, for he giveth God thanks; and he that eateth not, to the Lord he eateth not, and giveth God thanks.*

*7 For none of us liveth to himself, and no man dieth to himself.*

*8 For whether we live, we live unto the Lord; and whether we die, we die unto the Lord: whether we live therefore, or die, we are the Lord's.*

*9 For to this end Christ both died, and rose, and revived, that he might be Lord both of the dead and living.*

*10 But why dost thou judge thy brother? or why dost thou set at nought thy brother? for we shall all stand before the judgment seat of Christ.*

*11 For it is written, As I live, saith the Lord, every knee shall bow to me, and every tongue shall confess to God.*

*12 So then every one of us shall give account of himself to God.*

*13 Let us not therefore judge one another any more: but judge this rather, that no man put a stumblingblock or an occasion to fall in his brother's way.*

*14 I know, and am persuaded by the Lord Jesus, that there is nothing unclean of itself: but to him that esteemeth any thing to be unclean, to him it is unclean.*

*15 But if thy brother be grieved with thy meat, now walkest thou not charitably. Destroy not him with thy meat, for whom Christ died.*

*16 Let not then your good be evil spoken of:*

*17 For the kingdom of God is not meat and drink; but righteousness, and peace, and joy in the Holy Ghost.*

*18 For he that in these things serveth Christ is acceptable to God, and approved of men.*

*19 Let us therefore follow after the things which make for peace, and things wherewith one may edify another.*

*20 For meat destroy not the work of God. All things indeed are pure; but it is evil for that man who eateth with offence.*

*21 It is good neither to eat flesh, nor to drink wine, nor any thing whereby thy brother stumbleth, or is offended, or is made weak.*

*22 Hast thou faith? have it to thyself before God. Happy is he that condemneth not himself in that thing which he alloweth.*

*23 And he that doubteth is damned if he eat, because he eateth not of faith: for whatsoever is not of faith is sin.*

<!-- post 1151 · 2022-05-09 05:39 · study -->

Romans 14 is often confused by the unsaved with works salvation, but it's addressing those with faith, brothers in Christ, weak in the faith they may be, who add as sin things not inherently sinful, to their own judgement in service to God.

It's also twisted by some who take the liberty found in Christ as a license to sin, saying "The Spirit hasn't convicted me on that" as they engage in wickedness clearly commanded against in the Bible.

What Paul is speaking to is holier than thou judgments of those with higher standards than even God's, or the mocking of those who see it as vain and foolish, knowing the liberty we have, obeying commandments but not adding things like not eating meat. 

Both serve God and are acting in their conscience, and to get a brother to stumble in his belief is to occasion them to sin, even if the thing itself objectively isn't sinful. 

Basically, not being a fruit inspector and minding your own business when someone holds themselves to a higher standard to the glory of God and their service to Him, even if you know it's a little silly.

<!-- post 1152 · 2022-05-09 05:46 · study -->

Again, there are conditional statements and orders of operations here that tell us the specific context of what's being preached. As always, scripture references itself to provide the full picture. That's why we study the whole Bible, comparing spiritual with spiritual, not just taking a verse out of context, stripped of the conditionals and order, removed from supporting scripture. 

You can't form doctrine around a verse or two and your assumptions and own reasonings, to fit doctrine you've decided on. You have to let God's word tell you what it means directly. God is not an author of confusion. The Bible defines itself and gives clear unambiguous statements for doctrine.

<!-- post 1154 · 2022-05-09 05:54 · study -->

Dietary laws amended to the sanctification process of the believer often end in the place where "Pharisees were offended" having made those carnal ordinances into works of righteousness themselves.

Jesus declares the vanity of these carnal ordinances and dietary laws. Paul reminds that in Jesus all things are good for meat (meat means food to eat in the Bible, not just flesh of animals). But the greater good is loving your brothers and not tempting them to betray what they see as separation and sanctification. To not offend their conscience to please yours. To not be spiteful to those weaker in the faith. To not give them occasion to stumble in their walk.

*18 For I will not dare to speak of any of those things which Christ hath not wrought by me, to make the Gentiles obedient, by word and deed,*

*23 But now having no more place in these parts, and having a great desire these many years to come unto you;*

*26 For it hath pleased them of Macedonia and Achaia to make a certain contribution for the poor saints which are at Jerusalem.*

*32 That I may come unto you with joy by the will of God, and may with you be refreshed.*

<!-- post 1187 · 2022-05-10 05:38 · study -->

We are called to show mercy and long-suffering to the weak. To the unsaved neighbor. And to support brothers in the Spirit where we can to meet both spiritual and carnal need. This can be difficult in a world of Judeo-sodomite culture with capitalist values. But we have to be gracious to those in need. Not enabling. Not patronizing. Not seeking our own glory or righteousness, signalling virtue. Just doing what's needed in God. 

Paul wraps up the scriptural case for replacement theology, that the Gentiles are adopted to the promise to the Israelites, fulfilled in Jesus Christ. That there is no dispensation other than faith in Christ. It's iron clad and the work of Schofield and Darby to undermine should be clear to those saved reading in the spirit. 

And yet... Zogged out evangelicals are blinded to this truth, and so, blinded to much more. Jesus *will* come as a thief in the night to them, because they can't see the signs of the times, and it won't be a secret rapture to spare them tribulation, but to their condemnation. 

Paul wraps up this portion detailing his plans, and as we see in Acts, God had others for him and he was to see Rome sooner than perhaps he suspected.

<!-- post 1188 · 2022-05-10 06:55 · study -->

Being like-minded with God in showing patience and consolation, especially to those weaker than ourselves in the faith, in general standards, in sin, intelligence, physically.... It's a struggle. 

With those who become a burden, incapable of helping themselves, entitled and unwilling, who rebuff reproach, it becomes so difficult. But in it there's a lesson for those of us willing to see, how much worse it must be for God dealing with us. 

Even the most spiritually minded of us try God's patience. Even those who stay in God's word daily in constant prayer, not forsaking the assembly, and winning souls to Christ, (there are very few this upright, and it certainly isn't me), try God's patience.

<!-- post 1189 · 2022-05-10 07:03 · study -->

I think, for me at least, this is one of the hardest things about living a Godly Christian life. We talk about how the more spiritual you become the closer you draw yourself to God the closer He draws to you, and the greater the gifts He bestows on you in reward for your faith. We received salvation, then we received blessings upon blessings, we received knowledge and wisdom, answers to prayer. But *unto whomsoever much is given, of him shall be much required.* 

The stronger you grow in your faith the larger the burden you're expected to tow. But even then, His yoke is light in comparison to the glory we are given in grace.

<!-- post 1190 · 2022-05-10 07:10 · study -->

The turning from sin and giving up worldly pleasures and habits is actually the easiest part. With the spirit and willingness, we naturally become repulsed by the weakness of the old man of the flesh. Free of guilt and resentment, and with discernment that comes with devotion to the spiritual, it's almost nothing to go cold turkey from our carnal life.

Living for God, and loving others as ourselves, magnifying the patience and love He has shown us, that's the challenge. That's the cross I take up daily. Having faith that this will be to the glory and benefit of God, even if it costs us today, that's hard. 

Paul's exhortation to do this, (and the listing of the saints who have, immortalized in Romans 16), is a sobering reminder of our shared calling in Christ.

<!-- post 1191 · 2022-05-10 07:31 · study -->

A lot of people make their faith in vain, because they intellectually understand the need for salvation, but don't believe that God's gift of eternal life is eternal. Everlasting. That the perfect work of Christ is enough. They place their faith in themselves, their works, either front loading - requiring works before salvation, or worse, back loading it - trusting in their works to maintain it. 

Grace-mixers, who add their own righteousness to Jesus' deny the power of the Gospel.

<!-- post 1193 · 2022-05-10 07:48 · study -->

I could do this all day. Verses used to teach the damnable doctrine of lost salvation make God a liar, make those deceived by it debtors to the whole of the law, and put their faith in their own works, and thus fall out of grace having never been saved.

<!-- post 1195 · 2022-05-10 08:10 · study -->

Someone in the ApeMountain chat was asking about the verses in Revelation 2 and 3 where Jesus tells John about His issues with the 7 various independent churches. The question was basically aren't those verses where Jesus tells the churches to repent or He will "remove thy candlestick" saying they are going to lose their salvation? 

The answer is a resounding no. As the Bible makes clear in **Revelation 1:12-20**, the candlesticks represent the churches themselves and their fellowship and blessing with Jesus:

*12 And I turned to see the voice that spake with me. And being turned, I saw seven golden candlesticks;*

*13 And in the midst of the seven candlesticks one like unto the Son of man, clothed with a garment down to the foot, and girt about the paps with a golden girdle.*

*14 His head and his hairs were white like wool, as white as snow; and his eyes were as a flame of fire;*

*15 And his feet like unto fine brass, as if they burned in a furnace; and his voice as the sound of many waters.*

*16 And he had in his right hand seven stars: and out of his mouth went a sharp twoedged sword: and his countenance was as the sun shineth in his strength.*

*17 And when I saw him, I fell at his feet as dead. And he laid his right hand upon me, saying unto me, Fear not; I am the first and the last:*

*18 I am he that liveth, and was dead; and, behold, I am alive for evermore, Amen; and have the keys of hell and of death.*

*19 Write the things which thou hast seen, and the things which are, and the things which shall be hereafter;*

*20 The mystery of the seven stars which thou sawest in my right hand, and the seven golden candlesticks. The seven stars are the angels of the seven churches: and the seven candlesticks which thou sawest are the seven churches.*


There's a myth that the dark sayings in Revelation are mysterious, but frequently when John seems confused or unsure Jesus or an angel will say "here's what this means." Obviously some remains through a glass darkly, but much of it is explained. 

When Christ says "*Repent, and do the first works; or else I will come unto thee quickly, and will remove thy candlestick,"* He is literally speaking of their authority, hedge of protection, etc, as His church in fellowship with Him. He doesn't threaten those saints with damnation, because that would contradict the Gospel given that led them to being God's servants. 

Jesus repeatedly says something like "He that hath an ear, let him hear what the Spirit saith unto the churches" in His letters to these churches. Far too many do not have an ear to hear.

<!-- post 1196 · 2022-05-10 08:11 · study -->

BTW the false doctrine of "Church ages" that claim there are seven church ages that correspond with how each is described in Revelation, and claiming that we now live in a Laodicean era of lukewarm churches, is foolishness. 

There are always churches in all ages that have these traits, that's the point of the verses.

*7 Wherefore receive ye one another, as Christ also received us to the glory of God.*

*8 Now I say that Jesus Christ was a minister of the circumcision for the truth of God, to confirm the promises made unto the fathers:*

*9 And that the Gentiles might glorify God for his mercy; as it is written, For this cause I will confess to thee among the Gentiles, and sing unto thy name.*

*10 And again he saith, Rejoice, ye Gentiles, with his people.*

*11 And again, Praise the Lord, all ye Gentiles; and laud him, all ye people.*

*12 And again, Esaias saith, There shall be a root of Jesse, and he that shall rise to reign over the Gentiles; in him shall the Gentiles trust.*

*13 Now the God of hope fill you with all joy and peace in believing, that ye may abound in hope, through the power of the Holy Ghost.*

*14 And I myself also am persuaded of you, my brethren, that ye also are full of goodness, filled with all knowledge, able also to admonish one another.*

*15 Nevertheless, brethren, I have written the more boldly unto you in some sort, as putting you in mind, because of the grace that is given to me of God,*

*16 That I should be the minister of Jesus Christ to the Gentiles, ministering the gospel of God, that the offering up of the Gentiles might be acceptable, being sanctified by the Holy Ghost.*

*17 I have therefore whereof I may glory through Jesus Christ in those things which pertain to God.*

*18 For I will not dare to speak of any of those things which Christ hath not wrought by me, to make the Gentiles obedient, by word and deed,*

*19 Through mighty signs and wonders, by the power of the Spirit of God; so that from Jerusalem, and round about unto Illyricum, I have fully preached the gospel of Christ.*

*20 Yea, so have I strived to preach the gospel, not where Christ was named, lest I should build upon another man's foundation:*

*21 But as it is written, To whom he was not spoken of, they shall see: and they that have not heard shall understand.*

*22 For which cause also I have been much hindered from coming to you.*

*23 But now having no more place in these parts, and having a great desire these many years to come unto you;*

*24 Whensoever I take my journey into Spain, I will come to you: for I trust to see you in my journey, and to be brought on my way thitherward by you, if first I be somewhat filled with your company.*

*25 But now I go unto Jerusalem to minister unto the saints.*

*26 For it hath pleased them of Macedonia and Achaia to make a certain contribution for the poor saints which are at Jerusalem.*

*27 It hath pleased them verily; and their debtors they are. For if the Gentiles have been made partakers of their spiritual things, their duty is also to minister unto them in carnal things.*

*28 When therefore I have performed this, and have sealed to them this fruit, I will come by you into Spain.*

*29 And I am sure that, when I come unto you, I shall come in the fulness of the blessing of the gospel of Christ.*

*30 Now I beseech you, brethren, for the Lord Jesus Christ's sake, and for the love of the Spirit, that ye strive together with me in your prayers to God for me;*

*31 That I may be delivered from them that do not believe in Judaea; and that my service which I have for Jerusalem may be accepted of the saints;*

*32 That I may come unto you with joy by the will of God, and may with you be refreshed.*

*33 Now the God of peace be with you all. Amen.*

<!-- post 11004 · 2023-07-03 05:46 · study -->

Romans 15 caps off the doctrines of Christ that Paul has laid out for us, the truth of God and His word, finally shared throughout the world to every nation. 

Carrying over the admonishment of chapter 14, to those stronger in the faith and with better understanding of scripture to be mindful of those weaker than us, babes in Christ. Those easily led to sin by their own devices. To not judge, but support. 

*We then that are strong ought to bear the infirmities of the weak, and not please ourselves.* 

This is spiritual. The true religion. We put too much emphasis on our own comfort and satisfaction and not the needs of others. When Paul writes to please our neighbors, it isn't as manpleasers, not being weak and being obsequious. But being meek, and leading them for their good and edification. Building people up. 

This is a hard concept for many. A healthy person can put aside their own wants and desires to make another stronger, and in doing so, shine the light of God that they may come to believe. To ask you the reason for your hope. And so whatever small things you do for them, do it with cheer and charity. Not enabling or partaking in sin, but by example as we are given by the Spirit. Just as Christ by example led us to His truth. He took the pains of our sin upon Himself, loved us, shows compassion for the afflicted, but never once backs down to say what's right. Preaching the truth in love, with all long-suffering.

<!-- post 11005 · 2023-07-03 05:53 · study -->

*For whatsoever things were written aforetime were written for our learning, that we through patience and comfort of the scriptures might have hope.*

This is another proof text for scripture being the means by which we learn the doctrines of God. God's word is powerful and where our hope comes from, the object of our faith. The truth of God's word. 

Paul's prayer is that the patience and consolation of the Gospel, taught in scriptures, the understanding of the implications of our shared faith and common salvation, would bring the churches in Rome, and the thousands of churches that have read Romans, to be likeminded regarding these things, that they may in one accord glorify God. That they may receive each other and the brethren in peace and patience and love, the way Jesus received us to the glory of God. To reveal the righteousness of God to the nations through our salvation.

<!-- post 11006 · 2023-07-03 06:01 · study -->

Continuing, Paul reinforces his ministry to the Gentiles, the nations of the world outside of Israel. That scripture has declared that they shall come under God's mercy and so he preaches the Lord Jesus Christ throughout the world. 

*But as it is written, To whom he was not spoken of, they shall see: and they that have not heard shall understand.*

And this blessing has resounded throughout the centuries that we may come to the same faith, the same foundation of Christ, and be justified by the grace of God. Revealed from heaven by those that come before us. Faith to faith.

<!-- post 11007 · 2023-07-03 06:16 · study -->

As Paul begins to wrap up the letter with his intentions to visit the churches in Rome, which he will eventually if not in the manner he thinks as he wrote this, he delves into some of the plans he has made that will delay his journey. 

One of those things is the bringing of alms to the church at Jerusalem, collected from the churches throughout Asia Minor and Greece to benefit the believers there suffering through a great famine, as we were shown in **Acts 11**:

*And in these days came prophets from Jerusalem unto Antioch. And there stood up one of them named Agabus, and signified by the Spirit that there should be great dearth throughout all the world: which came to pass in the days of Claudius Caesar. Then the disciples, every man according to his ability, determined to send relief unto the brethren which dwelt in Judaea*.

This is discussed in other epistles, and is very specific to the time and place and need. It is about saved brethren in the Jerusalem of that time, in the famine that plagued the land as it drew nearer and nearer to its destruction. Relief from the other churches of believers, that the brethren of saved believing Jews and others in that church may ride out the hardship to the glory of God. 

Many enemies of the Gospel take the following verses from **Romans 15** and twist them into something else;

*But now I go unto Jerusalem to minister unto the saints. For it hath pleased them of Macedonia and Achaia to make a certain contribution for the poor saints which are at Jerusalem. It hath pleased them verily; and their debtors they are. For if the Gentiles have been made partakers of their spiritual things, their duty is also to minister unto them in carnal things.*

Unbelieving Jews twist this to guilt Christians into supporting all Jews. All the time. Begging for money in the most obscene ways, and tricking those weak in the faith into thinking they are mandated to fund "Israel" forever. 

But we have been made partakers of their spiritual things only in Christ. The need was temporary. For believers. Brethren caught in the midst of God's wrath upon the rest of the Jews. 

Others cite this passage in the same manner as the unrepentant Jews, claiming we are cucked by our faith, and required to serve Jews. This isn't the case and it is always interesting to me how much in common Jews and pagan Gentiles have in regards to their abuse of scripture to tell us what we believe.

<!-- post 11008 · 2023-07-03 06:19 · study -->

*Pray...That I may be delivered from them that do not believe in Judaea*... Just to make that last point even clearer. This isn't a command to financially support unbelieving Jews. 

(But it was those Jews who brought Paul before magistrates, and ultimately to Rome, and so in a way the prayers were answered)

## Romans 16  ·  Primary study  ·  2023-07-04

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/11016

<!-- post 11016 · 2023-07-04 07:02 · scripture -->

**Romans 16**
 *I commend unto you Phebe our sister, which is a servant of the church which is at Cenchrea:*

*2 That ye receive her in the Lord, as becometh saints, and that ye assist her in whatsoever business she hath need of you: for she hath been a succourer of many, and of myself also.*

*3 Greet Priscilla and Aquila my helpers in Christ Jesus:*

*4 Who have for my life laid down their own necks: unto whom not only I give thanks, but also all the churches of the Gentiles.*

*5 Likewise greet the church that is in their house. Salute my well-beloved Epaenetus, who is the firstfruits of Achaia unto Christ.*

*6 Greet Mary, who bestowed much labour on us.*

*7 Salute Andronicus and Junia, my kinsmen, and my fellow-prisoners, who are of note among the apostles, who also were in Christ before me.*

*8 Greet Amplias my beloved in the Lord.*

*9 Salute Urbane, our helper in Christ, and Stachys my beloved.*

*10 Salute Apelles approved in Christ. Salute them which are of Aristobulus' household.*

*11 Salute Herodion my kinsman. Greet them that be of the household of Narcissus, which are in the Lord.*

*12 Salute Tryphena and Tryphosa, who labour in the Lord. Salute the beloved Persis, which laboured much in the Lord.*

*13 Salute Rufus chosen in the Lord, and his mother and mine.*

*14 Salute Asyncritus, Phlegon, Hermas, Patrobas, Hermes, and the brethren which are with them.*

*15 Salute Philologus, and Julia, Nereus, and his sister, and Olympas, and all the saints which are with them.*

*16 Salute one another with an holy kiss. The churches of Christ salute you.*

*17 Now I beseech you, brethren, mark them which cause divisions and offences contrary to the doctrine which ye have learned; and avoid them.*

*18 For they that are such serve not our Lord Jesus Christ, but their own belly; and by good words and fair speeches deceive the hearts of the simple.*

*19 For your obedience is come abroad unto all men. I am glad therefore on your behalf: but yet I would have you wise unto that which is good, and simple concerning evil.*

*20 And the God of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly. The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you. Amen.*

*21 Timotheus my workfellow, and Lucius, and Jason, and Sosipater, my kinsmen, salute you.*

*22 I Tertius, who wrote this epistle, salute you in the Lord.*

*23 Gaius mine host, and of the whole church, saluteth you. Erastus the chamberlain of the city saluteth you, and Quartus a brother.*

*24 The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you all. Amen.*

*25 Now to him that is of power to stablish you according to my gospel, and the preaching of Jesus Christ, according to the revelation of the mystery, which was kept secret since the world began,*

*26 But now is made manifest, and by the scriptures of the prophets, according to the commandment of the everlasting God, made known to all nations for the obedience of faith:*

*27 To God only wise, be glory through Jesus Christ for ever. Amen.*

<!-- post 11017 · 2023-07-04 07:23 · study -->

The eternal salute of these saints in Christ, who Paul calls fellow laborers in the preserved word of God is a mighty honor. Their qualities of faith and work towards the ministry of the Gospel are an example we should strive for. 

Paul, having called out those he sought to honor to the churches in Rome, can't help but add more doctrine to what has been a master class of Christian theology. 

Mark them which cause divisions. Who are teaching contrary to the doctrines they have learned. We have learned the same, being blessed by scripture. Then this command is for us as well. We should mark them and avoid them. 

*For they that are such serve not our Lord Jesus Christ, but their own belly; and by good words and fair speeches deceive the hearts of the simple.*

A major theme to the epistles is warning against false prophets and those bringing in damnable heresy, and what is consistently noted is their tendency to be in it for filthy lucre sake. As Jesus said in **Matthew 23**; 

*Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye devour widows' houses, and for a pretence make long prayer: therefore ye shall receive the greater damnation.*


Or Paul writing in **2 Timothy 3**;

*For of this sort are they which creep into houses, and lead captive silly women laden with sins, led away with divers lusts, Ever learning, and never able to come to the knowledge of the truth.*

False teachers are vipers. Reprobates. Dogs. Swine. **Never** able to come to the knowledge of the truth. Starting with warnings about reprobates, Paul finishes on the same topic from a different angle, and understanding that God has marked the reprobate in two distinct and clear ways that man may know who they are; if they are sodomites or false teachers (often both) who usurp the authority of the Gospel to promote damnable heresy and lead others astray, they are all together corrupt. 

This isn't someone who is wrong on some secondary or tertiary doctrine. Or someone who disagrees with you about some verse meaning or whatever. It isn't an unsaved person who doesn't understand the Gospel. 

This is the curse of those who turn the truth of God into a lie. Who hold the truth in unrighteousness. Who knowingly subvert and undermine churches for their own pleasure, recognition or wealth, passing themselves off as men of God. 

**Jude 10-13**
*But these speak evil of those things which they know not: but what they know naturally, as brute beasts, in those things they corrupt themselves. Woe unto them! for they have gone in the way of Cain, and ran greedily after the error of Balaam for reward, and perished in the gainsaying of Core. These are spots in your feasts of charity, when they feast with you, feeding themselves without fear: clouds they are without water, carried about of winds; trees whose fruit withereth, without fruit, twice dead, plucked up by the roots; Raging waves of the sea, foaming out their own shame; wandering stars, to whom is reserved the blackness of darkness for ever.*

Some people think that many who preach the Reprobate Doctrine spend too much time on it, or consider it wrong to make assumptions about people's status as a saved Christian, or not. And generally speaking, we should give a professing brother or sister the benefit of the doubt. Carefully and with love correcting them from scripture if they stray. If it is a minor thing, like in Romans 14, we should ignore it and humor them in it that we not lead them to sin. 

Given that context, and the understanding that, despite that understanding, the holy Spirit has inspired verse after verse, chapter after chapter, from multiple writers, over and over again in the New Testament, giving these warnings and how to recognize them. It is clearly for our edification. Those that reject it do so at their peril.

<!-- post 11018 · 2023-07-04 07:24 · study -->

*For your obedience is come abroad unto all men. I am glad therefore on your behalf: but yet I would have you wise unto that which is good, and simple concerning evil. And the God of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly. The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you. Amen*

<!-- post 11019 · 2023-07-04 07:36 · study -->

*Stand fast therefore in the liberty wherewith Christ hath made us free, and be not entangled again with the yoke of bondage*.

This 4th of July, Independence Day, remember that the blessings of liberty come from the liberty found in Christ. There is no liberty outside of Christ. 

It isn't an abstraction that can be grasped and measured. It isn't a concept to be understood and maintained in a secular setting. It has no meaning without the Gospel, the beautiful law of Liberty. 

*But whoso looketh into the perfect law of liberty, and continueth therein, he being not a forgetful hearer, but a doer of the work, this man shall be blessed in his deed.*

America secured the blessings of liberty through the faith of the founding stock. Not their masters. Not their king, not in the men that replaced him. Not in their documents. It isn't to be found in the Constitution. No parchment has or can grant liberty, or the blessings that come from it. Only salvation by grace through faith in Christ Jesus can. 

And only God may bless those who maintain the faith and live it out, regardless of where or when. 

And those blessings that abound to the faithful can continue and grow to the benefit of everyone in their nation, regardless of their faith. But the only way to secure those blessings is in the Lord;

*Now the Lord is that Spirit: and where the Spirit of the Lord is, there is liberty.*

<!-- post 1238 · 2022-05-11 05:50 · study -->

Last chapter in the letter to the church at Rome from Paul. It's basically a post script, but there's doctrine in all scripture and definitely here as well. 

Note that Paul first praises a woman, Phebe, commending her for her great work laboring for God, spreading the Gospel and being a strength to others in the faith. She is one of many women God immortalized in scripture for their labors.

Many assume because Paul reiterates God's command that women remain silent in church, and not be pastors (a position many men couldn't qualify for either), that Paul was a misogynist or something. But a saved woman is called to give the gospel and save souls and use their gifts to the edification of the church, just as a man is. That there are roles defined for us by God doesn't make us greater or lesser in His service. *There is neither Jew nor Greek, there is neither bond nor free, there is neither male nor female: for ye are all one in Christ Jesus.* 

Both men and women lose sight of this in current year, and resentments and divisions surrounding this keep us from fulfilling the labors God has for us. 

Paul goes on to salute and praise men and women of the faith who are his fellow laborors. Helpers. Being about the business of the Father. Winning souls and edifying the saints. We should strive to be so worthy as to be known in Heaven for the work we do for the Lord. That the *churches* of Christ salute us. 

Yes, churches is plural. That's the NT Christian perspective on a church. Local, independent, Jesus-led, pastor-run, gospel-based. A fellowship of like-minded churches. Not a universal church on earth. 

We are to avoid people coming in with false Gospels, extra-biblical doctrines and traditions. Verse 18 is a condensed version of the warnings given by Paul elsewhere, Peter and Jude against letting these types creep in unawares to make merchandise of the believers, and feed their bellies in the communal suppers. Taking advantage of the hospitality, and deceiving even believers with false teachings. 

We are to be wise about what's good, simple or that is uninformed about the particulars of evil. Wisdom of good gives enough to discern what's evil without dwelling in it. 

Paul finishes with praise of God, and in it he dispels the false doctrine of gnostic revelation regarding salvation. That while the OT saints believed in faith a mystery, we now know it is Jesus who is the Christ.

<!-- post 12846 · 2023-12-13 05:48 · study -->

**Mark 9:35**
*And he sat down, and called the twelve, and saith unto them, If any man desire to be first, the same shall be last of all, and servant of all*.

In discussing the gifts of the Spirit given to believers as part of the body of Christ, Paul mentions "helps;"

**1 Corinthians 12:28**
*And God hath set some in the church, first apostles, secondarily prophets, thirdly teachers, after that miracles, then gifts of healings, helps, governments, diversities of tongues.*

In **Romans 12:4-16**, it's ministry, but the same concept, those helping others to edify the whole body, as servants towards one another;

*For as we have many members in one body, and all members have not the same office: So we, being many, are one body in Christ, and every one members one of another. Having then gifts differing according to the grace that is given to us, whether prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of faith*; *Or ministry, let us wait on our ministering: or he that teacheth, on teaching; Or he that exhorteth, on exhortation: he that giveth, let him do it with simplicity; he that ruleth, with diligence; he that sheweth mercy, with cheerfulness.*

*Let love be without dissimulation. Abhor that which is evil; cleave to that which is good. Be kindly affectioned one to another with brotherly love; in honour preferring one another; Not slothful in business; fervent in spirit; serving the Lord; Rejoicing in hope; patient in tribulation; continuing instant in prayer; Distributing to the necessity of saints; given to hospitality. Bless them which persecute you: bless, and curse not. Rejoice with them that do rejoice, and weep with them that weep. Be of the same mind one toward another. Mind not high things, but condescend to men of low estate. Be not wise in your own conceits.*

And just as **Romans 16** ends with Paul praising those fellow laborers in the ministry of the gospel, immortalizing the saints who'd helped him, he thanks others throughout the epistles for their help. Their willingness to serve the larger body. To condesend to those of lower prominence, that they may benefit from the charitable love which is the fruit of the Spirit within us.

<!-- post 12847 · 2023-12-13 05:54 · study -->

**John 13:1-17**
*Now before the feast of the passover, when Jesus knew that his hour was come that he should depart out of this world unto the Father, having loved his own which were in the world, he loved them unto the end. And supper being ended, the devil having now put into the heart of Judas Iscariot, Simon's son, to betray him; Jesus knowing that the Father had given all things into his hands, and that he was come from God, and went to God; He riseth from supper, and laid aside his garments; and took a towel, and girded himself. After that he poureth water into a bason, and began to wash the disciples' feet, and to wipe them with the towel wherewith he was girded. Then cometh he to Simon Peter: and Peter saith unto him, Lord, dost thou wash my feet?Jesus answered and said unto him, What I do thou knowest not now; but thou shalt know hereafter. Peter saith unto him, Thou shalt never wash my feet. Jesus answered him, If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with me. Simon Peter saith unto him, Lord, not my feet only, but also my hands and my head. Jesus saith to him, He that is washed needeth not save to wash his feet, but is clean every whit: and ye are clean, but not all. For he knew who should betray him; therefore said he, Ye are not all clean.*

*So after he had washed their feet, and had taken his garments, and was set down again, he said unto them, Know ye what I have done to you? Ye call me Master and Lord: and ye say well; for so I am. If I then, your Lord and Master, have washed your feet; ye also ought to wash one another's feet. For I have given you an example, that ye should do as I have done to you. Verily, verily, I say unto you, The servant is not greater than his lord; neither he that is sent greater than he that sent him. If ye know these things, happy are ye if ye do them.*

Jesus emphasized the idea of service towards others, and that in doing so, we are serving Him. That what we do for the least of our brothers, we do also to Him. 

The ministry of helping others within a church, and in the world, is the point of the Christian life. It is the gift of the Spirit, but one that isn't as flashy and attention grabbing as "speaking in tongues" or being "slain in the spirit."

<!-- post 12848 · 2023-12-13 06:01 · study -->

**Luke 4:16-19**
*And he came to Nazareth, where he had been brought up: and, as his custom was, he went into the synagogue on the sabbath day, and stood up for to read. And there was delivered unto him the book of the prophet Esaias. And when he had opened the book, he found the place where it was written,*
 
*The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me to preach the gospel to the poor; he hath sent me to heal the brokenhearted, to preach deliverance to the captives, and recovering of sight to the blind, to set at liberty them that are bruised, To preach the acceptable year of the Lord***.**

As I have commented elsewhere, this reading of Isaiah by Christ Jesus, a prophecy of Him fulfilled before those in the synagogue that day, serves as a mission statement for Christians and our purpose as servants of God. 

As it is written in **James 1:27**;

*Pure religion and undefiled before God and the Father is this, To visit the fatherless and widows in their affliction, and to keep himself unspotted from the world*.

Religion is the outward practice of the faith we have inwardly. It is also the means of justifying us before others, as James exhortation to the brethren in chapter 2 says, faith without works is dead, not profiting others. 

We are meant to serve. To help. To preach the gospel to the poor and heal the broken hearted. To feed and clothe the needy, both physically and spiritually. Not just pray "be ye warmed and filled," but to genuinely care about others.

<!-- post 12849 · 2023-12-13 06:08 · study -->

I could do a week's worth of posts from scripture that teaches this simple doctrine, one that is so badly overlooked, being the less comely of the gifts. And though it is the easiest of the gifts to be manifest in a believer, it is often the most neglected, the least coveted, the one responsibility so many shirk. (Of which I am chief...)

If all parts of the body are equally important in serving the head, which is Christ, and not being a respecter of persons is a command, then we should diligently seek the ability to be a help to others. To show up to church, not to feed our own needs, being wise in our own conceits, but looking whereby we can be a blessing. Asking what needs be done, with cheer. Rejoicing in our liberty that we have the opportunity to be a help to the Kingdom of God itself.

Seek to emulate those mentioned in the epistles as great helpers of the gospel, who minister the saints.

## Romans 16  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2024-12-20

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/15438

<!-- post 15438 · 2024-12-20 05:32 · scripture -->

**Romans 16:1-20**
*I commend unto you Phebe our sister, which is a servant of the church which is at Cenchrea: That ye receive her in the Lord, as becometh saints, and that ye assist her in whatsoever business she hath need of you: for she hath been a succourer of many, and of myself also. Greet Priscilla and Aquila my helpers in Christ Jesus: Who have for my life laid down their own necks: unto whom not only I give thanks, but also all the churches of the Gentiles. Likewise greet the church that is in their house. Salute my well-beloved Epaenetus, who is the firstfruits of Achaia unto Christ. Greet Mary, who bestowed much labour on us. Salute Andronicus and Junia, my kinsmen, and my fellow-prisoners, who are of note among the apostles, who also were in Christ before me. Greet Amplias my beloved in the Lord. Salute Urbane, our helper in Christ, and Stachys my beloved. Salute Apelles approved in Christ. Salute them which are of Aristobulus' household. Salute Herodion my kinsman. Greet them that be of the household of Narcissus, which are in the Lord. Salute Tryphena and Tryphosa, who labour in the Lord. Salute the beloved Persis, which laboured much in the Lord. Salute Rufus chosen in the Lord, and his mother and mine. Salute Asyncritus, Phlegon, Hermas, Patrobas, Hermes, and the brethren which are with them. Salute Philologus, and Julia, Nereus, and his sister, and Olympas, and all the saints which are with them. Salute one another with an holy kiss. The churches of Christ salute you. Now I beseech you, brethren, mark them which cause divisions and offences contrary to the doctrine which ye have learned; and avoid them. For they that are such serve not our Lord Jesus Christ, but their own belly; and by good words and fair speeches deceive the hearts of the simple. For your obedience is come abroad unto all men. I am glad therefore on your behalf: but yet I would have you wise unto that which is good, and simple concerning evil. And the God of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly. The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you. Amen.*

Paul often closes his Epistles with these lists of shout-outs to the saints, who are at the churches he is writing to, delivering the inspired word of God to the assemblies at the various cities. Even as today, God's word is delivered to those who gather together in His name. 

These brothers and sisters in Christ are commended for their work, in service to the churches and the gospel. Fellow laborers. What an honor it is for them to be listed in scripture, for all time, memorialized by God. 

How can you read these commendations and neglect the very acts they are included to inspire? 

Who greets you with a holy kiss? Who has heard of your works and service for the Lord? Anyone? Some randos on a Discord server? 

Being least in the Kingdom of Heaven isn't a goal any Christian should have. But to run the race well. 

Go to church.

<!-- post 15439 · 2024-12-20 05:37 · study -->

BTW, just because people are mentioned by the household they belong to doesn't mean their church is some guys living room. Likely they are literal servants to a household of another. 

A new church plant may well meet in a living room, or tent, or another temporary place for services, but the pastor should have a goal of something more, by evangelizing and members doing the work of the Lord, to find a more fitting location. To trade tabernacles and tents for a temple. 

No seeker is going to walk past someone's house on a Sunday morning, and see a few cars parked outside and think of wandering in to see what's going on. 

Go to church.
